Let \(r=r_{k-1}\), let \(c\in K^\times\) be the unique cube root of
\(r\), and set
\[
 \alpha=\frac{z_k}{c^\ell}\in T(K).                  \tag{\(\mathsf{KT}_1\)}
\]
Then
\[
 z_k\in(L^\times)^\ell
 \iff \alpha\in\ell T(K).                           \tag{\(\mathsf{KT}_2\)}
\]
The fixed-norm coefficient fibre is naturally isomorphic to the finite
\(\acute{e}\)tale torsor
\[
 X=[\ell]^{-1}(\alpha)
\]
under \(T[\ell]\), of degree \(\ell^2\).  Its class
\[
 \kappa=\delta(\alpha)
 \in H^1(K,T[\ell])
 \simeq T(K)/\ell T(K)\simeq\F_\ell                 \tag{\(\mathsf{KT}_3\)}
\]
vanishes exactly on failure; the desired \(C\)-conclusion is
\(\kappa\ne0\).

There is an exact Euler representative containing the full selected
lower ancestry:
\[
 \boxed{\Omega=\alpha^{N/\ell}
 =\frac{z_k^{N/\ell}}{r}\in T[\ell](K),}
 \qquad
 \kappa=0\iff\Omega=1.                               \tag{\(\mathsf{KT}_4\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 \Omega^{q-1}=\eta_k^{-2N/\ell},                     \tag{\(\mathsf{KT}_5\)}
\]
so this is an invertible power of the original selected obstruction,
not a new lower invariant.

More precisely, identify
\[
 A=T[\ell](\overline K)
 \simeq\{(u_0,u_1,u_2)\in\F_\ell^3:
                u_0+u_1+u_2=0\}.
\]
Arithmetic \(q\)-Frobenius is \(F=qP\), with \(P\) a three-cycle; its
eigenvalues on \(A\) are \(1,q^2\).  Choosing a geometric origin makes
Frobenius on \(X\) the affine map \(x\mapsto Fx+v\), where the class of
\(v\) on the fixed line is \(\kappa\).  For every \(s\geq1\),
\[
 \#X(\F_{q^s})=
 \begin{cases}
 \ell^{1+\mathbf1_{3\mid s}},&\kappa=0,\\
 0,&\kappa\ne0\text{ and }\ell\nmid s,\\
 \ell^{1+\mathbf1_{3\mid s}},&\kappa\ne0\text{ and }\ell\mid s.
 \end{cases}                                          \tag{\(\mathsf{KT}_6\)}
\]
Equivalently, the Frobenius cycle types and zeta functions are
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c|c}
 &\text{cycles}&Z(X,t)\\ \hline
 \kappa=0&
 \ell[1]+\dfrac{\ell^2-\ell}{3}[3]&
 (1-t)^{-\ell}(1-t^3)^{-(\ell^2-\ell)/3}\\[2mm]
 \kappa\ne0&
 [\ell]+\dfrac{\ell-1}{3}[3\ell]&
 (1-t^\ell)^{-1}(1-t^{3\ell})^{-(\ell-1)/3}.
 \end{array}                                          \tag{\(\mathsf{KT}_7\)}
\]
Thus the entire fibre geometry differs only by translation along one
fixed line.  The torus \(T\) has no nontrivial \(K\)-rational
character.  More precisely, let \(B\) be a finite-dimensional
\(\F_\ell\)-representation of \(F\), let \(f:A\to B\) be an
\(F\)-equivariant linear homomorphism, and let \(v\in A^F\) represent
\(\kappa\).  The induced quotient map sends
\[
 \kappa\longmapsto[f(v)]\in B/(F-1)B.
\]
It kills \(\kappa\) exactly when \(f(v)\in(F-1)B\), in particular if
\(f\) kills \(A^F\).  When \(B=B^F\oplus(F-1)B\) is semisimple,
nonvanishing of \(f(v)\) is sufficient to retain only an image of the
same unresolved coordinate.  Thus scalar characters and the ordinary
norm/determinant routes are blind; a transfer to another anisotropic
torus must be checked through its particular module map.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since \(3\nmid q-1\), the cube root \(c\) is unique.  The norm of
\(z_k\) is \(z_{k-1}=r^\ell=c^{3\ell}\), proving
\(\alpha\in T(K)\).  If \(b^\ell=z_k\), norm coherence gives
\(\Norm(b)=r=c^3\), so \(b/c\in T(K)\); the converse is immediate.
This proves \((\mathsf{KT}_2)\).  To identify \(X\) with the coefficient
fibre, let \(\sigma_0,\sigma_1,\sigma_2\) be the three embeddings of
\(L/K\).  Over \(\overline K\), a point
\(t=(t_0,t_1,t_2)\in X\) satisfies
\[
 t_i^\ell=\sigma_i(\alpha),\qquad t_0t_1t_2=1.
\]
Send it to
\[
 (C,D)=\left(
   c\sum_i t_i,\,
   c^2\sum_{i<j}t_it_j
 \right).                                             \tag{\(\mathsf{KT}_8\)}
\]
The three source roots are \(ct_i\); their \(\ell\)-th powers are the
three distinct conjugates of \(z_k\), and their product is
\(c^3=r\).  Thus \((\mathsf{KT}_8)\) lands in the selected fixed-norm
coefficient fibre.  Conversely those distinct powered targets recover
the labeled \(t_i\), so the map is a bijection on geometric points.
It is \(K\)-defined, and both sides are finite \(\acute{e}\)tale;
hence it is an isomorphism.  The Kummer sequence, together with Lang's
theorem \(H^1(K,T)=0\), gives
\((\mathsf{KT}_3)\).

Modulo \(q-1\), one has \(N\equiv3\), hence \(c^N=c^3=r\).
The cyclic Euler criterion in the group \(T(K)\), of order \(N\),
now proves \((\mathsf{KT}_4)\).  Equation
\((\mathsf{KT}_5)\) follows from
\(z_k^{q-1}=\eta_k^{-2}\), since the base scalar \(c\) is killed by
raising to \(q-1\).

On the augmentation plane \(A\), the three-cycle has eigenvalues
\(q,q^2\), because \(q\) has order three modulo \(\ell\); therefore
\(qP\) has eigenvalues \(1,q^2\).  The cohomology quotient
\(A/(F-1)A\) is exactly its fixed line.  If \(v\in A^F\) represents
\(\kappa\), then over \(\F_{q^s}\) the exact restriction formula is
\[
 \operatorname{res}_{\F_{q^s}/\F_q}(\kappa)
 =[(1+F+\cdots+F^{s-1})v]=[sv]
 \in A/(F^s-1)A.                                     \tag{\(\mathsf{KT}_9\)}
\]
Moreover,
\[
 |A^{F^s}|=\ell^{1+\mathbf1_{3\mid s}}.
\]
This proves \((\mathsf{KT}_6)\), and orbit enumeration gives
\((\mathsf{KT}_7)\).  Finally the character lattice of the norm-one
cubic torus is the \(C_3\)-augmentation lattice, whose invariant
subgroup is zero.  Functoriality of the quotient
\(H^1=A/(F-1)A\) sends \([v]\) to \([f(v)]\); the final assertions
follow, with the stated semisimple direct-sum condition when it is
invoked.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[one normalized Singer element carries every current class]
\label{prop:cubic-normalized-singer}
For \(k\geq1\), put
\[
 q=2^{3^{k-1}},\qquad K=L_{k-1}=\F_q,qquad
 L=L_k=\F_{q^3},\qquad N=q^2+q+1.
\]
Let \(d_k\in K^\times\) be the unique cube root of \(z_{k-1}\), and
define
\[
 d_k^3=z_{k-1},\qquad s_k=d_k^2,\qquad
 \tau_k=\frac{z_k}{d_k}.                             \tag{\(\mathsf{NS}_1\)}
\]
Then \(\tau_k\in R^1_{L/K}\mathbb G_m(K)\), and its irreducible
cubic over \(K\) is
\[
 \boxed{X^3+s_kX^2+1.}                               \tag{\(\mathsf{NS}_2\)}
\]
In particular
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{L/K}(\tau_k)=s_k,\qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{L/K}(\tau_k^{-1})=0,\qquad
 \Norm_{L/K}(\tau_k)=1.                             \tag{\(\mathsf{NS}_3\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 \tau_k^{q-1}=\eta_k^{-2},\qquad
 \ord(\tau_k)=\ord(\eta_k).                         \tag{\(\mathsf{NS}_4\)}
\]

The coefficients retain the complete selected ancestry.  For \(k\geq2\),
\[
 \Norm(d_k)=d_{k-1},\qquad
 \Norm(s_k)=s_{k-1},\qquad
 \boxed{s_k^3=s_{k-1}\tau_{k-1}^2.}                 \tag{\(\mathsf{NS}_5\)}
\]
Also \(\F_2(s_k)=L_{k-1}\); conditional on \(C_{k-1}\), the
coefficient \(s_k\) is primitive in \(K^\times\).

Conversely, the normalized recursion is Frobenius-rigid.  Starting from
\(s_1=1\), every finite sequence in the prescribed fields satisfying
\((\mathsf{NS}_2)\) and the boxed recursion in
\((\mathsf{NS}_5)\) is a compatible absolute-Frobenius conjugate of the
selected finite sequence.  An infinite compatible sequence is similarly a
conjugate under the procyclic group
\(\operatorname{Gal}(\bigcup_jL_j/\F_2)\simeq\mathbb Z_3\).  Thus retaining
the complete recursion does not replace the primitivity problem: it
reconstructs the same selected elements up to an order-preserving Galois
action.

Now retain a current prime \(\ell\mid N\), the lower root
\(r=r_{k-1}\) with \(r^\ell=z_{k-1}\), and the cube root
\(c^3=r\) from Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-torus-torsor}.  Then
\[
 c^\ell=d_k,
 \qquad
 \boxed{\alpha_{k,\ell}=\tau_k,\qquad
 \Omega_{k,\ell}=\tau_k^{N/\ell}.}                  \tag{\(\mathsf{NS}_6\)}
\]
Thus the current torsors are the preimages
\([\ell]^{-1}(\tau_k)\) of one selected norm-one element, while
\(\Omega_{k,\ell}\in T[\ell](K)\) is their mod-\(\ell\) Euler
obstruction.  At this level,
\[
 C_k\iff C_{k-1}\ \hbox{and}\ \ord(\tau_k)=N.
\]
Consequently the universal cubic arm is the conjunction of these maximal-order
statements over all levels, not a separate prime-dependent family of torsors.

There is a corresponding selected critical-value criterion.  For odd
\(u\), use the generalized sums of \((\mathsf{IF}_2)\) and put
\[
 A_u(S)=T_u(S,0,1),\qquad
 B_u(S)=T_u(0,S,1),\qquad
 \Delta_u(S)=A_u(S)B_u(S)+1.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{NS}_7\)}
\]
If \(u=N/\ell\), then
\[
 \boxed{
 \Omega_{k,\ell}=1
 \iff \Delta_u(s_k)=0,}                             \tag{\(\mathsf{NS}_8\)}
\]
and more explicitly
\[
 \boxed{
 \Delta_u(s_k)
 =\Norm_{L/K}\!\left(1+\eta_k^{-2u}\right).}        \tag{\(\mathsf{NS}_9\)}
\]
If \(S_k\) is the minimal polynomial of \(s_k\) over \(\F_2\),
the desired current-\(\ell\) conclusion is therefore
\[
 \operatorname{Res}_X\!\left(S_k(X),\Delta_u(X)\right)=1.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{NS}_{10}\)}
\]
For \(u=1\), one has \(\Delta_1=1\), recovering the prime-step
closure.  For composite \(N\), the displayed resultant remains a
selected nonvanishing assertion.  Primitivity of \(s_k\) alone cannot
force it: primitive base coefficients with nonprimitive norm-one roots
of \(X^3+sX^2+1\) already occur in finite fields.  Any induction must
use the full recursion \((\mathsf{NS}_5)\), not only the sparse cubic.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Because \(3\nmid q-1\), cube roots are unique in \(K^\times\).
Substitute \(z_k=d_k\tau_k\) and \(z_{k-1}=d_k^3\) into
\((\mathsf{RS}_2)\), then divide by \(d_k^3\).  This gives
\((\mathsf{NS}_2)\); irreducibility follows because \(z_k\) and
\(\tau_k\) differ by a base scalar.  Its coefficients prove
\((\mathsf{NS}_3)\).  A base scalar is killed by \(q-1\), so
\((\mathsf{RS}_5)\) gives the first identity in
\((\mathsf{NS}_4)\).  On the norm-one group, both \(q-1\) and two
are coprime to \(N\); hence this power preserves order.

Taking norms in \(d_k^3=z_{k-1}\), using
\(\Norm(z_{k-1})=z_{k-2}\), and invoking uniqueness of cube roots
proves the first identity in \((\mathsf{NS}_5)\); squaring proves the
second.  Since \(z_{k-1}=d_{k-1}\tau_{k-1}\),
\[
 s_k^3=z_{k-1}^2=s_{k-1}\tau_{k-1}^2,
\]
which proves the recursion.  The equality \(s_k^3=z_{k-1}^2\) also
shows that \(s_k\) and \(z_{k-1}\) generate the same field: inverse
Frobenius recovers \(z_{k-1}\) from \(z_{k-1}^2\), while the unique
cube root in \(K^\times\) is a power and recovers \(s_k\) from
\(z_{k-1}^2\).  Under \(C_{k-1}\),
Corollary~\ref{cor:cubic-reciprocal-selector} makes \(z_{k-1}\)
primitive.  Squaring and cubing are automorphisms of the multiplicative
group \(K^\times\), so \(s_k\) is primitive as well.

For the rigidity assertion, the cubic at level one is irreducible, so its
three roots are one absolute-Frobenius orbit.  Inductively suppose the chosen
pair at level \(j-1\) is a compatible Frobenius conjugate of the selected
pair.  The recursion determines \(s_j\) uniquely, because cubing is an
automorphism of \(L_{j-1}^\times\), and gives the corresponding conjugate of
the selected coefficient.  The three roots of
\(X^3+s_jX^2+1\) are its relative-Frobenius orbit, hence exactly the three
extensions of that lower embedding to \(L_j\).  Choosing any of them extends
the Frobenius-compatible chain and proves the induction.

The two identities \(c^3=r\) and \(r^\ell=z_{k-1}=d_k^3\) give
\((c^\ell)^3=d_k^3\).  Cube-root uniqueness proves \(c^\ell=d_k\),
and substitution in \((\mathsf{KT}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{KT}_4)\)
proves \((\mathsf{NS}_6)\).
Finally \((\mathsf{RS}_5)\), together with
\(\ord(\tau_k)=\ord(\eta_k)\), proves the displayed equivalence
between \(C_k\) and the lower-level assertion plus \(\ord(\tau_k)=N\).

The conjugates of \(\tau_k^u\) have polynomial
\[
 X^3+A_u(s_k)X^2+B_u(s_k)X+1.
\]
Its characteristic-two discriminant is \(\Delta_u(s_k)^2\), and the
odd-index form of \((\mathsf{IF}_3)\) identifies \(\Delta_u(s_k)\) with the Jacobian
of \(\Psi_{u,1}\) at \((s_k,0)\).  It vanishes exactly when two
conjugates collide.  In that event \(\tau_k^u\in K^\times\), while
also \(\tau_k^u\) has norm one; since
\(K^\times\cap R^1_{L/K}\mathbb G_m=\{1\}\), this is precisely
\(\Omega_{k,\ell}=1\).  The converse is immediate, proving
\((\mathsf{NS}_8)\).

Put \(\delta=\tau_k^{q-1}=\eta_k^{-2}\).  The product of the three
pairwise differences of the conjugates of \(\tau_k^u\) is
\[
 \Norm_{L/K}(1+\delta^u).
\]
This is \((\mathsf{NS}_9)\).  Equivalently its denominator relative
to \(u=1\) is one, since
\[
 \Norm(1+\eta_k^{-2})
 =\Norm(1+\eta_k)^2=1;
\]
the last equality follows by evaluating the selected reciprocal cubic
of \(\eta_k\) at one.  Finally a nonzero element of the finite field
generated by \(s_k\) has absolute norm one, while zero has norm zero.
The product formula for the resultant proves
\((\mathsf{NS}_{10})\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[trace-recursive reparameterization of the Singer class]
\label{cor:cubic-trace-recursive-singer}
Set \(\gamma_0=1\), \(h_0=0\), and, for \(k\geq1\),
\[
 h_k=1+\gamma_k^{-1}.
\]
Then \(h_k\) has the irreducible relative cubic
\[
 \boxed{X^3+h_{k-1}X^2+X+1}                        \tag{\(\mathsf{HTR}_1\)}
\]
over \(L_{k-1}\).  Consequently
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}(h_k)=h_{k-1},\qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}(h_k^{-1})=1,\qquad
 \Norm(h_k)=1.                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{HTR}_2\)}
\]
In the notation of Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-normalized-singer},
\[
 \boxed{
 \tau_k^3=h_k^4,\qquad
 h_k=\frac{1+\zeta_k^3}{(1+\zeta_k)^3},\qquad
 h_k^2=\eta_k^{q+2},\qquad
 h_k=\eta_k^{q/2+1}.}                               \tag{\(\mathsf{HTR}_3\)}
\]
All exponents in the last line are invertible on the norm-one group:
\[
 \gcd(3,N)=\gcd(4,N)=\gcd(q/2+1,N)=1.
\]
Hence
\[
 \boxed{\ord(\tau_k)=\ord(h_k)=\ord(\eta_k).}        \tag{\(\mathsf{HTR}_4\)}
\]
Thus \(h_k\) gives a particularly sparse trace-recursive tower, but it is an
invertible-power reparameterization of the same selected order coordinate,
not an inductive order estimate.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Substitute \(\gamma_k=(h_k+1)^{-1}\) into
\(\gamma_k^3+\gamma_k+\gamma_{k-1}=0\), clear \((h_k+1)^3\), and use
\(\gamma_{k-1}=(h_{k-1}+1)^{-1}\).  This gives
\((\mathsf{HTR}_1)\).  The fractional-linear substitution preserves the
relative degree, so the cubic is irreducible; its coefficients give
\((\mathsf{HTR}_2)\).

The identities \(\gamma_{k-1}=\gamma_k(\gamma_k+1)^2\) and
\(z_j=\gamma_j^{-2}\) give
\[
 \tau_k^3=\frac{z_k^3}{z_{k-1}}
 =\left(1+\gamma_k^{-1}\right)^4=h_k^4.
\]
The displayed formula in \(\zeta_k\) is a direct simplification of
\(1+\gamma_k^{-1}\).  Using
\(\eta_k=\gamma_k^{q-1}\) and
\(\gamma_k^N=\gamma_{k-1}\) gives \(h_k^2=\eta_k^{q+2}\); injectivity of
squaring gives the last formula.  The gcds are immediate except possibly the
last: a common divisor of \(q/2+1\) and \(N=q^2+q+1\) divides three, while
\(q/2+1\not\equiv0\pmod3\).  The order equalities now follow from
\((\mathsf{HTR}_3)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[one-branch norm descent of the cubic critical value]
\label{prop:cubic-critical-norm-descent}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-normalized-singer}.  For every positive
odd \(u\), the critical polynomial in \((\mathsf{NS}_7)\) has a unique
compression
\[
 \boxed{\Delta_u(S)=\widehat\Delta_u(S^3)},\qquad
 \widehat\Delta_u(V)\in\F_2[V].                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CND}_1\)}
\]
For independent indeterminates \(V,Y\), define an operator
\(\mathcal C:\F_2[V]\to\F_2[V]\) by
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal C(F)(V)
 =\operatorname{Res}_Y\!\left(
   Y^3+VY^2+V,F(Y)
  \right).}                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{CND}_2\)}
\]
At every actual normalized edge \(j\geq2\),
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal C(F)(s_{j-1}^3)
 =\Norm_{L_{j-1}/L_{j-2}}F(s_j^3).}                \tag{\(\mathsf{CND}_3\)}
\]

Now let \(u=N/\ell\) be a current value at level \(k\).  Then
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal C^{\circ(k-1)}(\widehat\Delta_u)(1)
 =\Norm_{L_{k-1}/\F_2}\Delta_u(s_k).}              \tag{\(\mathsf{CND}_4\)}
\]
For \(k=1\), the left side means zero iterations and \(s_1=1\).  Consequently
\[
 \boxed{
 \Omega_{k,\ell}=1
 \iff
 \mathcal C^{\circ(k-1)}(\widehat\Delta_u)(1)=0,}  \tag{\(\mathsf{CND}_5\)}
\]
and desired nonfailure is exactly the terminal value one.

This is stricter than the unstructured resultant in
\((\mathsf{NS}_{10})\): one fixed cubic norm operator carries the selected
critical value through every actual normalized ancestry edge.  It is still a
lossless descent, not a universal nonvanishing theorem.  The terminal bit is
the same selected order coordinate, and its degree is not forced below the
minimal polynomial by the recursion.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Give \(C,D,E\) the weights \(1,2,3\).  The recurrence
\((\mathsf{IF}_2)\) makes \(T_n(C,D,E)\) homogeneous of weight \(n\).
Therefore every exponent of \(S\) in \(A_u(S)\) is congruent to \(u\)
modulo three, while every exponent in \(B_u(S)\) is congruent to \(2u\).
Every exponent in \(A_uB_u+1\) is consequently divisible by three, proving
the existence and uniqueness in \((\mathsf{CND}_1)\).

Put \(U_j=s_j^3=z_{j-1}^2\).  Squaring the selected irreducible cubic
\((\mathsf{RS}_2)\) for \(z_{j-1}\) shows that, over \(L_{j-2}\),
the element \(U_j\) has the irreducible polynomial
\[
 Y^3+U_{j-1}Y^2+U_{j-1}.
\]
The monic root-product formula for \((\mathsf{CND}_2)\) therefore gives
\((\mathsf{CND}_3)\) with no sign or leading factor.  Iteration and transitivity of
norms give \((\mathsf{CND}_4)\), using \(s_1=1\).  Finally
\((\mathsf{NS}_8)\) says that \(\Omega_{k,\ell}=1\) exactly when
\(\Delta_u(s_k)=0\); a finite-field norm vanishes exactly with its argument,
and every nonzero element of \(\F_2\) is one.  This proves
\((\mathsf{CND}_5)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[cyclotomic factorization of the cubic critical value]
\label{prop:cubic-critical-cyclotomic-factorization}
Let \(u\geq1\) be odd and \(d=(u-1)/2\).  There is a unique monic
\(H_u\in\F_2[X]\) such that
\[
 \mathcal D_u(X,1)=XH_u(X)^2,\qquad
 \deg H_u=d,\qquad H_u(0)=1.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{CCF}_1\)}
\]
Its roots are the distinct nonzero elements
\[
 x_\rho=\rho+\rho^{-1},
 \qquad
 \{\rho,\rho^{-1}\}\subset\mu_u\setminus\{1\},   \tag{\(\mathsf{CCF}_2\)}
\]
where all nonidentity \(u\)-th roots, not only primitive roots, occur.  The
critical compression in Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-critical-norm-descent}
has the exact factorization, with multiplicity,
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \widehat\Delta_u(V)
 &=\operatorname{Res}_X\!\left(
   H_u(X),VX+(X+1)^3
  \right)\\
 &=\prod_{\{\rho,\rho^{-1}\}}
   \left(x_\rho V+(x_\rho+1)^3\right)\\
 &=\prod_{\{\rho,\rho^{-1}\}}
   \left(V+\frac{(x_\rho+1)^3}{x_\rho}\right).
 \end{aligned}}                                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CCF}_3\)}
\]
The last equality uses \(\prod x_\rho=H_u(0)=1\).  The displayed critical
values need not be distinct; for example
\(\widehat\Delta_7(V)=(V+1)^3\).

The selected specialization remembers the actual Conway cubic exactly.  For
arbitrary \(z,Y\) in characteristic two, put
\[
 C_V(X)=X^3+X^2+(V+1)X+1.
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{
 C_{z^2}(1+zY^2)
 =z^3(Y^3+Y)^2+z^2.}                                \tag{\(\mathsf{CCF}_4\)}
\]
At level \(k\), take
\(z=z_{k-1}=\gamma_{k-1}^{-2}\) and
\(V=s_k^3=z_{k-1}^2\).  Because \(z\gamma_{k-1}^2=1\), this becomes
\[
 C_V(1+zY^2)=z^3(Y^3+Y+\gamma_{k-1})^2.             \tag{\(\mathsf{CCF}_5\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \boxed{
 \widehat\Delta_u(s_k^3)
 =\Norm_{L_k/L_{k-1}}
   H_u\!\left(1+z_{k-1}\gamma_k^2\right).}          \tag{\(\mathsf{CCF}_6\)}
\]
For \(u=N/\ell\), failure is equivalently that the selected cubic
\(C_{s_k^3}\) shares a root with \(H_u\).  This reconstructs the exact
cyclotomic collision behind the terminal bit; it does not force the resultant
nonzero when \(u\) is composite.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The standard Dickson factorization
\(\mathcal D_u(\rho+\rho^{-1},1)=\rho^u+\rho^{-u}\), with reciprocal
pairs grouped together, gives \((\mathsf{CCF}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{CCF}_2)\).
Let \(t_1,t_2,t_3\) be the roots of
\(T^3+ST^2+1\), so \(e_1=S,e_2=0,e_3=1\).  By the definitions in
\((\mathsf{NS}_7)\),
\[
 \Delta_u(S)
 =\prod_{i<j}(t_i^u+t_j^u).
\]
Factor each power difference over \(\mu_u\), remove the \(\rho=1\) layer,
and pair \(\rho\) with \(\rho^{-1}\).  For
\(x=\rho+\rho^{-1}\), the resulting symmetric product is
\[
 \prod_{i<j}(t_i^2+xt_it_j+t_j^2)=xS^3+(x+1)^3.
\]
Indeed \(e_2=0\) gives \(t_it_j=t_k(t_i+t_j)\); each factor is then
\((S+t_k)(S+(x+1)t_k)\), and multiplication over \(k\) gives the displayed
identity.  Taking the product over the roots of the monic \(H_u\) proves the
resultant and both products in \((\mathsf{CCF}_3)\), including multiplicity.

Expanding \(C_{z^2}(1+zY^2)\) proves \((\mathsf{CCF}_4)\).  The selected
relation \(z\gamma_{k-1}^2=1\) gives \((\mathsf{CCF}_5)\).  The roots of the
right-hand Conway cubic are the three relative conjugates of \(\gamma_k\),
so its transported roots are precisely the roots of the monic \(C_V\).
The root-product formula for the resultant now yields
\((\mathsf{CCF}_6)\).  Finally \((\mathsf{NS}_8)\) identifies its vanishing
with the original Euler obstruction.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the selected Singer trace is the whole remaining phase]
\label{prop:cubic-prescribed-trace}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-wendt-singer}.  For \(a\in\F_q\)
put
\[
 D_a=\{x\in D:\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{q^3}/\F_q}(x)=a\}.
\]
Each \(D_a\) is empty or one three-element relative-Frobenius orbit.
Let \(\chi:U\to\mu_\ell\) have exact order \(\ell\), where
\(\ord_\ell(q)=3\), and put \(H=\ker\chi\).  If \(D_a\ne\varnothing\),
define
\[
 \Theta_\chi(a)=\sum_{x\in D_a}\chi(x).
\]
Then
\[
 D_a\subset H\iff\Theta_\chi(a)=3.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{ST}_1\)}
\]
In particular, current failure is exactly
\[
 \Theta_\chi(\eta_{j-1})=3.                           \tag{\(\mathsf{ST}_2\)}
\]
For any nontrivial additive character \(\psi\) of \(\F_q\), Fourier
inversion gives
\[
 \Theta_\chi(a)
 =\frac1q\sum_{b\in\F_q}\psi(-ba)
   \sum_{x\in D}\chi(x)
      \psi\!\left(b\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{q^3}/\F_q}(x)\right).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{ST}_3\)}
\]
Whenever the moment criterion \((\mathsf{SW}_4)\) forces
\(D\cap H\ne\varnothing\), some trace fibre actually attains the
extremal value \(3\).  Hence a uniform character bound cannot exclude
\((\mathsf{ST}_2)\); one must distinguish the selected Conway fibre.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The trace-\(a\) elements of \(D\) are the roots of
\[
 T^3+aT^2+(a+1)T+1.
\]
Relative Frobenius has no fixed point on \(D\): an element of
\(D\cap\F_q\) would lie in \(\F_q^\times\cap U=\{1\}\), while
\(1\notin D\).  Thus every nonempty fibre is one orbit of length
three.

For \(x\in D_a\), put \(\theta=\chi(x)\).  Then
\[
 \Theta_\chi(a)=\theta+\theta^q+\theta^{q^2}.
\]
The fibre lies in \(H\) exactly when \(\theta=1\).  Conversely, equality
to \(3\) in the displayed sum forces all three unit complex numbers to
equal one, by equality in the triangle inequality.  This proves
\((\mathsf{ST}_1)\).  The distinguished fibre is the relative orbit of
\(\eta_j\), whose trace is \(\eta_{j-1}\), proving
\((\mathsf{ST}_2)\).  Character orthogonality on the additive group of
\(\F_q\) gives \((\mathsf{ST}_3)\).  Finally, if \(x\in D\cap H\), its
whole relative orbit lies in \(H\), so its trace fibre has
\(\Theta_\chi=3\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exact one-step lifting of the previous Singer set]
\label{prop:cubic-previous-singer-lift}
Let \(j\geq2\), put \(r=2^{3^{j-2}}\), \(q=r^3\), and fix
\(\omega\in\F_4\setminus\F_2\).  For \(s\in\{r,q\}\), write
\[
 U_s=\ker(\Norm_{\F_{s^3}/\F_s}),\qquad
 D_s=\{x\in U_s:x^{s+1}+x+1=0\}.
\]
In a common algebraic closure put
\[
 M(T)=\frac{\omega+\omega^2T}{1+T}.
\]
Then \(M\) gives a bijection
\[
 \{t:t^{s+1}=\omega^2\}\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}D_s,
\]
and, for \(t^{q+1}=\omega^2\),
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{q^3}/\F_q}(M(t))=M(t^3).   \tag{\(\mathsf{SL}_1\)}
\]
Consequently the restricted trace map
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}:\operatorname{Tr}^{-1}(D_r)\cap D_q\longrightarrow D_r
\]
is surjective with every fibre of cardinality exactly three, and
\[
 |\operatorname{Tr}^{-1}(D_r)\cap D_q|=3(r+1).      \tag{\(\mathsf{SL}_2\)}
\]
Thus precisely \(r+1\) of the \((q+1)/3\) nonempty current Singer
trace fibres lie over the previous Singer set.  In particular, the
selected coefficient's membership in \(D_r\) does not distinguish its
current fibre: every previous Singer point has a current three-point
fibre satisfying the same norm-one, Singer, and cubic-trace identities.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For \(x\in D_s\), put
\[
 t=\frac{x+\omega}{x+\omega^2}.
\]
Since \(s\) is a power of two with odd exponent,
\(\omega^s=\omega^2\).  Using
\(x^s=(x+1)/x\) gives \(t^s=\omega^2/t\), and hence
\(t^{s+1}=\omega^2\) and \(x=M(t)\).  Conversely,
\(t^{s+1}=\omega^2\) gives
\[
 M(t)^s=\frac{M(t)+1}{M(t)}.
\]
Iterating this order-three Möbius map shows that \(M(t)\) belongs to
\(\F_{s^3}\), has norm one, and lies in \(D_s\).  This proves the
bijection.

For \(x=M(t)\in D_q\), put
\(a=\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{q^3}/\F_q}(x)\).  The relative orbit has cubic
\[
 X^3+aX^2+(a+1)X+1.
\]
Substitution and cross-multiplication give
\[
 \left(\frac{x+\omega}{x+\omega^2}\right)^3
 =\frac{a+\omega}{a+\omega^2}.
\]
Thus \(t^3=M^{-1}(a)\), proving \((\mathsf{SL}_1)\).

Now let \(a=M(u)\in D_r\), so \(u^{r+1}=\omega^2\), and choose any
of the three roots \(t^3=u\).  Write
\[
 C=\frac{r^2-r+1}{3},\qquad q+1=3(r+1)C.
\]
For \(j=2\), one has \(r=2\) and \(C=1\).  For \(j\geq3\),
\(r\equiv-1\pmod9\), so
\(r^2-r+1\equiv3\pmod9\).  In either case \(C\equiv1\pmod3\), and
\[
 t^{q+1}
 =(t^3)^{(r+1)C}
 =(\omega^2)^C
 =\omega^2.
\]
All three roots therefore give distinct points of \(D_q\) with trace
\(a\).  Conversely, \((\mathsf{SL}_1)\) shows that every point above
\(D_r\) arises this way, proving \((\mathsf{SL}_2)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[the complete cubic recursion is Frobenius-rigid]
Suppose \(\xi_0=0\) and, for every \(i\geq1\), the element \(\xi_i\)
is a root of
\[
 X^3+\xi_{i-1}X^2+(\xi_{i-1}+1)X+1.
\]
Then \(\xi_i=\eta_i^{2^{e_i}}\) for some \(e_i\), with
\[
 e_i\equiv e_{i-1}\pmod{3^{i-1}}.
\]
Indeed, after
\(\xi_{i-1}=\eta_{i-1}^{2^e}\), the stage-\(i\) cubic is the
\(2^e\)-Frobenius twist of the selected cubic.  Its three roots are
\[
 \eta_i^{\,2^{e+t3^{i-1}}},\qquad t=0,1,2.
\]
Induction proves the assertion.  Thus imposing the complete recursive
chain determines one absolute Frobenius orbit, whose members all have
the same multiplicative order.  Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-previous-singer-lift}
therefore rules out a one-step membership argument; it does not create
a false recursive tower.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[the natural secant Kummer cover is rational]
Over \(\F_4\), the Möbius function

\[
 M(T)=\frac{\omega+\omega^2T}{1+T}
\]

which expresses the cyclotomic secant has Kummer cover

\[
 Z^\ell=M(T),\qquad
 T=\frac{Z^\ell+\omega}{Z^\ell+\omega^2}.
\]

Its function field is therefore \(\F_4(Z)\), of genus zero.  A
positive-genus Weil estimate on this cover cannot rule out the
distinguished torsion fibre; the remaining information is its actual
Artin value.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Capelli form of the surviving lemma]
\label{prop:capelli}
Put
\[
 A(X)=X^3+X+1,\qquad B(X)=X^2+X,\qquad
 R(X)=\frac{A(X)}{B(X)}.
\]
Let $Q_0(X)=X$ and, for $k\geq1$, define the monic binary polynomial
\[
 Q_k(X)=B(X)^{3^{k-1}}
        Q_{k-1}\!\left(R(X)\right).                         \tag{\(\star\)}
\]
Then $Q_k$ is the minimal polynomial of $\eta_k$ over $\F_2$ and is
irreducible of degree $3^k$.  Its first four values are
\begin{align*}
 Q_1(X)&=X^3+X+1,\\
 Q_2(X)&=X^9+X+1,\\
 Q_3(X)&=X^{27}+X^{25}+X^{24}+X^{19}+X^{18}+X^{16}
          +X^{10}+X^9+X^3+X+1,\\
 Q_4(X)&=X^{81}+X^{64}+X^{16}+X+1.
\end{align*}
For every prime $\ell\mid\Phi_{3^k}(2)$, the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item $\chi_{k,\ell}(\eta_k)\neq1$;
\item $\eta_k$ is not an $\ell$-th power in
      $\F_{2^{3^k}}^{\times}$;
\item $X^{\ell}-\eta_k$ is irreducible over $\F_{2^{3^k}}$;
\item $Q_k(X^{\ell})$ is irreducible over $\F_2$.
\end{enumerate}
Thus the surviving character lemma is equivalently the assertion that
all the explicitly recursive compositions $Q_k(X^{\ell})$, for
$\ell\mid\Phi_{3^k}(2)$, are irreducible.  At the first composite
level $k=4$, the three targets are the monomial compositions of the
displayed pentanomial $Q_4$ by
$\ell\in\{2593,71119,97685839\}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower} gives $R(\eta_k)=\eta_{k-1}$ and an
irreducible cubic for $\eta_k$ over $\F_{2^{3^{k-1}}}$.  Since
$\eta_{k-1}\in\F_2(\eta_k)$, induction gives
$[\F_2(\eta_k):\F_2]=3^k$.  Clearing the denominator in the minimal
polynomial of $\eta_{k-1}$ gives \((\star)\), a monic polynomial of
degree $3^k$ having $\eta_k$ as a root: its leading term is
$A(X)^{3^{k-1}}=X^{3^k}+\cdots$.  It is therefore the minimal
polynomial.  Direct substitution in \((\star)\) gives the four displayed
polynomials.

Here $\Phi_{3^k}(2)=q_k^2+q_k+1\equiv1\pmod3$, so a prime
$\ell\mid\Phi_{3^k}(2)$ is not $3$.  The cyclotomic-divisor criterion
therefore gives $\ord_{\ell}(2)=3^k$, and hence
$\mu_{\ell}\subset\F_{2^{3^k}}$.  Since $\ell$ is prime, Kummer theory
says that $X^{\ell}-\eta_k$ either splits off a linear factor or is
irreducible, according as $\eta_k$ is or is not an $\ell$-th power.
This proves the equivalence of (ii) and (iii), while
Capelli's lemma gives the equivalence of (iii) and (iv).  The direct
product
$\F_{2^{3^k}}^{\times}\simeq
 \F_{q_k}^{\times}\times U_k$ and
$\ell\nmid(q_k-1)$ show that being an $\ell$-th power in the full
field is equivalent, for $\eta_k\in U_k$, to lying in $U_k^{\ell}$.
This is the equivalence with (i).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Singer membership is not primitivity]
\label{rem:singer-not-primitive}
The difference-set identification cannot replace the character test.
Indeed, exponentiation
\[
 s_k:U_k\longrightarrow U_k,\qquad s_k(y)=y^{q_k+1},
\]
is an automorphism of order $6$: one has
$\gcd(q_k+1,|U_k|)=1$ and
$(q_k+1)^3\equiv-1\pmod{|U_k|}$, while
$(q_k+1)^2\equiv q_k\not\equiv1$.  The Singer equation is simply
$s_k(y)=y+1$.  Thus its consequence
$\ord(y+1)=\ord(y)$ is automatic order preservation by an
automorphism and supplies no order divisor by itself.
For example, take $q=32$ and let $y$ be a root of
$X^3+X^2+1$ over $\F_2$.  Then $y$ has order $7$ and
$q+1\equiv5\pmod7$; the defining cubic gives
$y^5+y+1=0$.  Hence $y\in\{x:x^{q+1}+x+1=0\}$, but $y$ does not
generate the norm-one group of order
$q^2+q+1=7\cdot151$.  What must be used in the Conway tower is the
recursive coefficient $\eta_{k-1}$ in \((*)\), not Singer membership
alone.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[prime-step closure]\label{prop:prime-step}
If $\Phi_{3^k}(2)$ is prime, then
\[
 C_k\iff C_{k-1}.
\]
Consequently $C_1,C_2,C_3$ hold analytically: the three new kernel
orders are respectively
\[
 7,\qquad73,\qquad262657,
\]
and all are prime.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower} proves that $\eta_k$ has degree $3$
over $\F_{q_k}$, so in particular $\eta_k\neq1$.  A nonidentity element
of a group of prime order generates it, and \((**)\) gives the first
claim.  The primality of $7$ and $73$ is immediate.  For completeness,
$262657-1=2^9\cdot3^3\cdot19$, and base $5$ gives the Pocklington
certificate
\[
 5^{262656}\equiv1\pmod{262657},\qquad
 \gcd\!\left(5^{262656/r}-1,262657\right)=1
 \quad(r=2,3,19),
\]
which proves the third primality assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[cubic-arm equivalence]\label{prop:gauss-arm}
For primes $r$ with $f(r)\mid 3^k$ a $3$-power:
\[
  C_k
  \iff
  m_r=1\ \text{for every prime $r$ with } f(r)\in\{3,9,\dots,3^k\}.
\]
The family formula is the $0/1/4$ candidate of
Section~\ref{sec:introduction}
restricted to the $3$-power column.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[cyclotomic-unit residue reduction]
\label{prop:unit-residue}
Let $h=3^k$ and let $\ell$ be any prime divisor of
$2^h-1$.  Then
\[
 \gamma_k=\zeta^{-1}(1+\zeta)^2
\]
is an $\ell$-th power in $\F_{2^h}$ if and only if $1+\zeta$ is an
$\ell$-th power in $\F_{2^{2h}}$.  The same equivalence holds at every
power $\ell^a\mid2^h-1$; non-$\ell$-power already implies that
$\gamma_k$ has the full $\ell$-primary part of $2^h-1$ in its order.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Because $\ell\mid2^h-1$, one has $\ell\nmid2^h+1$, so extension from
$\F_{2^h}^{\times}$ to $\F_{2^{2h}}^{\times}$ preserves the
$\ell$-primary quotient.  Also $\ell\neq3$, hence $\zeta^{-1}$ is an
$\ell$-th power in the $3^{k+1}$-group generated by $\zeta$; squaring
is an automorphism of every odd $\ell$-primary quotient.  The displayed
factorization proves both assertions.
\end{proof}

General multiplicative-order bounds for Gaussian periods do not imply
maximal order~\cite{ASV10}.  Here the norm recursion
$\gamma_k^3+\gamma_k=\gamma_{k-1}$ propagates the old factors, but a
proof must still show that each new prime-power factor of
$\Phi_{3^k}(2)$ divides $\ord(\gamma_k)$.  Irreducibility of the
corresponding Dickson polynomial is likewise weaker than primitivity
\cite{MM05}.  Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower} supplies the appropriate
cubic replacement and places the root on a Singer difference set, but
Remark~\ref{rem:singer-not-primitive} shows that Singer membership
alone cannot prove generation.

\subsection{The cubic-spine conjecture \texorpdfstring{\((C)\)}{(C)}}

The universal cubic arm is Conjecture~\ref{conj:ck}.  The prime-step
argument proves \(C_k\) analytically for \(k\leq3\), and
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-partitions} proves the required primary
order above an explicit partition threshold at every level.  The
remaining task is the selected Kummer-class nonvanishing at the smaller
new primary factors.  Propositions~\ref{prop:cubic-ray-exceptional-character}
and~\ref{prop:cubic-real-resultant} show respectively that the current
ray Euler factor has a forced simple zero whose circular-unit regulator is
exactly \(L(1)\), and that the real norm criterion has no viable size
bound.  Proposition~\ref{prop:parity-code-no-go} rules out a generic
cyclic-code or difference-set contradiction.  Finite order checks, their factorization
dependencies, and the distinction between local certificates and
external primality records are isolated in
Appendix~\ref{sec:cubic-evidence}.  Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-coleman-exceptional-zero}
also closes the natural three-adic Coleman route: the circular unit has
the explicit series \((2+T)^2/(1+T)\), but its algebraic
\(\widetilde\lambda x^{3^k-1}\)-specialization is forced to vanish
modulo every current \(\ell\), even at the proved level
\((k,\ell)=(2,73)\).  After division by that exceptional factor one
is left with a generalized Bernoulli value for which Coleman theory
supplies no \(\ell\)-adic unit
statement or cross-prime identification with the selected Kummer index.
\section{The singleton-even Conway--Fermat arm}\label{sec:fermat}

The candidate contains strong explicit maximal-order assertions even
before the non-cyclotomic singleton chains are reached.  This prevents a
proof from following from field degree or irreducibility alone.

\subsection{Maximal projective order}

\begin{proposition}[the singleton-even arm is Conway--Fermat maximality]
\label{prop:fermat-arm}
For $n\geq0$, put
\[
 h_n=2^{n+1},\qquad c_n=\kappa_{h_n}=2^{2^n},\qquad
 E_n=\F_{2^{h_n}},\qquad F_n=2^{2^n}+1.
\]
With $E_{-1}=\F_2$, let
\[
 \delta_n=\ord\bigl(c_nE_{n-1}^{\times}\bigr)
 \quad\text{in}\quad E_n^{\times}/E_{n-1}^{\times}.
\]
Then $1<\delta_n\mid F_n$, and the $m=0$ arm of
Conjecture~\ref{conj:rule} for every prime $p\mid F_n$ is equivalent to
\[
  \delta_n=F_n.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Conway finite-nimber tower satisfies
\[
 c_n^2+c_n=c_{n-1}c_{n-2}\cdots c_0
\]
(with the empty product $1$).  Thus $E_n/E_{n-1}$ is quadratic and
$c_n\notin E_{n-1}$, so the coset order is nontrivial.  Its ambient
quotient has order
\[
 \frac{2^{2^{n+1}}-1}{2^{2^n}-1}=F_n,
\]
which proves $1<\delta_n\mid F_n$; equivalently the relative norm is
$c_n^{F_n}=c_{n-1}\cdots c_0$.

If $p\mid F_n$, then $2^{2^n}\equiv-1\pmod p$, hence
$f(p)=2^{n+1}=h_n$.  Fermat numbers are pairwise coprime, so the entire
$p$-primary part of $|E_n^{\times}|$ lies in the quotient by
$E_{n-1}^{\times}$.  The full power criterion therefore gives
$m_p=0$ iff
$v_p(\delta_n)=v_p(F_n)$.  Requiring this for every prime divisor of
$F_n$ is exactly $\delta_n=F_n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[exact primitive-product equivalence]
\label{cor:fermat-popovych-equivalence}
Put \(a_n=\prod_{j=0}^n c_j\).  For every \(n\geq2\),
\[
 \ord(c_n)=\ord(a_n)=\prod_{j=1}^n\delta_j.             \tag{\(\mathsf{PP}_1\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 c_0c_n\text{ is primitive in }E_n
 \iff
 \delta_j=F_j\quad(1\leq j\leq n).                    \tag{\(\mathsf{PP}_2\)}
\]
Thus the all-level Conway--Fermat arm is exactly the all-level
primitive-product problem studied by Popovych~\cite{Popovych18}, in both
directions, rather than merely an analogous maximal-order question.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Write \(q=2^{2^n}\).  Since
\(\gcd(q-1,q+1)=1\), the cyclic group \(E_n^\times\) is the direct
product of \(E_{n-1}^\times\), of order \(q-1\), and its subgroup of
order \(F_n=q+1\).  Write accordingly
\(c_n=g_nu_n\), where \(u_n\in E_{n-1}^\times\) and
\(\ord(g_n)=\delta_n\).  The tower norm gives
\[
 a_{n-1}=c_n^{q+1}=u_n^2,
\]
so \(\ord(u_n)=\ord(a_{n-1})\).  The two factors have coprime orders,
and therefore
\[
 \ord(c_n)=\delta_n\ord(a_{n-1}),
 \qquad
 a_n=a_{n-1}c_n=g_nu_n^3.
\]
At \(n=1\), the quotient has prime order \(F_1=5\), so
\(\delta_1=5\); the same decomposition gives \(u_1^3=1\) and
therefore \(a_1=g_1\), of order \(5\).  Inductively
\(\ord(u_n)=\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}\delta_j\), which is prime to three
because every \(\delta_j\mid F_j\) for \(j\geq1\).  Cubing therefore
preserves the order of \(u_n\), proving \((\mathsf{PP}_1)\).

The element \(c_0\) has order three, coprime to \(\ord(c_n)\), while
\[
 |E_n^\times|=3\prod_{j=1}^nF_j.
\]
Hence \(c_0c_n\) is primitive exactly when
\(\prod_{j=1}^n\delta_j=\prod_{j=1}^nF_j\).  Since the Fermat numbers
are pairwise coprime and \(\delta_j\mid F_j\), this is equivalent to
all the displayed equalities in \((\mathsf{PP}_2)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the Conway and Wiedemann norm-one drivers are not Frobenius-identical]
\label{prop:fermat-wiedemann-separation}
Put \(q=|E_{n-1}|=2^{2^n}\) and
\[
 w_n=c_n^{q-1}=\frac{c_n+1}{c_n}.
\]
The canonical isomorphism
\[
 E_n^\times/E_{n-1}^\times
   \longrightarrow
 \ker\!\left(\Norm_{E_n/E_{n-1}}\right),
 \qquad zE_{n-1}^\times\longmapsto z^{q-1},
\]
preserves order, and therefore
\[
 \ord(w_n)=\delta_n,\qquad
 w_n+w_n^{-1}=a_{n-1}^{-1}.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{WW}_1\)}
\]
If \(d_n=a_{n-1}^{-1}\), then the Conway drivers themselves obey
\[
 d_{n+1}=d_n(w_n+1)=\frac{(w_n+1)^3}{w_n}.           \tag{\(\mathsf{WW}_2\)}
\]
This has the same norm-one shape as Wiedemann's iterated quadratic
tower~\cite{Wiedemann88}, whose selected elements satisfy
\[
 x_n+x_n^{-1}=x_{n-1},
 \qquad \ord(x_n)\mid F_n,
\]
but it is not the same recursion.  For every \(n\geq3\), no
\(\F_2\)-automorphism of an algebraic closure can send \(x_n\) to
\(w_n\).  Thus Wiedemann maximality cannot be transferred to the
Conway--Fermat arm by a Frobenius identification of the selected
elements.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The displayed quotient map is injective because its kernel is
\(E_{n-1}^\times\), and both groups have order \(q+1=F_n\), proving the
order assertion.  The Conway relation gives
\(c_n(c_n+1)=a_{n-1}\), whence
\[
 w_n+w_n^{-1}
 =\frac{(c_n+1)^2+c_n^2}{c_n(c_n+1)}
 =a_{n-1}^{-1}
\]
in characteristic two.
Also \(a_n=a_{n-1}c_n\) and
\(c_n=(w_n+1)^{-1}\), so
\[
 d_{n+1}=d_n(w_n+1)
 =(w_n+w_n^{-1})(w_n+1)
 =\frac{(w_n+1)^3}{w_n},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{WW}_2)\).

If an \(\F_2\)-automorphism sent \(x_n\) to \(w_n\), it would send
\(x_{n-1}=x_n+x_n^{-1}\) to \(a_{n-1}^{-1}\), and hence preserve their
multiplicative orders.  But
\(\ord(x_{n-1})\mid F_{n-1}\), whereas
\[
 \ord(a_{n-1}^{-1})
 =\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}\delta_j
\]
by \((\mathsf{PP}_1)\), and this order is divisible by
\(\delta_1=F_1=5\).  Fermat numbers are pairwise coprime, so
\(5\nmid F_{n-1}\) for \(n\geq3\), a contradiction.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[two-step quartic and exact Kummer dichotomy]
\label{prop:fermat-two-step-quartic}
Let \(n\geq2\), put \(B=a_{n-2}\), \(K_0=E_{n-2}\), and
\(c=c_n\).  Then the minimal polynomial of \(c\) over \(K_0\) is
\[
 f_n(X)=X^4+(B+1)X^2+BX+B^3.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{TQ}_1\)}
\]
For every prime \(\ell\mid F_n\), the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item the order \(\delta_n\) contains the full \(\ell\)-primary part
      of \(F_n\);
\item \(c\) is not an \(\ell\)-th power in \(E_n^\times\);
\item \(f_n(X^\ell)\) is irreducible over \(K_0\).
\end{enumerate}
If these conditions fail, then \(f_n(X^\ell)\) is a product of exactly
\(\ell\) distinct irreducible quartics over \(K_0\).  Thus a
minimal-counterexample argument may be descended across two quadratic
levels without discarding the selected coefficient \(B\), but failure
remains a complete quartic splitting pattern rather than a degree
contradiction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write \(a=a_{n-1}=Bc_{n-1}\).  The two consecutive Conway relations
give
\[
 c_{n-1}^2+c_{n-1}=B,\qquad c^2+c=a.
\]
Multiplying the first by \(B^2\) and using \(a=Bc_{n-1}\) gives
\[
 a^2+Ba+B^3=0.
\]
Eliminating \(a=c^2+c\) now gives \(f_n(c)=0\).
Moreover \(B\ne0\), so \(c_{n-1}=a/B\) belongs to \(K_0(c)\).
Consequently
\[
 K_0(c)\supset K_0(c_{n-1})=E_{n-1},
\]
and adjoining \(c\) gives \(E_n\).  Since
\([E_n:K_0]=4\), the displayed quartic is the minimal polynomial.

Fix \(\ell\mid F_n\).  The entire \(\ell\)-primary part of
\(E_n^\times\) lies in
\(E_n^\times/E_{n-1}^\times\), so (i) and (ii) are the exact cyclic
power criterion.  Also \(\mu_\ell\subset E_n\).  Capelli's lemma gives
\[
 f_n(X^\ell)\text{ irreducible over }K_0
 \iff X^\ell-c\text{ irreducible over }E_n,
\]
and Kummer theory over \(E_n\) identifies the latter condition with
\(c\notin E_n^{\times\ell}\).  This proves the equivalence.

Under failure write \(c=t^\ell\) with \(t\in E_n\).  Every root of
\(f_n(X^\ell)\) lies in \(E_n\), since \(\mu_\ell\subset E_n\).
No such root lies in the unique intermediate field \(E_{n-1}\):
otherwise its \(\ell\)-th power, one of the four \(K_0\)-conjugates of
\(c\), would lie there, forcing \(c\) itself into \(E_{n-1}\).
Hence every root has degree four over \(K_0\).  The polynomial is
separable and has \(4\ell\) roots, so it factors into exactly
\(\ell\) distinct irreducible quartics.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[coefficient conic of the bad quartics]
\label{prop:fermat-quartic-conic}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-two-step-quartic}, put
\(Q=|K_0|\), \(L=E_{n-1}\), and suppose that \(c=t^\ell\) in
\(E_n\).  Set
\[
 r=\Norm_{E_n/L}(t),\qquad e=r+r^Q,\qquad d=rr^Q.
\]
Then
\[
 r^\ell=a_{n-1},\qquad e\ne0,\qquad d^\ell=B^3.       \tag{\(\mathsf{TQ}_2\)}
\]
For \(\zeta\in\mu_\ell\), put
\[
 y_\zeta=t\zeta,\qquad s_\zeta=y_\zeta+y_\zeta^{Q^2}.
\]
Writing \(\mathcal D_\ell(S,P)\) for the Dickson power sum of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-root-ancestry}, one has
\[
 \mathcal D_\ell(s_\zeta,r)=1.                       \tag{\(\mathsf{TQ}_3\)}
\]
The \(\ell\) irreducible quartic factors of \(f_n(X^\ell)\) are
exactly
\[
 \begin{split}
 g_{s_\zeta}(X)
 &=\Norm_{L/K_0}(X^2+s_\zeta X+r)\\
 &=X^4+\alpha_\zeta X^3+\beta_\zeta X^2
       +\gamma_\zeta X+d,
 \end{split}
\]
where
\[
 \alpha_\zeta=s_\zeta+s_\zeta^Q,\qquad
 \beta_\zeta=e+s_\zeta s_\zeta^Q,\qquad
 \gamma_\zeta=s_\zeta r^Q+s_\zeta^Q r.
\]
Every coefficient triple lies on the conic
\[
 \gamma_\zeta^2+\alpha_\zeta e\gamma_\zeta
 +\alpha_\zeta^2d=e^2(\beta_\zeta+e).                \tag{\(\mathsf{TQ}_4\)}
\]
Conversely, \(\alpha_\zeta\) and \(\gamma_\zeta\) recover
\[
 s_\zeta=\frac{\gamma_\zeta+\alpha_\zeta r}{e},
\]
and \(\beta_\zeta\) is then forced.  Thus the coefficient conic is
a lossless change of coordinates on the same Dickson fibre
\(\mathcal D_\ell(S,r)=1\); it supplies no independent obstruction
to the bad factorization.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Taking the norm of \(t^\ell=c\) from \(E_n\) to \(L\) gives
\(r^\ell=a_{n-1}\).  If \(r\) belonged to \(K_0\), then so would
\(a_{n-1}=Bc_{n-1}\); since \(B\ne0\) and
\(c_{n-1}\notin K_0\), this is impossible.  Hence \(e\ne0\).  A second
norm and the quadratic equation for \(a_{n-1}\) give
\[
 d^\ell=\Norm_{L/K_0}(a_{n-1})=B^3.
\]

Because \(\ell\mid Q^2+1\), every \(\zeta\in\mu_\ell\) has norm one
from \(E_n\) to \(L\).  Thus \(y_\zeta\) has relative trace
\(s_\zeta\) and norm \(r\).  The Dickson identity gives
\[
 \mathcal D_\ell(s_\zeta,r)
 =y_\zeta^\ell+(y_\zeta^{Q^2})^\ell
 =c+c^{Q^2}=1.
\]
The traces \(s_\zeta\) are distinct: equality would identify the two
unordered root pairs of the corresponding quadratic; the crossed
identification has \(\ell\)-th powers \(c\) and \(c+1\), which is
impossible.  Taking the \(Q\)-conjugate norm of these quadratics gives
all \(\ell\) quartics and the displayed coefficient formulas; their
irreducibility was proved in Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-two-step-quartic}.

Finally
\[
 \gamma_\zeta+\alpha_\zeta r=e s_\zeta.
\]
Taking the norm from \(L\) to \(K_0\) and expanding both sides gives
\((\mathsf{TQ}_4)\).  Since \(e\ne0\), the same identity recovers
\(s_\zeta\), after which
\(\beta_\zeta=e+s_\zeta s_\zeta^Q\) is forced.  This proves the
lossless assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Fibonacci-polynomial criterion]
\label{prop:fermat-fibotomic}
Fix $n\geq0$, put $m=2^n$, $q=2^m$, and write
$K=E_{n-1}=\F_q$, $L=E_n=\F_{q^2}$ and
$a=a_{n-1}=\prod_{j<n}c_j$.  If
\[
 S_0(X)=0,\qquad S_1(X)=1,\qquad
 S_{r+2}(X)=S_{r+1}(X)+XS_r(X),
\]
then
\[
 \delta_n=\min\{r>0:S_r(a)=0\},
\]
and hence
\[
 \delta_n=F_n
 \iff
 S_{F_n/\ell}(a)\ne0
 \quad\hbox{for every prime }\ell\mid F_n.       \tag{\(\mathsf F_n\)}
\]

More precisely, for odd $d>1$ define the fibotomic polynomial
\[
 \mathfrak F_d(X)=
 \prod_{\substack{\{v,v^{-1}\}\\ \ord(v)=d}}
 \left(X-\frac{v}{(v+1)^2}\right),
\]
where the product takes one representative of each inverse-pair of
primitive $d$-th roots.  Then $\mathfrak F_d\in\F_2[X]$,
\[
 S_r(X)=\prod_{\substack{d\mid r\\d>1}}\mathfrak F_d(X)
 \quad(r\text{ odd}),                                      \tag{\(\mathsf B_r\)}
\]
and, if $A_{n-1}$ is the minimal polynomial of $a_{n-1}$ over $\F_2$,
\[
 \delta_n=d\iff A_{n-1}\mid\mathfrak F_d.
\]
Every irreducible factor of every $\mathfrak F_d$ with
$1<d\mid F_n$ has the same degree $m$.  Thus the Conway--Fermat
conjecture is exactly the assertion
$A_{n-1}\mid\mathfrak F_{F_n}$; field degree cannot distinguish that
stratum from any proper-divisor stratum.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Put $c=c_n$ and $u=(c+1)/c$.  Since $c^2+c=a$ and
$c^q=c+1$, the element $u$ lies in the norm-one group of order
$q+1=F_n$, and $\delta_n=\ord(u)$.  The two roots $c,c+1$ of
$T^2+T+a$ give the Binet identity
\[
 S_r(a)=c^r+(c+1)^r=\frac{1+u^r}{(1+u)^r}.                 \tag{\(\mathsf B\)}
\]
This proves the first criterion.  In a cyclic group of order $F_n$ an
element has full order precisely when its $F_n/\ell$-th power is
nontrivial for every prime $\ell\mid F_n$, proving
\((\mathsf F_n)\).

For odd $r$, the nontrivial $r$-th roots $v$ occur in inverse pairs,
and the common value $v/(v+1)^2$ of a pair is, by
\((\mathsf B)\), a root of $S_r$.  Frobenius permutes the pairs, so
the displayed factors have coefficients in $\F_2$.  Both sides of
\((\mathsf B_r)\) are monic of degree $(r-1)/2$, which proves the
factorization and the divisibility criterion.

Finally $2^m\equiv-1\pmod d$ for every nontrivial $d\mid F_n$.
Because $m$ is a power of two, $\ord_d(2)=2m$, and the least $j>0$
with $2^j\equiv\pm1\pmod d$ is $m$.  This is exactly the Frobenius
orbit length of $v/(v+1)^2$: indeed
\[
 \frac{v}{(v+1)^2}=\frac{w}{(w+1)^2}
 \iff (v+w)(vw+1)=0,
\]
so only the inverse pair $\{v,v^{-1}\}$ has the same image.  This
proves the equal-degree assertion.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The selected factor and the limits of one-step induction}

\begin{proposition}[coarse top-step data cannot force maximality]
\label{prop:fermat-countermodels}
For every divisor $d>1$ of $F_n$ there are elements
$a_d\in\F_q$ and $c_d\in\F_{q^2}$ such that
\[
 [\F_2(a_d):\F_2]=m,\qquad
 [\F_2(c_d):\F_2]=2m,\qquad
 c_d^q=c_d+1,\qquad c_d^2+c_d=a_d,
\]
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_q/\F_2}(a_d)=1,\qquad
 \Norm_{\F_{q^2}/\F_q}(c_d)=a_d,
\]
but the order of $c_d\F_q^\times$ is exactly $d$.  In particular all
proper-divisor strata obey the same degree, trace, Artin--Schreier and
relative-norm data as the Conway top step.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose $v$ of order $d$ in the norm-one subgroup of
$\F_{q^2}^{\times}$ and put
\[
 c_d=\frac1{v+1},\qquad a_d=\frac{v}{(v+1)^2}.
\]
Then $v^q=v^{-1}$, so $c_d^q=c_d+1$; the remaining displayed
identities follow directly.  The orbit calculation in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-fibotomic} gives the two field degrees.
Consequently $T^2+T+a_d$ is irreducible over $\F_q$, which is
equivalent to the stated absolute trace, and
$(c_d+1)/c_d=v$ gives quotient order $d$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the exact recursively selected factor]
\label{prop:fermat-resultant}
Set $a_{-1}=1$, $a_i=\prod_{j\leq i}c_j$, and let $A_i$ be
its minimal polynomial over $\F_2$, with $A_{-1}(Y)=Y+1$.  Then
\[
 a_i^2+a_{i-1}a_i+a_{i-1}^3=0
\]
and
\[
 A_i(X)=\operatorname{Res}_Y
 \bigl(A_{i-1}(Y),X^2+YX+Y^3\bigr).                       \tag{\(\mathsf R_i\)}
\]
Hence the unresolved Conway--Fermat lemma is the precise recursive
claim that the factor selected by \((\mathsf R_i)\) lies in the
full-order fibotomic stratum, rather than in any equal-degree
$\mathfrak F_d$ with $d\mid F_{i+1}$ and $d<F_{i+1}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Multiplying $c_i^2+c_i=a_{i-1}$ by $a_{i-1}^2$ gives the first
identity.  We prove the degree assertion and the resultant formula
inductively from $a_{-1}=1$.  The resultant in \((\mathsf R_i)\) is monic of degree
$2\deg A_{i-1}$ and vanishes at $a_i$.  Since
$c_i=a_i/a_{i-1}\notin E_{i-1}$, one has $a_i\notin E_{i-1}$.
But $E_i/\F_2$ has degree $2^{i+1}$, and every proper subfield of
$E_i$ is contained in its unique index-two subfield $E_{i-1}$.
Therefore $a_i$ has full degree $2^{i+1}=2\deg A_{i-1}$, so the
resultant is its minimal polynomial.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exact quadratic remainder descent]
\label{prop:fermat-remainder-descent}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-resultant}.  For
\(R(X)\in\F_2[X]\), divide \(R(X)\), now regarded as an element of
\(\F_2[Y][X]\), by the monic quadratic
\[
 Q_Y(X)=X^2+YX+Y^3.
\]
There are unique \(U_R,V_R\in\F_2[Y]\) such that
\[
 R(X)\equiv U_R(Y)+XV_R(Y)\pmod{Q_Y(X)}.             \tag{\(\mathsf{QR}_1\)}
\]
Then, for every \(i\geq0\),
\[
 \boxed{
 A_i(X)\mid R(X)
 \iff
 A_{i-1}(Y)\mid U_R(Y)
 \qquad\text{and}\qquad
 A_{i-1}(Y)\mid V_R(Y).}                             \tag{\(\mathsf{QR}_2\)}
\]
Consequently the selected divisibility
\(A_{n-1}\mid S_{F_n/\ell}\) can be pushed, without loss, through
every preceding Conway quadratic: iterating \((\mathsf{QR}_1)\) gives
an exact binary dependency tree of divisibilities at \(A_{-1}=Y+1\).
This criterion uses the complete deterministic ancestry, so a one-step
countermodel with the wrong predecessor does not certify the selected
divisibility.  Its leaf
conditions are nevertheless an exact re-expression of the original
selected Fibonacci value; degree alone does not make any leaf
coprimality automatic.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Euclidean division by the monic polynomial \(Q_Y\) proves existence
and uniqueness in \((\mathsf{QR}_1)\).  Substitute
\(Y=a_{i-1}\) and \(X=a_i\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-resultant}
gives \(Q_{a_{i-1}}(a_i)=0\), so
\[
 R(a_i)=U_R(a_{i-1})+a_iV_R(a_{i-1}).                 \tag{\(\mathsf{QR}_3\)}
\]
The extension
\(\F_2(a_i)/\F_2(a_{i-1})\) has degree two; hence
\(1,a_i\) is a basis over the lower field.  Thus the right side of
\((\mathsf{QR}_3)\) vanishes exactly when both lower coefficients
vanish.  The minimal-polynomial criterion gives
\((\mathsf{QR}_2)\), and iteration proves the final assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[one-branch norm descent for the Fermat arm]
\label{prop:fermat-norm-descent}
For \(R(X)\in\F_2[X]\), retain the remainder
\[
 R(X)\equiv U_R(Y)+XV_R(Y)\pmod{X^2+YX+Y^3}
\]
from Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-remainder-descent}, and define
\[
 \mathcal N(R)(Y)
 =\operatorname{Res}_X\!\left(X^2+YX+Y^3,R(X)\right).
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal N(R)
 =U_R^2+YU_RV_R+Y^3V_R^2,}                           \tag{\(\mathsf{QN}_1\)}
\]
and, for every \(i\geq0\), every actual Conway quadratic edge satisfies
\[
 \boxed{A_i\mid R\iff A_{i-1}\mid\mathcal N(R).}   \tag{\(\mathsf{QN}_2\)}
\]
After identifying \(\F_2[Y]\) with \(\F_2[X]\) by \(Y\mapsto X\),
regard \(\mathcal N\) as an endomorphism before iterating it.
Consequently, for every \(n\geq0\),
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal N^{\circ n}(R)(1)
 =\Norm_{E_{n-1}/\F_2}\!\left(R(a_{n-1})\right).}   \tag{\(\mathsf{QN}_3\)}
\]
The right side is one exactly when \(R(a_{n-1})\ne0\).

The Fibonacci family itself has a closed first norm layer.  Put
\(W_d=\mathcal N(S_d)\).  Then, for every \(d\geq0\),
\[
 W_0=0,\qquad W_1=W_2=1,\qquad W_3=1+Y+Y^3,
\]
and
\[
 \boxed{
 W_{d+4}=W_{d+3}+YW_{d+2}+Y^3W_{d+1}+Y^6W_d.}       \tag{\(\mathsf{QN}_4\)}
\]
For every \(n\geq0\), prime \(\ell\mid F_n\), and current test
\(d=F_n/\ell\), however,
\[
 \mathcal N^{\circ n}(S_d)(1)
 =\begin{cases}
   0,&\delta_n\mid d,\\
   1,&\delta_n\nmid d.
  \end{cases}                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{QN}_5\)}
\]
Thus the single terminal bit compresses the complete binary remainder
tree, but its required value one is exactly the original selected
Conway--Fermat assertion.  The recurrence exposes rather than removes
the remaining obstruction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let \(x,x'\) be the two roots of \(X^2+YX+Y^3\).  Their sum is \(Y\)
and their product is \(Y^3\), so
\[
 (U_R+xV_R)(U_R+x'V_R)
 =U_R^2+YU_RV_R+Y^3V_R^2.
\]
This is the product formula for the resultant and proves
\((\mathsf{QN}_1)\).  At \(Y=a_{i-1}\), the two roots are the
relative conjugates of \(a_i\); hence
\[
 \mathcal N(R)(a_{i-1})
 =\Norm_{E_i/E_{i-1}}R(a_i).
\]
The field norm and its argument vanish together, proving
\((\mathsf{QN}_2)\).  Iterate, use transitivity of norms, descend to
\(A_{-1}=Y+1\), and use
\(a_{-1}=1\) to obtain \((\mathsf{QN}_3)\).

For \((\mathsf{QN}_4)\), let \(\lambda_1,\lambda_2\) and
\(\mu_1,\mu_2\) be the roots of
\[
 T^2+T+x,\qquad T^2+T+x'.
\]
The four products \(\lambda_i\mu_j\) have characteristic polynomial
\[
 T^4+T^3+YT^2+Y^3T+Y^6.
\]
The Binet formula gives
\[
 W_d=S_d(x)S_d(x')
 =\sum_{i,j}(\lambda_i\mu_j)^d,
\]
so their power-sum recurrence is exactly
\((\mathsf{QN}_4)\), with the displayed initial values obtained
directly.  Finally \(S_d(a_{n-1})=0\) exactly when
\(\delta_n\mid d\), by Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-fibotomic};
\((\mathsf{QN}_3)\) now proves \((\mathsf{QN}_5)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the Fermat terminal word is the order word]
\label{cor:fermat-terminal-order-word}
For every \(n\geq0\) and \(d\geq0\), put
\[
 T_n(d)=\mathcal N^{\circ n}(S_d)(1).
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{T_n(d)=\epsilon_{\delta_n}(d).}              \tag{\(\mathsf{ZOW}_1\)}
\]
Consequently \(T_n\) has least period \(\delta_n\), exact linear complexity
\(\delta_n-1\), and exactly \(\delta_n\) states in both its \(2\)-kernel and
minimal binary DFAO.  Since \(\delta_n\mid F_n\), for every prime
\(\ell\mid F_n\),
\[
 \boxed{
 T_n(F_n/\ell)=1
 \iff v_\ell(\delta_n)=v_\ell(F_n).}                \tag{\(\mathsf{ZOW}_2\)}
\]
Thus exact quadratic ancestry compression does not reduce the state
complexity below the unknown Conway--Fermat quotient order.  It presents that
order itself as the terminal binary word.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-norm-descent} gives
\(T_n(d)=0\) exactly when \(S_d(a_{n-1})=0\), while
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-fibotomic} identifies the latter condition with
\(\delta_n\mid d\).  This proves \((\mathsf{ZOW}_1)\).
Proposition~\ref{prop:order-indicator-word} supplies the period, complexity,
and automaton assertions.  Finally, for divisors
\(\delta_n\mid F_n\), the failure of \(\delta_n\mid F_n/\ell\) is equivalent
to equality of the two \(\ell\)-adic valuations, proving
\((\mathsf{ZOW}_2)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[one-step maximality is not an inductive invariant]
\label{prop:fermat-one-step-no-go}
Let

\[
 C(Y)=Y^{16}+Y^{15}+Y^{12}+Y^{11}+Y^{10}+Y^8+1
\]

and apply the exact Conway resultant operator of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-resultant}:

\[
\begin{split}
 B(X)&=\operatorname{Res}_Y(C(Y),X^2+YX+Y^3)\\
 &=X^{32}+X^{31}+X^{30}+X^{27}+X^{26}+X^{24}
   +X^{23}+X^{22}\\
 &\qquad{}+X^{19}+X^{15}+X^{14}+X^{11}+X^8+X^7+1.
\end{split}                                                    \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_1\)}
\]

Then \(C\) is an irreducible factor of the full-order fibotomic
stratum \(\mathfrak F_{F_4}\), where \(F_4=65537\), whereas \(B\) is
an irreducible factor of the proper-order stratum

\[
 \mathfrak F_{6700417},\qquad
 6700417=F_5/641.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_2\)}
\]

Moreover, if \(y\) is a root of \(C\), then
\[
 \ord(y)=21845=\frac{2^{16}-1}{3}=5\cdot17\cdot257.    \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_{2a}\)}
\]
If \(x\) is a root of \(B\), then in fact
\[
 \ord(x)=\frac{2^{32}-1}{3}
        =F_1F_2F_3F_4.                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_{2b}\)}
\]
Thus the countermodel also satisfies the exact multiplicative-order
consequence that all preceding Conway--Fermat maximalities impose on
both its degree-sixteen element and its successor.  In
particular, the successor contains every preceding Fermat factor with
full multiplicity even though its next norm-one component loses the
factor \(641\).  Even that strengthened predecessor/successor order
data, together with the exact resultant transition, does not force
target maximality.

Thus the exact one-step resultant can send a full-order predecessor
factor to a proper-order target factor, already at the first composite
Fermat level.  In particular, predecessor maximality plus the transition
formula alone is not an induction theorem.  This is not a counterexample
to the distinguished Conway path: \(C\) is an alternative factor at the
\(A_3\) level and need not have the earlier ancestry of the actual
\(A_3\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
First, exact modular squaring gives

\[
 Y^{2^{16}}\equiv Y\pmod C,
\]

and

\[
 Y^{256}+Y\equiv
 R:=Y^{15}+Y^{14}+Y^{13}+Y^9+Y^8+Y^2+Y\pmod C.
\]

The Bézout identity \(UC+VR=1\) holds for

\[
\begin{split}
 U={}&Y^{14}+Y^{13}+Y^{12}+Y^{11}+Y^7+Y^6+Y^5+Y^4\\
    &\quad{}+Y^3+Y+1,\\
 V={}&Y^{15}+Y^{14}+Y^{12}+Y^{11}+Y^9+Y^8+Y^5+Y^3+Y+1.
\end{split}
\]

Rabin's criterion, for which two is the only prime divisor of sixteen,
therefore proves that \(C\) is irreducible.

The Fibonacci polynomials satisfy, in characteristic two,

\[
 S_{2r}=S_r^2,\qquad
 S_{2r+1}=S_{r+1}^2+YS_r^2,
\]

and hence

\[
 S_{2^m+1}(Y)=1+\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Y^{2^j}.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_3\)}
\]

For a root \(y\) of \(C\), the coefficient of \(Y^{15}\) gives
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{2^{16}}/\F_2}(y)=1\).  Equation
\((\mathsf{NI}_3)\) therefore gives
\(S_{65537}(y)=0\).  Since \(65537\) is prime, \(y\) belongs to the
full-order stratum \(\mathfrak F_{65537}\).

The predecessor's own multiplicative order is certified independently
by the exact reductions
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 Y^{21845}&\equiv1,\\
 Y^{4369}&\equiv\mathtt{7576},&
 Y^{1285}&\equiv\mathtt{1653},&
 Y^{85}&\equiv\mathtt{2464}
 \pmod C,
 \end{aligned}
\]
where hexadecimal bit \(j\) is the coefficient of \(Y^j\).  The last
three residues are not one.  Since
\(21845=5\cdot17\cdot257\), these identities prove
\((\mathsf{NI}_{2a})\).  Under
\(\delta_j=F_j\) for \(1\leq j\leq3\),
Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-popovych-equivalence} gives the same order
for \(a_3\).

Now choose \(z\) with \(z^2+z=y\) and put \(x=yz\).
The trace just computed makes \(Z^2+Z+y\) irreducible over
\(\F_{2^{16}}\).  Thus \(x\notin\F_{2^{16}}\); because every proper
subfield of \(\F_{2^{32}}\) lies in \(\F_{2^{16}}\), \(x\) has degree
thirty-two over \(\F_2\).  Moreover

\[
 x^2+yx+y^3=y^2(z^2+z+y)=0.                          \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_4\)}
\]

Modulo the relation in \((\mathsf{NI}_4)\), direct reduction gives

\[
 B(X)=C(Y)\bigl(Q_1(Y)X+Q_0(Y)\bigr),
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_5\)}
\]

where

\[
\begin{split}
 Q_1={}&Y^{29}+Y^{28}+Y^{25}+Y^{24}+Y^{21}+Y^{18}
       +Y^{17}+Y^{16}\\
      &\quad{}+Y^{15}+Y^{14}+Y^{11}+Y^{10}+Y^9+Y^6,\\
 Q_0={}&Y^{32}+Y^{31}+Y^{29}+Y^{28}+Y^{27}+Y^{26}
       +Y^{25}+Y^{23}\\
      &\quad{}+Y^{20}+Y^{14}+Y^{12}+Y^{11}+Y^{10}+Y^9+1.
\end{split}
\]

Thus \(B(x)=0\).  Its displayed degree is thirty-two, so it is the
minimal polynomial of \(x\), and consequently the stated resultant.

The multiplicative order of this successor can also be read exactly,
without sampling.  Put \(q=2^{16}\).  Since \(y^q=y\) and the two roots
of \(Z^2+Z+y\) are \(z,z+1\), one has \(z^q=z+1\).  Therefore, with
\(v=(z+1)/z\),
\[
 x^{q+1}=y^3,
 \qquad
 x^{q-1}=v.                                             \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_{5a}\)}
\]
The element \(v\) is a nonidentity element of the norm-one group of
order \(q+1=65537\); that order is prime, so \(\ord(v)=q+1\).  On the
other hand, \((\mathsf{NI}_{2a})\) and
\(\gcd(3,21845)=1\) give
\(\ord(y^3)=(q-1)/3\).

Now
\[
 \F_{q^2}^{\times}
 =\F_q^{\times}\times
   \ker\!\left(\Norm_{\F_{q^2}/\F_q}\right),
\]
because the two factor orders \(q-1\) and \(q+1\) are coprime.  Write
the corresponding components of \(x\) as \(x_-\) and \(x_+\).
Equation \((\mathsf{NI}_{5a})\) has orders
\(\ord(x_-^2)=\ord(x_-)\) and
\(\ord(x_+^{-2})=\ord(x_+)\), since both factor groups have odd order.
It follows that
\[
 \ord(x_-)=\frac{q-1}{3},\qquad
 \ord(x_+)=q+1,
\]
and their coprime product proves \((\mathsf{NI}_{2b})\).  Notice that
the loss of \(641\) below concerns the following quotient of order
\(2^{32}+1=F_5\), not either component in this decomposition; keeping
these two adjacent quadratic levels separate is essential.

It remains to identify the target stratum.  From a pair
\((f,g)=(S_r,S_{r+1})\), put

\[
 A=f^2,\qquad D=g^2+Xf^2.
\]

Reading one further binary digit sends the pair to \((A,D)\) for a
zero and to \((D,g^2)\) for a one.  Repeated reduction modulo \(B\)
along the binary word

\[
 6700417=(11001100011110110000001)_2
\]

ends at the exact residue pair

\[
 \bigl(S_{6700417},S_{6700418}\bigr)
 \equiv
 \bigl(\mathtt{00000000},\mathtt{1875a343}\bigr)
 \pmod B,                                           \tag{\(\mathsf{NI}_6\)}
\]

where a hexadecimal bit in position \(j\) is the coefficient of \(X^j\).
This is an \(O(\log 6700417)\) polynomial-identity certificate, not
field sampling.  Hence \(S_{6700417}(x)=0\).  The integer \(6700417\)
is prime, so \(x\) belongs to the exact order-\(6700417\) fibotomic
stratum.  Finally, if \(v=(z+1)/z\), then

\[
 y=\frac{v}{(v+1)^2},\qquad
 x=yz=\frac{v}{(v+1)^3},
\]

showing directly that this is the same fibotomic/elliptic transition as
the Conway resultant.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Ancestry refinements and exact no-go results}

\begin{proposition}[reciprocal-trace ancestry and a trace-refined no-go]
\label{prop:fermat-reciprocal-trace}
Every distinguished Conway factor satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_i/\mathbb F_2}(a_i^{-1})=1.       \tag{\(\mathsf{RT}_1\)}
\]
Equivalently, the constant and linear coefficients of every \(A_i\)
are both one.  More generally, this reciprocal-trace bit is preserved
by every full-degree transition of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-resultant}.

This extra ancestry invariant still does not make one-step maximality
inductive.  Put
\[
 C_1(Y)=Y^{16}+Y^{15}+Y^{12}+Y^{11}+Y^6+Y+1.
\]
Then \(C_1\) is an irreducible full-order factor of
\(\mathfrak F_{65537}\), has constant and linear coefficients one, and
its exact Conway resultant is
\[
\begin{split}
 B_1(X)={}&X^{32}+X^{31}+X^{30}+X^{28}+X^{27}+X^{26}
 +X^{24}+X^{23}+X^{22}\\
 &+X^{20}+X^{19}+X^{18}+X^{16}+X^{15}+X^{14}
 +X^{13}+X^{12}+X^{11}\\
 &+X^9+X^8+X^7+X^5+X^2+X+1.
\end{split}                                             \tag{\(\mathsf{RT}_2\)}
\]
The polynomial \(B_1\) is irreducible but belongs to the proper-order
stratum
\[
 \mathfrak F_{6700417},\qquad 6700417=F_5/641.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{RT}_3\)}
\]
Thus predecessor maximality, the exact transition, and the first
reciprocal coefficient together still do not force target maximality.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The two \(E_i/E_{i-1}\)-conjugates of \(a_i^{-1}\) have sum
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_i/E_{i-1}}(a_i^{-1})
 =\frac{a_{i-1}}{a_{i-1}^3}=a_{i-1}^{-2}.
\]
Taking absolute traces and using compatibility with squaring gives
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}(a_i^{-1})
 =\operatorname{Tr}(a_{i-1}^{-1})^2.
\]
Starting with \(a_{-1}=1\) proves \((\mathsf{RT}_1)\).  The absolute
norms are also one, so the constant coefficient is one and the linear
coefficient is norm times reciprocal trace.

For the more general preservation statement, let \(A\) be a monic
irreducible polynomial with constant coefficient one and root \(t\).
Its transition satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{Res}_Y(A(Y),X^2+YX+Y^3)
 =\operatorname{Norm}_{\mathbb F_2(t)/\mathbb F_2}
   (1+t^{-2}X+t^{-3}X^2).
\]
The coefficient of \(X\) is therefore
\(\operatorname{Tr}(t^{-2})=\operatorname{Tr}(t^{-1})^2\), the same
bit as the linear coefficient of \(A\).

We now certify the countermodel.  Modulo \(C_1\),
\[
 Y^{2^{16}}=Y,
 \qquad
 Y^{256}+Y=R:=Y^{13}+Y^9+Y^7+Y^4+Y,
\]
and the exact B\'ezout identity \(UC_1+VR=1\) holds for
\[
\begin{split}
 U&=Y^{10}+Y^9+Y^7+Y^6+Y^5+Y^4+Y^3+1,\\
 V&=Y^{13}+Y^{11}+Y^{10}+Y^7+Y^3+Y^2+1.
\end{split}
\]
Rabin's criterion proves \(C_1\) irreducible.  If \(y\) is a root,
its trace is one, and the identity
\[
 S_{2^m+1}(Y)=1+\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Y^{2^j}
\]
gives \(S_{65537}(y)=0\).  Since \(65537\) is prime, this is the
full-order stratum.

Direct expansion of the resultant gives \((\mathsf{RT}_2)\).  If
\(z^2+z=y\) and \(x=yz\), then \(x^2+yx+y^3=0\).  The trace-one
condition makes \(Z^2+Z+y\) irreducible over \(\mathbb F_{2^{16}}\),
so \(x\) has degree thirty-two.  Hence the displayed degree-thirty-two
resultant is irreducible.

Finally, repeated use of
\[
 S_{2r}=S_r^2,
 \qquad
 S_{2r+1}=S_{r+1}^2+XS_r^2
\]
along
\(6700417=(11001100011110110000001)_2\) gives the exact residues
\[
 (S_{6700417},S_{6700418})
 \equiv(0,\mathtt{3407b7e4})\pmod{B_1},
\]
where hexadecimal bit \(j\) is the coefficient of \(X^j\).  The
second residue is nonzero, and \(6700417\) is prime.  The fibotomic
criterion proves \((\mathsf{RT}_3)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[nine absolute moments do not force one-step maximality]
\label{prop:fermat-nine-moment-no-go}
The distinguished degree-sixteen factor is
\[
 A_3=X^{16}+X^{15}+X^{11}+X^{10}+X^8+X^7+X^6
      +X^3+X^2+X+1.
\]
Put
\[
 C=X^{16}+X^{15}+X^{11}+X^8+X^5+X^3+X^2+X+1.
\]
Then \(A_3+C=X^{10}+X^7+X^6+X^5\).  If \(a\) and \(y\)
are roots of \(A_3\) and \(C\), respectively, then
\[
 \bigl(\operatorname{Tr}(a^k)\bigr)_{k=1}^5
 =\bigl(\operatorname{Tr}(y^k)\bigr)_{k=1}^5
 =(1,1,1,1,0),
\]
and
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}(a^{-k})=\operatorname{Tr}(y^{-k})=1
 \qquad(1\leq k\leq4).
\]
The factor \(C\) is irreducible and lies in the full-order stratum
\(\mathfrak F_{65537}\).  Nevertheless its exact Conway resultant is
\[
\begin{split}
 B={}&X^{32}+X^{31}+X^{30}+X^{20}+X^{19}+X^{16}+X^{15}
       +X^{14}\\
    &{}+X^{11}+X^8+X^5+X+1,
\end{split}
\]
which is irreducible and lies in the proper stratum
\(\mathfrak F_{6700417}\), where \(6700417=F_5/641\).
Thus predecessor maximality, the exact transition, the first five
forward power traces, and the first four inverse-power traces still do
not force target maximality.  These are absolute \(\mathbb F_2\)-moment
data only: \(C\) does not have the distinguished relative trace--norm
ancestry of \(A_3\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a monic polynomial
\(P=X^{16}+e_1X^{15}+\cdots+e_{16}\), Newton's identities in
characteristic two read
\[
 p_k+e_1p_{k-1}+\cdots+e_{k-1}p_1+(k\bmod2)e_k=0,
 \qquad p_k=\sum_{P(r)=0}r^k.
\]
Although the final term vanishes for even \(k\), equality of
\(e_1,\ldots,e_s\) still inductively forces equality of
\(p_1,\ldots,p_s\).  The leading five coefficient tuples of \(A_3\)
and \(C\) are both \((1,0,0,0,1)\), giving
\((p_1,\ldots,p_5)=(1,1,1,1,0)\).  Apply the same identities to
\(X^{16}P(X^{-1})/P(0)\): the first four coefficient tuples are both
\((1,1,1,0)\), giving the asserted inverse traces.

We give exact polynomial certificates for the remaining claims.  In the
following hexadecimal strings, bit \(j\) is the coefficient of degree
\(j\).  Modulo \(C=\mathtt{1892f}\),
\[
 Y^{2^{16}}=Y,
 \qquad R_C:=Y^{2^8}+Y=\mathtt{3877},
\]
and
\[
 U_C=\mathtt{cca},\qquad V_C=\mathtt{73b1},\qquad
 U_CC+V_CR_C=1.
\]
Rabin's criterion proves that \(C\) is irreducible.  Its
\(Y^{15}\)-coefficient gives \(\operatorname{Tr}(y)=1\), and hence
\[
 S_{65537}(y)=1+\sum_{j=0}^{15}y^{2^j}=0.
\]
Since \(65537\) is prime, \(C\) lies in the full-order stratum.

In the quotient by \(X^2+YX+Y^3\), exact reduction gives
\[
 B(X)=C(Y)\bigl(Q_1(Y)X+Q_0(Y)\bigr),
 \quad Q_1=\mathtt{31180083},\quad Q_0=\mathtt{1a928cc53}.
\]
If \(z^2+z=y\) and \(x=yz\), then \(x^2+yx+y^3=0\).
The trace-one condition makes \(Z^2+Z+y\) irreducible over
\(\mathbb F_{2^{16}}\), so \(x\) has degree thirty-two.  Hence the
monic degree-thirty-two polynomial \(B\) is the exact resultant and is
irreducible.  Independently, its Rabin certificate is
\[
 X^{2^{32}}=X\pmod B,
 \qquad R_B:=X^{2^{16}}+X=\mathtt{e55f1c7a},
\]
\[
 U_B=\mathtt{3295d0c3},\qquad
 V_B=\mathtt{6748328a},\qquad U_BB+V_BR_B=1.
\]
Finally the exact Fibonacci residues are
\[
 (S_{6700417},S_{6700418})
 \equiv(0,\mathtt{bda50b33})\pmod B,
 \qquad S_{641}\equiv\mathtt{7acf6362}\pmod B.
\]
As \(6700417\) is prime, the fibotomic criterion places \(B\) in the
claimed exact proper-order stratum.  If \(C\) had the distinguished
relative trace--norm ancestry,
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-ancestry-exhaustion} would force it to be a
Frobenius conjugate of \(a_3\), contrary to \(C\ne A_3\); this proves
the final scope statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[higher reciprocal moments]
The full inverse-power trace sequence propagates exactly, but quickly
becomes deeper ancestry rather than a short invariant.  If \(t=a_{i-1}\)
and
\[
 D_0=0,\qquad D_1=t^{-2},\qquad
 D_j=t^{-2}D_{j-1}+t^{-3}D_{j-2},
\]
then
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_i/\mathbb F_2}(a_i^{-j})
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{E_{i-1}/\mathbb F_2}(D_j(a_{i-1})).
\]
For any fixed factor, sufficiently many moments recover its minimal
polynomial.  What is not known is any uniformly bounded initial segment
which forces the full fibotomic stratum throughout the tower.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[complete ancestry and exact trace-fibre exhaustion]
\label{prop:fermat-ancestry-exhaustion}
For \(i\geq0\) and \(-1\leq j<i\), the distinguished
elements satisfy
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_i/E_j}(a_i)=a_j,\qquad
 \operatorname{Norm}_{E_i/E_j}(a_i)=a_j^{3^{i-j}}.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{AN}_1\)}
\]
Conversely, fix \(i\geq0\) and \(x_i\in E_i\).  Put
\[
 x_j=\operatorname{Tr}_{E_i/E_j}(x_i)\qquad(-1\leq j<i).
\]
If \(x_{-1}=1\) and
\[
 \operatorname{Norm}_{E_j/E_{j-1}}(x_j)=x_{j-1}^3
 \qquad(0\leq j\leq i),                                \tag{\(\mathsf{AN}_2\)}
\]
then \(x_i\) is an \(\mathbb F_2\)-Frobenius conjugate of \(a_i\).
Thus \((\mathsf{AN}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{AN}_2)\) are a
necessary-and-sufficient ancestry invariant for the actual resultant
factor.

Now fix \(n\geq1\), put
\[
 F=E_{n-1}=\mathbb F_q,\qquad K=E_n=\mathbb F_{q^2},
\]
and let \(\ell\mid q+1\).  Set
\[
 H=(K^\times)^\ell,\qquad
 U=\ker\operatorname{Norm}_{K/F}.
\]
For every \(t\in F^\times\),
\[
 \#\{h\in H:\operatorname{Tr}_{K/F}(h)=t\}
   =\frac{q+1}{\ell}-1.                                \tag{\(\mathsf{AN}_3\)}
\]
If \(t=a_{n-1}\), every element counted in
\((\mathsf{AN}_3)\) has absolute trace one and is a normal, hence
full-degree, element of \(K/\mathbb F_2\).  Thus, whenever \(q+1\)
is composite, full degree, normality, the complete Frobenius-orbit
length, and the actual relative-trace value all occur among
\(\ell\)-th powers.

The normalized relative norms of the elements in
\((\mathsf{AN}_3)\) form exactly
\[
 \mathcal B_\ell=
 \left\{\frac{u}{(u+1)^2}:u\in U^\ell,\ u\ne1\right\},
 \qquad
 |\mathcal B_\ell|
 =\frac12\left(\frac{q+1}{\ell}-1\right).               \tag{\(\mathsf{AN}_4\)}
\]
More precisely, an element \(h\) of relative trace \(t\) is an
\(\ell\)-th power and has relative norm \(t^3\) if and only if
\(t\in\mathcal B_\ell\).  For \(t=a_{n-1}\) this is equivalent to
\[
 S_{(q+1)/\ell}(a_{n-1})=0.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{AN}_5\)}
\]
Finally \([a_n]=[c_n]\) in \(K^\times/K^{\times\ell}\).
Consequently the entire earlier ancestry is recoverable exactly, but
after the last quadratic step its power-residue content is precisely
the original fibotomic membership \((\mathsf{AN}_5)\).  Trace,
separate norm, normality, and Frobenius-orbit invariants give no
additional obstruction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The relation
\[
 a_i^2+a_{i-1}a_i+a_{i-1}^3=0
\]
is the minimal polynomial of \(a_i\) over \(E_{i-1}\).  It gives
immediate trace \(a_{i-1}\) and norm \(a_{i-1}^3\); transitivity gives
\((\mathsf{AN}_1)\).

For the converse, transitivity of trace gives
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{E_j/E_{j-1}}(x_j)=x_{j-1}\).
Together with \((\mathsf{AN}_2)\) this says that \(x_j\) is a root of
\[
 X^2+x_{j-1}X+x_{j-1}^3.
\]
Starting from \(x_{-1}=a_{-1}=1\), induct on \(j\).  If
\(x_{j-1}\) is a Frobenius conjugate of \(a_{j-1}\), the displayed
polynomial is the corresponding Frobenius conjugate of the polynomial
for \(a_j\); its two roots are the two lifts of that conjugacy to
\(E_j\).  Hence \(x_j\), and finally \(x_i\), is a Frobenius conjugate
of the distinguished element.

Since \(\gcd(\ell,q-1)=1\), one has \(F^\times\subset H\).
Multiplication by any \(t\in F^\times\) bijects the trace-one and
trace-\(t\) fibres in \(H\).  In characteristic two,
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{K/F}(h)=0\) is equivalent to \(h^q=h\), so the
nonzero trace-zero elements are exactly \(F^\times\), all of which lie
in \(H\).  If the common nonzero-fibre size is \(M\), then
\[
 \frac{q^2-1}{\ell}=|H|=(q-1)+(q-1)M,
\]
which gives \((\mathsf{AN}_3)\).

The distinguished \(a_{n-1}\) has absolute trace one by
\((\mathsf{AN}_1)\).  Thus every \(h\) in its relative-trace fibre
also has absolute trace one.  To justify normality, let
\(N=[K:\mathbb F_2]\), a power of two, and let \(\rho\) generate its
cyclic Galois group.  After choosing one normal generator, \(K\) is
the regular module over
\[
 \mathbb F_2[\langle\rho\rangle]
 \simeq\mathbb F_2[X]/((X+1)^N),
\]
a local ring.  An element is a normal generator exactly when its
group-ring coordinate is a unit, equivalently when multiplication by
\((\rho+1)^{N-1}\) does not kill it.  Since
\[
 (\rho+1)^{N-1}=1+\rho+\cdots+\rho^{N-1},
\]
this last value is its absolute trace.  Hence absolute trace one is
equivalent to normality.

Write \(h=tz\) in a nonzero trace fibre.  Then
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{K/F}(z)=1\), so \(z^q=z+1\) and
\[
 \operatorname{Norm}_{K/F}(h)=t^2z(z+1).
\]
Put \(u=(z+1)/z\in U\); then \(z=1/(u+1)\) and
\[
 z(z+1)=\frac{u}{(u+1)^2}.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{AN}_6\)}
\]
We claim \(z\in H\) if and only if \(u\in U^\ell\).  The forward
implication follows from \(u=z^{q-1}\).  Conversely, if
\(u\in U^\ell\), then \(u\in H\).  The right side of
\((\mathsf{AN}_6)\) lies in \(F^\times\subset H\), so in
\(K^\times/H\) one has
\[
 [u]=2[u+1].
\]
Because \(\ell\) is odd, multiplication by two is invertible; hence
\([u]=0\) forces \([u+1]=0\), and
\(z=(u+1)^{-1}\in H\).  The map
\(u\mapsto u/(u+1)^2\) identifies precisely the inverse pair
\(\{u,u^{-1}\}\), proving \((\mathsf{AN}_4)\).

The equality \(\operatorname{Norm}(h)=t^3\) is, after division by
\(t^2\), exactly \(t=z(z+1)\).  This proves the first assertion
following \((\mathsf{AN}_4)\).  The set \(U^\ell\) has order
\((q+1)/\ell\), and the Fibonacci-polynomial factorization identifies
its nontrivial inverse pairs with the roots of
\(S_{(q+1)/\ell}\), proving \((\mathsf{AN}_5)\).  Finally
\(a_n=a_{n-1}c_n\) and \(a_{n-1}\in F^\times\subset H\), so
\([a_n]=[c_n]\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[a completely normal primitive candidate]
\label{cor:fermat-completely-normal}
For every \(n\geq0\), the elements \(a_n\) and \(c_0a_n\) are normal
over every intermediate field \(E_j\), \(-1\leq j<n\).  In particular
they are completely normal over \(\mathbb F_2\).  Moreover

\[
 \ord(c_0a_n)=3\prod_{j=1}^n\delta_j,                 \tag{\(\mathsf{CN}_1\)}
\]

so

\[
 c_0a_n\text{ is primitive in }E_n
 \iff \delta_j=F_j\quad(1\leq j\leq n).              \tag{\(\mathsf{CN}_2\)}
\]

Thus the singleton-even conjecture may equivalently be stated as
primitivity of one explicit completely normal element.

By contrast, the Popovych product \(c_0c_n\) is normal only over the
immediately preceding field when \(n\geq2\):

\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_n/E_{n-1}}(c_0c_n)=c_0,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_n/E_j}(c_0c_n)=0
 \quad(-1\leq j\leq n-2).                             \tag{\(\mathsf{CN}_3\)}
\]

Hence complete normality does not supply the missing primary order,
and no absolute normal-basis theorem applies directly to \(c_0c_n\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
For a cyclic extension \(L/K\) of characteristic two and degree a power
of two, an element \(x\in L\) is normal over \(K\) if and only if
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{L/K}(x)\ne0\).  Indeed, after choosing a normal
generator, the regular module is

\[
 K[C_{2^r}]\simeq K[X]/((X+1)^{2^r}),
\]

a local ring.  The coordinate of \(x\) is a unit exactly when its
augmentation is nonzero, and that augmentation is nonzero exactly when
the relative trace of \(x\) is nonzero.

Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-ancestry-exhaustion} gives

\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_n/E_j}(a_n)=a_j\ne0
 \qquad(-1\leq j<n),
\]

so \(a_n\) is normal over every \(E_j\).  For \(j\geq0\), the scalar
\(c_0\) lies in \(E_j\), and hence

\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_n/E_j}(c_0a_n)=c_0a_j\ne0.
\]

At the bottom,

\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{E_n/\mathbb F_2}(c_0a_n)
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{E_0/\mathbb F_2}(c_0a_0)
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{E_0/\mathbb F_2}(c_0^2)=1.
\]

This proves complete normality.  Corollary~\ref{cor:fermat-popovych-equivalence}
gives \(\ord(a_n)=\prod_{j=1}^n\delta_j\), which is prime to three;
since \(c_0\) has order three, \((\mathsf{CN}_1)\) follows.  The identity

\[
 |E_n^\times|=3\prod_{j=1}^nF_j
\]

and the pairwise coprimality of the Fermat numbers prove
\((\mathsf{CN}_2)\).

Finally the quadratic equation for \(c_n\) has trace one, so the first
trace in \((\mathsf{CN}_3)\) is \(c_0\).  Every further relative
trace takes the lower-field element \(c_0\) through a positive even
extension degree and is therefore zero in characteristic two.  The
normality criterion above completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\(\ell\)-th-root ancestry and the exact Dickson obstruction]
\label{prop:fermat-root-ancestry}
For variables \(S,P\), define the characteristic-two Dickson power
sums by
\[
 \mathcal D_0(S,P)=0,\qquad
 \mathcal D_1(S,P)=S,\qquad
 \mathcal D_{r+2}(S,P)
   =S\mathcal D_{r+1}(S,P)+P\mathcal D_r(S,P).
\]
