$X^{2h}+X^h+1$ the irreducible minimal polynomial of $\zeta$.
We first prove that $N=\zeta^h+\zeta^2+\zeta$ also has degree $2h$.
Suppose $N^{2^j}=N$, and put $a\equiv2^j\pmod{3h}$.  The resulting
vanishing polynomial, of degree less than $3h$, has support equal to
the symmetric difference of
\[
 \{ah,a,2a\}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\{h,1,2\}.                \tag{1}
\]
Every multiple of $X^{2h}+X^h+1$ of degree less than $3h$ has support
a disjoint union of triples $\{r,r+h,r+2h\}$.
If $a\equiv2\pmod3$, the support in (1) contains $h$ and $2h$ but not
$0$, a contradiction.  If $a\equiv1\pmod3$, the $h$-terms cancel and
each residue class modulo $h$ occurs at most twice; a nonempty union of
such triples is again impossible.  Hence the support is empty, so
$\{a,2a\}=\{1,2\}$ modulo $3h$.  Since $h\geq3$, this forces $a=1$.
Thus the Frobenius orbit of $N$ has length $2h$, and the displayed
resultant is its irreducible minimal polynomial.

Writing $A=2^{3^{k-1}}$ gives
$\Phi_{2\cdot3^k}(2)=A^2-A+1\equiv3\pmod9$, so division by $3$
removes exactly the prime dividing the cyclotomic index.  The standard
cyclotomic-divisor criterion now says that the prime divisors of
$\Psi_k$ are precisely the $\ell$ with $\ord_\ell(2)=2h$.  Their
entire primary contribution to $2^{2h}-1$ lies in this cyclotomic
factor and, since $\ell\nmid2^h-1$, in $2^h+1$.  This proves the
valuation statement.

Fix $\ell\mid\Psi_k$.  Propositions~\ref{prop:translates}
and~\ref{prop:exception} make the translates
$\zeta,\zeta+1,\zeta+2,\zeta+3$ into $\ell$-th powers, so
$m_\ell\geq4$.  The corrected norm and the exact power criterion give
\[
 \beta^{(2^{4h}-1)/\ell}
 =N^{(2^{2h}-1)/\ell},
\]
and therefore
\[
 m_\ell=4\iff N\notin(K^\times)^\ell.                    \tag{2}
\]
This uses the full $\ell$-primary valuation and has no squarefreeness
or non-Wieferich hypothesis.  Since $q-1\equiv-2\pmod\ell$, raising
to $q-1$ is invertible on $K^\times/(K^\times)^\ell$, so
\[
 N\in(K^\times)^\ell\iff M_k\in(K^\times)^\ell.           \tag{3}
\]
The entire $\ell$-primary part of $K^\times$ lies in its norm-one
factor of order $q+1$.  In a cyclic group, nonmembership in the
subgroup of $\ell$-th powers is equivalent to the order containing the
full $\ell$-primary part.  Equations (2)--(3) prove the equivalence of
the first three assertions.

Finally $\mu_\ell\subset K$.  Kummer theory says that
$X^\ell-N$ is irreducible over $K$ exactly when
$N\notin(K^\times)^\ell$.  Since $K=\F_2(N)$, Capelli's lemma says in
turn that this is equivalent to irreducibility of $R_k(X^\ell)$ over
$\F_2$, completing the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[$D'_k$: the exact current exception factor]
\label{conj:dk}
\emph{Conjecture.} One has
\[
 D'_k:\qquad \Psi_k\mid\ord(M_k).
\]
By Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-current-level}, this is exactly the
$m=4$ upper bound for the primes at level $f=2\cdot3^k$.
\end{conjecture}

\subsection{Projection, fibotomic, and translated-binomial forms}

\begin{proposition}[exact current-layer projection and dominant-factor sieve]
\label{prop:dk-projection}
Let \(k\geq2\), put
\[
 s=3^{k-1},\qquad h=3s,\qquad a=2^s,\qquad q=a^3=2^h,
 \qquad \Lambda_k=a^2-a+1=3\Psi_k,
\]
and retain \(N,M_k,\zeta,\omega=\zeta^h\) from
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-current-level}.  Define
\[
 P_k:=M_k^{a+1}.
\]
Then \(P_k^{\Lambda_k}=1\), and for every prime
\(\ell\mid\Psi_k\),
\[
 v_\ell(\ord P_k)=v_\ell(\ord M_k).                    \tag{\(\mathsf D_1\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 P_k=\frac{\zeta^4+\zeta+\omega}
           {\omega^2\zeta^4+\zeta^3+1}.               \tag{\(\mathsf D_2\)}
\]
\[
 [\F_2(P_k):\F_2]=2h.                                 \tag{\(\mathsf D_3\)}
\]
and
\[
 v_3(\ord P_k)=
 \begin{cases}1,&k\text{ odd},\\0,&k\text{ even}.
 \end{cases}                                           \tag{\(\mathsf D_4\)}
\]
Set \(c_k=3\) for odd \(k\) and \(c_k=1\) for even \(k\).  Then
\[
 \ord(P_k)\geq c_k(2h+1).                              \tag{\(\mathsf D_5\)}
\]
Consequently, if \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\) and
\[
 \ell>\frac{\Lambda_k}{c_k(2h+1)},                    \tag{\(\mathsf D_6\)}
\]
then \(P_k\), and hence \(M_k\), contains the full
\(\ell\)-primary part of \(\Lambda_k\) in its order.  In particular
\(D'_k\) follows whenever \(\Psi_k\) is prime; this proves \(D'_2\)
and \(D'_3\) analytically, since \(\Psi_2=19\) and
\(\Psi_3=87211\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The factorization
\[
 q+1=(a+1)(a^2-a+1)=(a+1)\Lambda_k
\]
and \(M_k^{q+1}=1\) give \(P_k^{\Lambda_k}=1\).  Also
\(\gcd(\Lambda_k,a+1)=3\).  Since \(k\geq2\), one has
\(a\equiv-1\pmod9\), so
\(\Lambda_k\equiv3\pmod9\) and
\(\gcd(\Psi_k,a+1)=1\).  Exponentiation by \(a+1\) therefore preserves
all the primary valuations belonging to \(\Psi_k\), proving
\((\mathsf D_1)\).

The lifted congruence
\[
 2^{3^{k-1}}\equiv3^k-1\pmod{3^{k+1}}
\]
gives, modulo \(3h\),
\[
 a\equiv h-1,\quad a^2\equiv h+1,\quad
 a^3\equiv-1,\quad a^4\equiv2h+1.                     \tag{\(\mathsf D_7\)}
\]
As \(M_k=N^{a^3-1}\),
\[
 P_k=\frac{N^{a^4}N^{a^3}}{N^aN}.
\]
Using \(N=\zeta^2+\zeta+\omega\) and \((\mathsf D_7)\), direct Frobenius
expansion gives
\begin{align*}
 N^a&=\omega^2\zeta^{-2}+\omega\zeta^{-1}+\omega^2,\\
 N^{a^3}&=\zeta^{-2}+\zeta^{-1}+\omega^2,\\
 N^{a^4}&=\omega\zeta^2+\omega^2\zeta+\omega.
\end{align*}
Multiplying \(N^{a^3}\) and \(N^a\) by the same cancelling factor
\(\zeta^2\), and using \(1+\omega+\omega^2=0\), yields
\begin{align*}
 N^{a^4}(\zeta^2N^{a^3})&=\zeta^4+\zeta+\omega,\\
 (\zeta^2N^a)N&=\omega^2\zeta^4+\zeta^3+1,
\end{align*}
which proves \((\mathsf D_2)\).

The minimal polynomial of \(\zeta\) over \(\F_2\) is
\(X^{2h}+X^h+1\).  Write the numerator and denominator in
\((\mathsf D_2)\) as
\(C,D\).  Since \(P_k^q=P_k^{-1}\), membership in \(\F_q\) would force
\(P_k=1\).  But \(C=D\), after reducing
\(\zeta^{2h}=\zeta^h+1\), would give
\[
 \zeta^{h+4}+\zeta^h+\zeta^3+\zeta+1=0,               \tag{\(\mathsf D_8\)}
\]
a nonzero polynomial of degree \(h+4<2h\).

If \(P_k\in\F_{a^2}\), its order divides
\(\gcd(\Lambda_k,a^2-1)=3\), so
\(P_k\in\{1,\omega,\omega^2\}\).  The first case was excluded; the
other two respectively give
\[
 \zeta^{h+3}+\zeta=0
\]
and
\[
 \zeta^{h+4}+\zeta^{h+3}+\zeta^4+\zeta^3+\zeta+1=0.
\]
Both are again nonzero polynomials of degree below \(2h\).  Every proper
divisor of \(2h=2\cdot3^k\) divides either \(h\) or \(2h/3\); hence every
proper subfield of \(\F_{2^{2h}}\) lies in either \(\F_q\) or
\(\F_{a^2}\).  This proves \((\mathsf D_3)\).

For the \(3\)-part, observe that
\[
 P_k^{\Lambda_k/3}
 =M_k^{(q+1)/3}
 =N^{(q^2-1)/3}
 =\Norm_{\F_{2^{2h}}/\F_4}(N).                         \tag{\(\mathsf D_9\)}
\]
Choose \(u\in\F_{16}\) with \(u^2+u=\omega\).  Then
\(u^4=u+1\), \(u^5=\omega\), and \(u\) has order \(15\).  The
extensions \(\F_{2^{2h}}/\F_4\) and \(\F_{16}/\F_4\) are linearly
disjoint, and over \(\F_{16}\) the minimal polynomial of \(\zeta\)
is \(X^h+\omega\), irreducible because
\(\ord_{3h}(16)=h\).  Since
\[
 N=(\zeta+u)(\zeta+u+1),
\]
base change in \((\mathsf D_9)\) gives
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{2^{2h}}/\F_4}(N)
 =(u^h+\omega)((u+1)^h+\omega).
\]
The residues of \(h=3^k\) modulo \(15\) are \(3,9,12,6\) as
\(k\equiv1,2,3,0\pmod4\); reduction with \(u^4=u+1\) gives
\[
 P_k^{\Lambda_k/3}=
 \begin{cases}\omega^2,&k\text{ odd},\\1,&k\text{ even}.
 \end{cases}
\]
Because \(v_3(\Lambda_k)=1\), this proves \((\mathsf D_4)\).

Let \(e=\ord(P_k)\).  From \((\mathsf D_3)\),
\(\ord_e(2)=2h\).  Since
\(e\mid\Lambda_k\) and \(e\notin\{1,3\}\), some prime
\(r\mid\Psi_k\) divides \(e\); every such \(r\) has
\(\ord_r(2)=2h\), so \(r\equiv1\pmod{2h}\) and
\(r\geq2h+1\).  When \(k\) is odd, \((\mathsf D_4)\) also gives
\(3r\mid e\), which proves \((\mathsf D_5)\).

If the full \(\ell\)-primary part of \(\Lambda_k\) were missing from
\(e\), then \(e\mid\Lambda_k/\ell\).  This contradicts
\((\mathsf D_5)\) under \((\mathsf D_6)\).
Equation \((\mathsf D_1)\) transfers the conclusion back to \(M_k\), and the
last assertions follow.
\end{proof}


\begin{proposition}[fibotomic form of the current power-residue test]
\label{prop:dk-fibotomic}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-projection}, put
\[
 U=\{x\in\F_{q^2}^{\times}:x^{q+1}=1\},\qquad
 \pi(x)=\frac{x}{(x+1)^2}=\frac1{x+x^{-1}},
\]
and set \(Z=\pi(M_k)\).  The map \(\pi\) is two-to-one from
\(U\setminus\{1\}\) onto
\[
 \mathcal T_q=\{z\in\F_q:
   \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_q/\F_2}(z)=1\},
\]
with fibres \(\{x,x^{-1}\}\).

For an odd prime \(\ell\), define
\[
 H_\ell(W)=\sum_{j=0}^{(\ell-1)/2}
   \binom{\ell-j-1}{j}W^j\in\F_2[W].
\]
If \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), then
\[
 M_k\in U^\ell
 \iff
 \text{there is }W\in\mathcal T_q\text{ with }
 W^\ell+Z H_\ell(W)^2=0.                               \tag{\(\mathsf F\)}
\]
The polynomial
\[
 \mathcal F_{\ell,Z}(W)=W^\ell+ZH_\ell(W)^2
\]
either has no root in \(\F_q\), or splits there into \(\ell\)
distinct linear factors.  Finally \(H_\ell\) is squarefree and is a
product of exactly \((\ell-1)/(2h)\) irreducible polynomials over
\(\F_2\), each of degree \(h\).  Thus \(D'_k\) is equivalently the
assertion that \(\mathcal F_{\ell,Z}\) is rootless over \(\F_q\) for
every prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For \(x\in U\), one has \(x^q=x^{-1}\), so \(\pi(x)\in\F_q\).
The equation \(\pi(x)=z\) is
\[
 X^2+z^{-1}X+1=0.
\]
The characteristic-two quadratic criterion says that this polynomial
is irreducible over \(\F_q\) exactly when
\(\operatorname{Tr}(z)=1\).  Its roots are inverse and have norm one.
Counting, or the same quadratic argument in reverse, proves the stated
two-to-one description.

The defining polynomial identity is
\[
 \frac{X^\ell+1}{(X+1)^\ell}
 =H_\ell\!\left(\frac{X}{(X+1)^2}\right).              \tag{\(\mathsf H\)}
\]
If \(M_k=T^\ell\) with \(T\in U\), put \(W=\pi(T)\).  Identity
\((\mathsf H)\) gives
\[
 Z=\pi(T^\ell)=\frac{W^\ell}{H_\ell(W)^2},
\]
so \(W\) satisfies \((\mathsf F)\).  Conversely, lift a trace-one
solution \(W\) to \(T\in U\).  Then \((\mathsf H)\) gives
\(\pi(T^\ell)=\pi(M_k)\), hence \(T^\ell=M_k\) or \(M_k^{-1}\).
Replacing \(T\) by \(T^{-1}\) in the second case proves
\(M_k\in U^\ell\).

In fact every \(\F_q\)-root of \(\mathcal F_{\ell,Z}\) has trace one.
Indeed, \(H_\ell(W)\ne0\) at a root, since otherwise the equation
would force \(W=0\), whereas \(H_\ell(0)=1\).  Lift a nonzero root
through \(\pi(T)=W\).  If \(\operatorname{Tr}(W)=0\), the quadratic
criterion puts \(T\) in \(\F_q\).  Identity \((\mathsf H)\) then gives
\(\pi(T^\ell)=Z\), but for \(y\in\F_q\setminus\{1\}\),
\[
 \pi(y)=\left(\frac{y}{y+1}\right)^2+\frac{y}{y+1}
\]
has absolute trace zero, whereas \(Z=\pi(M_k)\) has trace one.  This
is a contradiction.

If one root exists, therefore lift it to \(T\in U\) and orient \(T\)
so that \(T^\ell=M_k\).  Then the
\(\ell\) elements
\[
 \pi(T\xi),\qquad \xi\in\mu_\ell,
\]
are roots in \(\F_q\).  They are distinct: an inverse collision would
give \(T^2\in\mu_\ell\), hence \(M_k^2=1\), whereas \(M_k\ne1\)
because \(N\notin\F_q\).  Since \(\mathcal F_{\ell,Z}\) has degree
\(\ell\), it splits completely.  Its derivative is \(W^{\ell-1}\),
and trace-one roots are nonzero, so they are simple.

Finally, the roots of \(H_\ell\) are
\[
 \pi(\xi),\qquad \xi\in\mu_\ell\setminus\{1\},
\]
with \(\xi\) and \(\xi^{-1}\) identified.  Their Frobenius-orbit
length is the least \(d>0\) with \(2^d\equiv\pm1\pmod\ell\).  Since
\(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\), this least \(d\) is \(h\).  The number and degrees
of the factors follow, and the roots are distinct, proving
squarefreeness.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Conway \(C\)-to-\(D\) selector bridge]
\label{prop:cd-selector-bridge}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-fibotomic}; in particular \(k\geq2\).  Put
\[
 h=3^k,\qquad t=2^{h/3},\qquad q=2^h=t^3,
\]
and write
\[
 \gamma=\gamma_k=\zeta+\zeta^{-1},\qquad
 g_k=\gamma^2+\gamma+1.
\]
For the corrected exceptional norm
\(N=\zeta^2+\zeta+\omega\), with
\(\overline N=N^q\), one has
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{q^2}/\F_q}(N)=g_k,
 \qquad
 \Norm_{\F_{q^2}/\F_q}(N)=g_k^{t^2}.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_1\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 N^2+g_kN+g_k^{t^2}=0.                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_2\)}
\]
The auxiliary periods are norm coherent:
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{2^{3^k}}/\F_{2^{3^{k-1}}}}(g_k)=g_{k-1},
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{2^{3^k}}/\F_{2^{3^{k-1}}}}(g_k)=1.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_3\)}
\]
In particular
\[
 \F_2(g_k)=\F_2(\gamma_k)=\F_q,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_q/\F_2}(g_k)=1.                \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_4\)}
\]

The coefficient \(Z=\pi(M_k)\) in
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-fibotomic} is therefore the explicit
ancestral power
\[
 \boxed{Z=g_k^{t^2-2}.}                              \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_5\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 \gcd(t^2-2,t^3-1)=1,                                \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_6\)}
\]
so \(Z\) and \(g_k\) have the same multiplicative order and generate
the same field over \(\F_2\).  If
\[
 w=\frac N{g_k},
\]
then
\[
 w^2+w+Z=0,
 \qquad
 \overline w=w+1,
 \qquad
 M_k=\frac{w+1}{w}=w^{q-1}.                          \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_7\)}
\]
For every current prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), failure at \(\ell\) is
equivalent to each of
\[
 M_k\in U^\ell,qquad
 w\in(\F_{q^2}^{\times})^\ell,qquad
 \mathcal F_{\ell,g_k^{t^2-2}}(W)
   \text{ having a root in }\F_q.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_8\)}
\]
The two roots \(w,w+1=w^q\) have the same \(\ell\)-power status, while
their Kummer classes are opposite.  Thus the remaining datum is the
unordered opposite pair \(\{[w],-[w]\}\), equivalently the norm-one
phase \(\{M_k,M_k^{-1}\}\); it is not a choice of orientation between
the two roots.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The \(q\)-Frobenius sends \(\zeta\) to \(\zeta^{-1}\) and
\(\omega\) to \(\omega^2\).  Hence
\[
 N+\overline N
 =\zeta^2+\zeta^{-2}+\zeta+\zeta^{-1}+\omega+\omega^2
 =\gamma^2+\gamma+1=g_k.
\]
The relation \(\zeta^t=\omega\zeta^{-1}\) from
Proposition~\ref{prop:eta-tower}, together with direct expansion, gives
\[
 N\overline N
 =1+\gamma+\gamma^t+(\gamma^t+\gamma)^2.
\]
The three conjugates of \(\gamma_k\) over the preceding cubic field
sum to zero, so
\(\gamma+\gamma^t+\gamma^{t^2}=0\).  Therefore
\[
 N\overline N
 =1+\gamma^{t^2}+\gamma^{2t^2}=g_k^{t^2},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{CD}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{CD}_2)\).

Let \(x_1,x_2,x_3\) be the roots of
\(P(X)=X^3+X+\gamma_{k-1}\).  Since
\(X^2+X+1=(X+\omega)(X+\omega^2)\),
\[
 \begin{split}
 \prod_{i=1}^3(x_i^2+x_i+1)
 &=P(\omega)P(\omega^2)\\
 &=(\gamma_{k-1}+\omega^2)
   (\gamma_{k-1}+\omega)
 =g_{k-1}.
 \end{split}
\]
This is the norm identity in \((\mathsf{CD}_3)\).  The relative trace
is
\[
 \sum_i(x_i^2+x_i+1)
 =\left(\sum_i x_i\right)^2+\sum_i x_i+1=1.
\]
The element \(\gamma\) is at most quadratic over \(\F_2(g_k)\), while
\([\F_2(\gamma):\F_2]=h\) is odd.  Hence that possible quadratic
index is one, proving the field equality in \((\mathsf{CD}_4)\).
Transitivity applied to the relative trace one proves the absolute
trace assertion.

For \(M_k=\overline N/N\), elementary cancellation gives
\[
 \pi(M_k)
 =\frac{\overline N/N}{(\overline N/N+1)^2}
 =\frac{N\overline N}{(N+\overline N)^2}
 =g_k^{t^2-2},
\]
proving \((\mathsf{CD}_5)\).  If an integer divides both
\(t^2-2\) and \(t^3-1\), then it divides \(2t-1\), and hence divides
seven.  Since \(k\geq2\), one has \(t\equiv1\pmod7\), and therefore
seven does not divide \(t^2-2\).
This proves \((\mathsf{CD}_6)\), and exponentiation by \(t^2-2\) is
therefore an automorphism of the cyclic group \(\F_q^\times\).

Dividing \((\mathsf{CD}_2)\) by \(g_k^2\) gives the first identity in
\((\mathsf{CD}_7)\); the remaining two follow from relative trace one.
For a current prime, \(\ell\mid q+1\) and \(\ell\nmid q-1\).
The direct product \(\F_{q^2}^\times=\F_q^\times\times U\) gives
\[
 U^\ell=U\cap(\F_{q^2}^\times)^\ell,
\]
because \(\ell\)-powering is an automorphism of \(\F_q^\times\).
Multiplication by \(q-1\) is consequently invertible on the
one-dimensional \(\ell\)-power quotient of \(\F_{q^2}^\times\), so
\[
 w^{q-1}\in U^\ell
 \iff w\in(\F_{q^2}^\times)^\ell.
\]
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-fibotomic} supplies the final equivalence in
\((\mathsf{CD}_8)\).  Frobenius preserves \(\ell\)-th powers and
sends \(w\) to \(w+1\), proving the last assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[what the selector bridge does not transfer]
The bridge transports the complete selected \(D\)-coefficient through
the recursively selected \(C\)-period and the norm-coherent tower
\((g_k)\), but it is not an implication \(C_k\Rightarrow D'_k\).
The current primes in the two arms satisfy respectively
\(\ord_\ell(2)=h\) and \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\), so they are disjoint.
At a \(D\)-current prime every element of \(\F_q^\times\), including
\(g_k\) and \(Z\), is automatically an \(\ell\)-th power.  The missing
class lives in the selected quadratic root \(w\), not in its base-field
trace or norm.  There is no known uniform exponent, coprime to \(q-1\),
which expresses \(g_k=\gamma_k^2+\gamma_k+1\) as a power of
\(\gamma_k\); the map \(x\mapsto x^2+x+1\) need not preserve
multiplicative order.  Thus \(C\)-primitivity supplies no order transfer
to \(g_k\).
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[\(C\)-tower-faithful exceptional countermodel]
\label{prop:dk-tower-countermodel}
Let \(k\geq1\), \(h=3^k\), \(q=2^h\), and retain the compatible
selected Conway roots \(\zeta_j=\kappa_{3^j}\), so
\(\zeta_{j-1}=\zeta_j^3\).  Thus
\(\zeta_k\in\F_{q^2}\) has order \(3h\) and
\(\zeta_k^q=\zeta_k^{-1}\).  Put
\[
 \gamma_k=\zeta_k+\zeta_k^{-1},\qquad
 Z_k^\circ=\gamma_k^{-1}
   =\frac{\zeta_k}{(\zeta_k+1)^2},\qquad
 w_k^\circ=\frac1{\zeta_k+1}.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{TC}_1\)}
\]
Then
\[
 (w_k^\circ)^2+w_k^\circ=Z_k^\circ,\qquad
 (w_k^\circ)^q=w_k^\circ+1,\qquad
 \frac{w_k^\circ+1}{w_k^\circ}
 =(w_k^\circ)^{q-1}=\zeta_k.                         \tag{\(\mathsf{TC}_2\)}
\]
The coefficient \(Z_k^\circ\in\F_q\) has exact degree \(h\) over
\(\F_2\), satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_q/\F_2}(Z_k^\circ)=1,         \tag{\(\mathsf{TC}_3\)}
\]
and, for \(k\geq2\), is norm coherent:
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{2^{3^k}}/\F_{2^{3^{k-1}}}}(Z_k^\circ)
 =Z_{k-1}^\circ.                                     \tag{\(\mathsf{TC}_4\)}
\]
Moreover
\[
 \ord(Z_k^\circ)=\ord(\gamma_k),                     \tag{\(\mathsf{TC}_5\)}
\]
so \(C_k\) makes \(Z_k^\circ\) primitive in \(\F_q^\times\).

Now let \(k\geq2\), let \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), and put
\(a=v_\ell(q+1)=v_\ell(\Psi_k)\).  Then this model misses the entire
current primary factor:
\[
 \zeta_k\in U^{\ell^a},\qquad
 w_k^\circ\in(\F_{q^2}^\times)^{\ell^a},\qquad
 v_\ell\!\left(
   \ord\frac{w_k^\circ+1}{w_k^\circ}
 \right)=0.                                          \tag{\(\mathsf{TC}_6\)}
\]
This is not a counterexample to \(D'_k\): its coefficient is
\(Z_k^\circ=\gamma_k^{-1}\), whereas the actual Conway coefficient is
\[
 Z_{D,k}=g_k^{t^2-2},\qquad
 g_k=\gamma_k^2+\gamma_k+1,\qquad t=2^{h/3}.
\]
It proves that trace one, exact degree, norm-coherent actual
\(C\)-period ancestry, and even \(C_k\)-primitivity of an
Artin--Schreier constant give no direct current-primary order transfer.
It does not rule out an implication \(C_k\Rightarrow D'_k\) for the
actual selector; any such implication must use the special rational
function selecting \(Z_{D,k}\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since
\((\zeta_k+1)^2=\zeta_k(\zeta_k+\zeta_k^{-1})\),
the identities in \((\mathsf{TC}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{TC}_2)\) are direct.
The element \(\zeta_k\) has irreducible quadratic
\[
 X^2+\gamma_kX+1
\]
over \(\F_q\).  Dividing by \(\gamma_k^2\) gives an
Artin--Schreier equation with coefficient \(\gamma_k^{-2}\), so that
coefficient has absolute trace one.  Absolute trace is invariant under
squaring, hence \(\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma_k^{-1})=1\).  The field
\(\F_2(\gamma_k)\) has degree \(h\), and inversion preserves it, which
proves the degree assertion.  Proposition~\ref{prop:tower} gives
\(\Norm(\gamma_k)=\gamma_{k-1}\); inversion proves
\((\mathsf{TC}_4)\).  Equation \((\mathsf{TC}_5)\) is immediate.

For a current prime, \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\), so
\(\ell\nmid3h\).  The element \(\zeta_k\), whose order is \(3h\), is
therefore an \(\ell^a\)-th power in the norm-one group \(U\).
Also \(\ell\nmid q-1\).  In the direct product
\[
 \F_{q^2}^\times=\F_q^\times\times U,
\]
\(\ell^a\)-powering is an automorphism on the first factor, while
raising the second factor to \(q-1\) is invertible on its
\(\ell\)-primary power quotient.  The last identity in
\((\mathsf{TC}_2)\) therefore gives
\(w_k^\circ\in(\F_{q^2}^\times)^{\ell^a}\), and the order assertion
follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the actual exceptional root's lower norm]
\label{prop:dk-normalized-root-norm}
Retain \(k\geq2\), \(h=3^k\), \(t=2^{h/3}\), and the actual normalized
root
\[
 w_k=\frac{N_k}{g_k},\qquad
 N_k=\zeta_k^2+\zeta_k+\omega
\]
from Proposition~\ref{prop:cd-selector-bridge}.  Put
\(\eta=\zeta_k^3\).  Then
\[
 \boxed{
 \Norm_{\F_{2^{2h}}/\F_{2^{2h/3}}}(w_k)
 =\frac{\eta^2+\omega^2\eta+1}{g_{k-1}}
 =\frac{\eta^2}{\eta^2+\omega\eta+1}.}                \tag{\(\mathsf{TN}_1\)}
\]
For every current prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), even this exact lower norm
is automatically an \(\ell^{v_\ell(\Psi_k)}\)-th power in
\(\F_{2^{2h/3}}^\times\).  It therefore carries no current Kummer
coordinate.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:twisted}'s twisted norm formula for \(N_k\),
divided by the norm-coherence formula for \(g_k\), gives the first expression in
\((\mathsf{TN}_1)\).  In characteristic two,
\[
 g_{k-1}
 =\eta^{-2}(\eta^4+\eta^3+\eta^2+\eta+1)
 =\eta^{-2}
   (\eta^2+\omega\eta+1)
   (\eta^2+\omega^2\eta+1),
\]
which gives the second.  Finally
\(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\), so
\(\ell\nmid 2^{2h/3}-1\).  Powering by the full current
\(\ell\)-primary factor is therefore bijective in the lower
multiplicative group.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[coprime torus projections of the exceptional root]
\label{prop:dk-coprime-projections}
Retain \(k\geq2\), and put
\[
 t=2^{3^{k-1}},\qquad Q=t^2,\qquad
 A=Q+t+1,\qquad B=Q-t+1=3\Psi_k,
\]
\[
 \Gamma=AB=Q^2+Q+1,\qquad
 K=\F_{t^6},\qquad E=\F_{t^2}.
\]
For the actual normalized root \(w=w_k\), set
\[
 H=\Norm_{K/E}(w),\qquad R=w^{Q-1}.
\]
Then
\[
 R\in\ker\Norm_{K/E}=\mu_\Gamma,\qquad
 \gcd(A,B)=1,                                       \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_1\)}
\]
and its two coprime projections are exactly
\[
 \boxed{R^A=P_k=M_k^{t+1},}                         \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_2\)}
\]
\[
 \boxed{R^B=Z_{D,k}^{\,t-1}
       =g_k^{(Q-2)(t-1)}.}                          \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_3\)}
\]
Thus \(P_k\) is literally the minus-torus coordinate of the relative
coboundary \(R\); the complementary plus coordinate is the
\(g_k\)-coboundary.

For every \(\ell^a\Vert\Psi_k\),
\[
 v_\ell(\ord w)=v_\ell(\ord R)=v_\ell(\ord P_k),\qquad
 \ell\nmid\ord(R^B),                                \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_4\)}
\]
and the exact full-primary criterion is
\[
 v_\ell(\ord P_k)=a
 \iff P_k^{B/\ell}\ne1
 \iff w\notin(K^\times)^\ell.                       \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_5\)}
\]
There is no squarefreeness assumption here.

The lower norm has the exact compatibility
\[
 \boxed{P_k^{B/3}=H^{(Q-1)/3}.}                     \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_6\)}
\]
By \((\mathsf D_9)\), this common value is \(\omega^2\) for odd \(k\)
and one for even \(k\).  Hence the lower norm determines precisely
the already-known \(3\)-component, and no current \(\ell\)-component.

More generally, the map
\[
 \Theta:K^\times\longrightarrow E^\times\times\mu_\Gamma,
 \qquad x\longmapsto
 \bigl(\Norm_{K/E}(x),x^{Q-1}\bigr)
                                                              \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_7\)}
\]
has kernel \(\mu_3\), and its image is exactly
\[
 \left\{(H_0,R_0):
 H_0^{(Q-1)/3}=R_0^{\Gamma/3}\right\}.              \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_8\)}
\]
Consequently, after fixing \(H\) and \(R^B\), multiplication of \(R\)
by an arbitrary current Sylow element of \(\mu_B\) preserves both
data while \(R^A\) ranges freely through that current coordinate.
These compatible variations need not preserve the selected
Artin--Schreier equation; that equation is the remaining coupling.

The coupling itself is lossless but supplies no order evaluation.
If \(x^{t^3}=x+1\), put \(Z=x^2+x\), and form \(H,R\) from \(x\) as
above.  Then
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{t^3}/\F_t}(Z)=H^{t+1},\qquad
 Z^{t-1}=R^B.                                       \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_9\)}
\]
The map
\[
 \Xi:\F_{t^3}^\times\longrightarrow\F_t^\times\times\mu_A,
 \qquad
 z\longmapsto\bigl(\Norm_{\F_{t^3}/\F_t}(z),z^{t-1}\bigr)
                                                              \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_{10}\)}
\]
is a bijection.  Thus \(H^{t+1}\) and \(R^B\) reconstruct a unique
  \(Z\).  Such a reconstructed scalar comes from an Artin--Schreier
 pair \(x,x+1\in K\setminus\F_{t^3}\) satisfying
 \(x^{t^3}=x+1\) exactly when
 \[
  \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{t^3}/\F_2}(Z)=1.
 \]
For the actual selected data, the two roots \(w,w+1\) replace
\(P_k\) only by \(P_k^{-1}\), leaving its order unchanged.

This reconstruction has an exact one-variable order form.  With
\[
 M=\frac{w+1}{w},\qquad Z=w^2+w,\qquad P=M^{t+1},
\]
one has
\[
 M+M^{-1}=Z^{-1},\qquad
 \boxed{P+P^{-1}=\mathcal D_{t+1}(Z^{-1},1).}        \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_{11}\)}
\]
Consequently, for every \(\ell^a\Vert\Psi_k\),
\[
 \boxed{
 P^{B/\ell}=1
 \iff
 \mathcal D_{(t^3+1)/\ell}(Z^{-1},1)=0.}           \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_{12}\)}
\]
Thus the selected trace-one scalar determines the inverse pair
\(\{P,P^{-1}\}\) and rewrites failure as one Dickson critical value;
it does not evaluate that value.

The aggregate reconstruction is rigid on the Artin--Schreier locus.  For any
root \(x^{t^3}=x+1\), put
\[
 Z_x=x^2+x,\qquad H_x=\Norm_{K/E}(x),\qquad
 C_x=(x^{Q-1})^B,qquad
 P_x=\left(\frac{x+1}{x}\right)^{t+1}.
\]
If two such roots \(x,y\) satisfy
\[
 H_x^{t+1}=H_y^{t+1},\qquad C_x=C_y,
\]
then
\[
 y\in\{x,x+1\},\qquad P_y\in\{P_x,P_x^{-1}\}.      \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_{13}\)}
\]
For the actual selected data, fixing the exact norm \(H\) sharpens this to
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c}
 k=2&\{w,w+1\}\text{ have the same }(H,C_x),\\
 k\geq3&w\text{ is the unique Artin--Schreier root with the fixed }(H,C_x).
 \end{array}                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_{14}\)}
\]
This is a rigidity theorem, not a nonprimitive countermodel: once the exact
Artin--Schreier coupling is imposed, the projection data reconstruct the
selected minus coordinate instead of bounding its order independently.

Finally the selected \(C\)-dependence of the plus projection is the
explicit reciprocal-quartic identity
\[
 \eta_k=\gamma_k^{t-1}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \boxed{
 \bigl(g_k^{t-1}\bigr)^2
 =\frac{\eta_k^4+\eta_k^3+1}
        {\eta_k^4+\eta_k+1}.}                       \tag{\(\mathsf{DCP}_{15}\)}
\]
 The denominator in the reciprocal-quartic identity is nonzero.  Indeed
 \(X^4+X+1\) is irreducible over \(\F_2\), while
 \(\eta_k\in\F_{2^{3^k}}\) and \(\gcd(4,3^k)=1\).  Therefore any
 proof of \(C_k\Rightarrow D'_k\) that proceeds through these
 projections would have to control this special rational image and the
 selected Artin--Schreier coupling, then still evaluate the disjoint
 minus coordinate \(P_k\); primitive order does not transfer directly.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The identities
\[
 AB=Q^2+Q+1,\qquad \gcd(A,B)=1
\]
are direct; the gcd uses that \(t\equiv-1\pmod3\).  Since
\(\Norm_{K/E}(R)=R^{1+Q+Q^2}=1\), one has \(R\in\mu_\Gamma\).
Also
\[
 (Q-1)A=(t^3-1)(t+1),
\]
and \(w^{t^3-1}=M_k\), proving \((\mathsf{DCP}_2)\).
Using \(w^{t^3}=w+1\), one obtains
\[
 \begin{split}
 R^B
 &=w^{(Q-1)(Q-t+1)}
   =\frac{w^{t^4}w^t}{w^{t^3}w}\\
 &=\frac{(w^t+1)w^t}{(w+1)w}
   =(w^2+w)^{t-1}=Z_{D,k}^{t-1},
 \end{split}
\]
and \((\mathsf{CD}_5)\) gives the last expression in
\((\mathsf{DCP}_3)\).

A current \(\ell\) divides \(B/3\) and is coprime to
\((Q-1)A\).  Raising to \(Q-1\) and then to \(A\) therefore preserves
its full primary order, while \(R^B\) kills it.  The cyclic Euler
criterion proves \((\mathsf{DCP}_4)\)--\((\mathsf{DCP}_5)\).
Furthermore
\[
 P_k^{B/3}=R^{\Gamma/3}
 =w^{(Q-1)\Gamma/3}=H^{(Q-1)/3},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{DCP}_6)\).

The kernel of \(\Theta\) consists of the elements of \(E^\times\)
whose cube is one, hence is \(\mu_3\).  Every image pair satisfies
the compatibility in \((\mathsf{DCP}_8)\).  Conversely, both the image
and the compatible-pair set have
\[
 \frac{(Q-1)\Gamma}{3}
\]
elements: the latter condition is the equality of two surjective
maps to \(\mu_3\).  This proves \((\mathsf{DCP}_8)\).  A current Sylow
element \(c\in\mu_B\) satisfies \(c^B=c^{\Gamma/3}=1\), while
\(c\mapsto c^A\) is an automorphism of its Sylow group, proving the
variation assertion.

For an Artin--Schreier root, \(Z=x^{t^3+1}\).  Expanding exponents gives
\[
 (t^3+1)(t^2+t+1)
 =(t+1)(t^4+t^2+1),
\]
which is the norm identity in \((\mathsf{DCP}_9)\); the second is the
same exponent calculation used in \((\mathsf{DCP}_3)\).
Since
\[
 |\F_{t^3}^\times|=(t-1)A,\qquad \gcd(t-1,A)=1,
\]
\(\Xi\) is injective and the two sides have the same cardinality, so
it is bijective.  The characteristic-two quadratic criterion gives
the stated trace-one boundary and the two conjugate roots.  When the
trace is zero the quadratic instead has two roots in \(\F_{t^3}\), so
they do not satisfy the required nontrivial Frobenius translation.

The first identity in \((\mathsf{DCP}_{11})\) is a direct calculation, and
the Dickson identity
\(\mathcal D_n(u+u^{-1},1)=u^n+u^{-n}\) gives the second.  Since \(P\)
has odd order,
\[
 \mathcal D_{B/\ell}(P+P^{-1},1)=0
 \iff P^{B/\ell}=1.
\]
Dickson composition and \((t+1)B=t^3+1\) prove
\((\mathsf{DCP}_{12})\).

If \(x,y\) satisfy the hypotheses of \((\mathsf{DCP}_{13})\), then
\((\mathsf{DCP}_9)\) and injectivity of \(\Xi\) give \(Z_x=Z_y\).
Thus \((x+y)^2+(x+y)=0\), so \(y=x\) or \(x+1\), and the displayed
claim about \(P_y\) follows by inversion.  Moreover
\[
 \Norm_{K/E}(w+1)=\Norm_{K/E}(w^{t^3})=H^t.
\]
For the actual norm, \((\mathsf{TN}_1)\) gives, with
\(\eta=\zeta_k^3\),
\[
 H=\frac{\eta^2}{\eta^2+\omega\eta+1},\qquad
 H^t=\frac1{\eta^2+\omega^2\eta+1}.
\]
Their equality is equivalent to
\[
 (\eta^3+\omega)(\eta+\omega^2)
 =\eta^4+\omega^2\eta^3+\omega\eta+1=0.
\]
Since \(\eta\) has order \(3^k\), for \(k\geq2\) this occurs exactly
when \(k=2\), proving \((\mathsf{DCP}_{14})\).  The statement concerns
coordinates that actually arise from Artin--Schreier roots; an arbitrary
ambient pair in \((\mathsf{DCP}_8)\) need not do so.

For the reciprocal-quartic identity, put \(y=\zeta_k^2\).  Directly from the
selected period formulas,
\[
 \eta_k=\frac{\omega+\omega^2y}{1+y},\qquad
 y=\frac{\eta_k+\omega}{\eta_k+\omega^2},\qquad
 g_k^2=\frac{y^5+1}{y^2(y+1)},\qquad
 y^t=\omega^2y^{-1}.
\]
Substitution, followed by \(1+\omega+\omega^2=0\), gives the displayed
reciprocal-quartic quotient.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the actual exceptional terminal resolvent]
\label{prop:dk-terminal-resolvent}
Retain \(k\geq2\), and write
\[
 t=2^{3^{k-1}},\qquad Q=t^2,\qquad q=t^3,\qquad
 a=\gamma_{k-1},\qquad C=a^2+a+1.
\]
Put \(r_i=\gamma_k^{t^i}\), with indices modulo three, and define the
oriented cyclic resolvents
\[
 \mathcal K=\sum_i r_i^2r_{i+1},\qquad
 \mathcal K'=\sum_i r_ir_{i+1}^2,\qquad
 \chi=\mathcal K/a.
\]
Then
\[
 \mathcal K+\mathcal K'=a,\qquad
 \mathcal K\mathcal K'=a^2+1,\qquad
 \boxed{\chi^2+\chi=1+a^{-2}.}                      \tag{\(\mathsf{DTR}_1\)}
\]
Reversing cyclic orientation sends \(\chi\) to \(\chi+1\).

The actual selected scalar
\[
 Y=Z_{D,k}^{-1}=g_k^{\,2-Q}\in\F_q
\]
has the irreducible monic polynomial over \(\F_t\)
\[
 \boxed{
 m_\chi(T)=T^3+
 \frac{a^2\chi+a^2+1}{C}T^2+
 \frac{a^2(a+1)\chi+a^3+a+1}{C}T+C.}               \tag{\(\mathsf{DTR}_2\)}
\]
Equivalently, its denominator-free equation is
\[
 CT^3+(a\mathcal K+a^2+1)T^2
 +(a(a+1)\mathcal K+a^3+a+1)T+C^2=0.               \tag{\(\mathsf{DTR}_3\)}
\]
For every current prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), with
\(n=(q+1)/\ell\),
\[
 \boxed{
 \text{failure at }\ell
 \iff m_\chi(T)\mid\mathcal D_n(T,1).}             \tag{\(\mathsf{DTR}_4\)}
\]

This divisor has no independent norm shadow.  If
\(V=\mathcal D_n(Y,1)\), then
\[
 \boxed{
 \operatorname{Res}_T(m_\chi,\mathcal D_n)
 =\Norm_{\F_q/\F_t}(V)
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_q/\F_t}(V)^2,}             \tag{\(\mathsf{DTR}_5\)}
\]
and these three quantities vanish exactly under failure.

Finally, for \(Z=Y^{-1}\), orientation reversal changes its relative trace by
\[
 \frac{a^2(a+1)}{C^2}
 =\left(\frac{a+1}{C}\right)^2+\frac{a+1}{C},       \tag{\(\mathsf{DTR}_6\)}
\]
and leaves its relative norm \(C^{-1}\) fixed.  Therefore absolute trace and
lower norm cannot distinguish the two orientations.  The actual
\(t\)-Frobenius does select one of them; the comparison proves blindness of
these scalar invariants, not ambiguity of the Conway datum.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The \(r_i\) are the roots of \(X^3+X+a\), so their elementary symmetric
functions are \(e_1=0,e_2=1,e_3=a\).  The cyclic-resolvent identities give
\[
 \mathcal K+\mathcal K'=e_1e_2+e_3=a,\qquad
 \mathcal K\mathcal K'=e_1^3e_3+e_2^3+e_3^2=1+a^2,
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{DTR}_1)\).

Put \(x_i=r_i^2+r_i+1\).  Direct symmetric reduction gives
\[
 e_1(x)=1,\qquad e_2(x)=a+1,\qquad e_3(x)=C,\qquad
 \mathcal K_x:=\sum_i x_i^2x_{i+1}=a\mathcal K+C.
\]
The three conjugates of \(Y\) are \(Y_i=x_i^2/x_{i-1}\).  Reducing their
elementary symmetric functions over the common denominator
\(C=x_0x_1x_2\) gives
\[
 \sum_iY_i=\frac{a\mathcal K+a^2+1}{C},\qquad
 \sum_{i<j}Y_iY_j
 =\frac{a(a+1)\mathcal K+a^3+a+1}{C},\qquad
 \prod_iY_i=C.
\]
This proves \((\mathsf{DTR}_2)\)--\((\mathsf{DTR}_3)\).
Here \(a,C\ne0\), and \(\gcd(Q-2,q-1)=1\).  Exponentiation by \(2-Q\)
therefore recovers \(g_k\) from \(Y\), so \(Y\) has relative degree three and
the polynomial is irreducible.  Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-coprime-projections},
specifically \((\mathsf{DCP}_{12})\), and the minimal-polynomial criterion
prove \((\mathsf{DTR}_4)\).

Let \(M=M_k\) and \(\xi=M^n\in\mu_\ell\).  Since
\(Y=M+M^{-1}\), Dickson's identity gives \(V=\xi+\xi^{-1}\).
Moreover \(\ord_\ell(t)=6\), hence \(t^2-t+1\equiv0\pmod\ell\).  With
\(x=\xi\) and \(y=\xi^t\), direct multiplication of the three relative
conjugates gives
\[
 \Norm(V)=\operatorname{Tr}(V)^2,\qquad
 xy\operatorname{Tr}(V)=(x+y)(x+1)(y+1).
\]
The second expression vanishes exactly when \(\xi=1\); thus both sides of
\((\mathsf{DTR}_5)\) vanish exactly under failure.  The first equality in
\((\mathsf{DTR}_5)\) is the root-product formula for the irreducible
\(m_\chi\).  Finally take the reciprocal of \((\mathsf{DTR}_2)\) and replace
\(\chi\) by \(\chi+1\).  The relative norm stays \(C^{-1}\), while the trace
changes by the first term in \((\mathsf{DTR}_6)\); the displayed
Artin--Schreier identity proves equality of the absolute traces.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[translated-binomial form of $D'_k$]
\label{prop:dk-translated-binomial}
Retain \(k\geq2\), \(h=3^k\), and choose
\(u\in\F_{16}\) with \(u^2+u=\omega\).  Put

\[
 L=\F_{16^h}=\F_{2^{4h}},\qquad \beta=\zeta+u.
\]

Then the minimal polynomial of \(\beta\) over \(\F_{16}\) is the
irreducible degree-\(h\) polynomial

\[
 B_k(Y)=(Y+u)^h+\omega.
\]

For every prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item the full \(\ell\)-primary part occurs in \(\ord(M_k)\);
\item \(\beta\notin(L^\times)^\ell\);
\item \(B_k(X^\ell)=(X^\ell+u)^h+\omega\) is irreducible over
      \(\F_{16}\).
\end{enumerate}
More precisely, \(B_k(X^\ell)\) is either irreducible of degree
\(h\ell\), or is a product of exactly \(\ell\) irreducibles of degree
\(h\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since

\[
 \ord_{3h}(16)
 =\frac{\ord_{3h}(2)}{\gcd(\ord_{3h}(2),4)}=h,
\]

the element \(\zeta\) has degree \(h\) over \(\F_{16}\).  As
\(\zeta^h=\omega\), its minimal polynomial there is
\(X^h+\omega\); translation by \(u\) gives \(B_k\).

Let \(K=\F_{2^{2h}}\).  The corrected norm is

\[
 N=\Norm_{L/K}(\beta)=\beta^{2^{2h}+1}.
\]

Because \(2^{2h}\equiv1\pmod\ell\), this norm induces multiplication
by two on \(L^\times/(L^\times)^\ell\).  Hence it first gives

\[
 \beta\in(L^\times)^\ell
 \iff N\in(L^\times)^\ell.
\]

The natural map

\[
 K^\times/(K^\times)^\ell
 \longrightarrow L^\times/(L^\times)^\ell
\]

is injective: restriction followed by the degree-two norm is
multiplication by two, which is invertible modulo the odd prime
\(\ell\).  Since \(N\in K\), this sharpens the equivalence to

\[
 \beta\in(L^\times)^\ell
 \iff N\in(K^\times)^\ell,
\]

and Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-current-level} identifies the negation
with the full \(\ell\)-primary assertion.  Kummer theory and Capelli's
lemma give the irreducibility equivalence.

If \(\beta=\alpha^\ell\) with \(\alpha\in L\), every root of
\(B_k(X^\ell)\) lies in \(L\), because \(\mu_\ell\subset L\).  Its
\(\ell\)-th power is a conjugate of \(\beta\), of degree \(h\) over
\(\F_{16}\); the root therefore has degree both at least and at most
\(h\).  All irreducible factors consequently have degree \(h\), and
there are exactly \(\ell\) of them.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[short translated cyclotomic period]
\label{cor:dk-short-period}
Let \(\chi:L^\times\to\mu_\ell\) have exact order
\(\ell\), realize its values as complex roots of unity, and write
\(\chi(\beta)=\xi_\ell^r\).  Then

\[
 \mathcal J_{k,\ell}
 :=\sum_{\theta^h=\omega}\chi(\theta+u)
 =\sum_{j=0}^{h-1}\xi_\ell^{\,r16^j},
\]

and

\[
 \beta\in(L^\times)^\ell
 \iff \mathcal J_{k,\ell}=h.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The roots of \(X^h+\omega\) are \(\zeta^{16^j}\), \(0\leq j<h\),
and \(u\in\F_{16}\).  Thus

\[
 \chi(\zeta^{16^j}+u)
 =\chi((\zeta+u)^{16^j})
 =\xi_\ell^{\,r16^j}.
\]

If \(r=0\), every summand is one.  Conversely, equality with the
positive real number \(h\) forces equality in the complex triangle
inequality and hence forces every summand, in particular the first,
to be one.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[full \(\F_{16}\)-line saturation]
\label{prop:dk-line-saturation}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-translated-binomial}, put
\(s=3^{k-1}\), and choose \(u\) so that it has order fifteen and
\(\omega=u^5\).  For a prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), write

\[
 \mathcal C=L^\times/(L^\times)^\ell,
 \qquad v(c)=[\zeta+c]\quad(c\in\F_{16}),
 \qquad A=2^s\pmod\ell,
\]

using additive notation for \(\mathcal C\).  Then \(A\) has order six,
\(A^2-A+1=0\), and \(A^3=-1\).  The four base-field classes vanish,

\[
 v(c)=0\qquad(c\in\F_4),                              \tag{\(\mathsf{L}_{16,1}\)}
\]

while for \(c\in\F_{16}^\times\),

\[
 v(\omega c^{-2^s})=A v(c),\qquad
 v(c^{-2^h})=-v(c).                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{L}_{16,2}\)}
\]

For the twelve exponents not divisible by five one has

\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
 j & \{m:v(u^m)=A^jv(u)\}\\ \hline
 0&\{1,4\}\\
 1&\{3,12\}\\
 2&\{11,14\}\\
 3&\{7,13\}\\
 4&\{6,9\}\\
 5&\{2,8\}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{\(\mathsf{L}_{16,3}\)}
\]

Consequently

\[
 \zeta+u\in(L^\times)^\ell
 \iff \zeta+c\in(L^\times)^\ell
      \quad\hbox{for every }c\in\F_{16}.              \tag{\(\mathsf{L}_{16,4}\)}
\]

If \(D'_k\) holds for \(\ell\), none of the twelve translates with
\(c\notin\F_4\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.  Equivalently, for an
exact-order-\(\ell\) complex character \(\chi\) of \(L^\times\) and
\(t=\chi(\zeta+u)\),

\[
 \sum_{c\in\F_{16}}\chi(\zeta+c)
 =4+2\sum_{j=0}^5t^{A^j},                              \tag{\(\mathsf{L}_{16}\)}
\]

and failure of \(D'_k\) is exactly the extremal value sixteen.
Thus Frobenius, conjugation, and all \(\F_4\)-translates generate only
scalar multiples of the one unresolved Kummer class \(v(u)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
One has \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h=6s\), proving the assertions about \(A\).
For \(c=0\), the element \(\zeta\) has \(\ell\)-coprime order \(3h\).
For \(c\in\{1,\omega,\omega^2\}\),
Propositions~\ref{prop:translates} and~\ref{prop:exception} give order
dividing \(3h(2^h-1)\), again coprime to \(\ell\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{L}_{16,1})\); constants in \(\F_{16}^\times\) and
\(\zeta\) also have zero class.

The congruence \(2^s\equiv h-1\pmod{3h}\) gives
\(\zeta^{2^s}=\omega\zeta^{-1}\).  Discarding scalar factors with
zero class,

\[
 A v(c)=[(\zeta+c)^{2^s}]
 =[\omega\zeta^{-1}+c^{2^s}]
 =[\zeta+\omega c^{-2^s}]
 =v(\omega c^{-2^s}).
\]

Likewise \(2^h\equiv-1\pmod{3h}\) and modulo \(\ell\), so

\[
 -v(c)=[(\zeta+c)^{2^h}]
 =[\zeta^{-1}+c^{2^h}]
 =[\zeta+c^{-2^h}].
\]

On exponents modulo fifteen the two transformations are

\[
 R(m)=5-(2^s\bmod15)m,\qquad
 S(m)=-(2^h\bmod15)m.
\]

For odd \(k\) they are \((5-2m,7m)\), and for even \(k\) they are
\((5-8m,13m)\).  Iterating either pair on the twelve residues outside
\(5\mathbb Z/15\mathbb Z\) gives the table
\((\mathsf{L}_{16,3})\).  Since every \(A^j\) is nonzero modulo
\(\ell\), all twelve classes vanish exactly when \(v(u)\) vanishes,
proving \((\mathsf{L}_{16,4})\) and its nonvanishing counterpart.

Applying \(\chi\) to the table gives each \(t^{A^j}\) twice, while
the four \(\F_4\)-terms are one.  If \(t=1\), the sum is sixteen;
conversely equality with the positive real number sixteen forces every
unit-modulus summand to be one by equality in the triangle inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exceptional gap and Conway-block product blindness]
\label{prop:dk-gap}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-line-saturation}, so \(k\geq2\),
and put \(K=\mathbb F_{2^{2h}}\).  For every
prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\),
\[
 m_\ell=4\qquad\hbox{or}\qquad m_\ell\geq16.             \tag{\(\mathsf{G}_{16}\)}
\]
Thus a failure of \(D'_k\) cannot produce an excess in the interval
\(5,\ldots,15\).

There is also no contradiction obtained by multiplying a complete
finite-Conway-subfield block.  More precisely, for \(a\geq1\), put
\[
 C_a=\mathbb F_{2^{2^a}},\qquad Q_a=|C_a|=2^{2^a},
 \qquad L_a=\mathbb F_{2^{\,2^a h}}=K C_a .
\]
Then, in \(L_a\),
\[
 \prod_{c\in C_a}(\zeta+c)
   =\zeta^{Q_a}+\zeta
   =\zeta\bigl(1+\zeta^{Q_a-1}\bigr)
   \in (L_a^\times)^\ell .                            \tag{\(\mathsf{G}_{C_a}\)}
\]
Consequently even an extension of the affine-line saturation from
\(\mathbb F_{16}\) to any complete finite Conway subfield would have a
product identity which is automatically invisible in the unresolved
\(\ell\)-Kummer quotient.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose the element \(u\) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-line-saturation} to be the finite nimber \(4\).
If \(m_\ell\ne4\), then the lower bound
\(m_\ell\geq4\) and the exact power criterion say that
\(\zeta+4\) is an \(\ell\)-th power in
\(L=\mathbb F_{2^{4h}}\).  Equation
\((\mathsf L_{16,4})\) then makes every \(\zeta+c\),
\(c\in\mathbb F_{16}\), an \(\ell\)-th power.  The finite nimbers
\(0,1,\ldots,15\) are exactly the elements of \(\mathbb F_{16}\), so
none of these sixteen translates can be the excess.  This proves
\((\mathsf G_{16})\).

For the second assertion, the roots of \(X^{Q_a}+X\) are precisely the
elements of \(C_a\), which gives the displayed product formula.  Since
\(2^a\) is even, lifting the exponent gives
\[
 v_3(Q_a-1)=v_3(2^{2^a}-1)=1.
\]
The element \(\zeta\) has order \(3h=3^{k+1}\), so
\(\zeta^{Q_a-1}\) has exact order \(h\) and lies in
\[
 \mathbb F_{2^{\operatorname{ord}_h(2)}}
   =\mathbb F_{2^{2\cdot3^{k-1}}}.
\]
This lower field embeds in \(K\), hence in every \(L_a\).
On the other hand \(\operatorname{ord}_\ell(2)=2h\).  Hence \(\ell\)
divides neither \(3h\) nor the multiplicative order of that lower
field.  It follows that both \(\zeta\) and
\(1+\zeta^{Q_a-1}\) are \(\ell\)-th powers in \(L_a\), proving
\((\mathsf G_{C_a})\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[constant-extension line criterion and an explicit
exceptional bound]
\label{prop:dk-constant-line}
Retain \(k\geq2\), \(h=3^k\),
\[
 K=\F_{4^h}=\F_4(\zeta),
 \qquad \ell\mid\Psi_k,
\]
and let \(\chi:K^\times\to\mu_\ell\) have exact order \(\ell\).
For \(a\geq1\), put
\[
 t=2^{a-1},\qquad
 C_a=\F_{2^{2^a}}=\F_{4^t},\qquad
 L_a=KC_a=\F_{4^{ht}},
\]
and let
\[
 \chi_a=\chi\circ\Norm_{L_a/K},\qquad
 S_{a,k,\ell}=\sum_{c\in C_a}\chi_a(\zeta+c).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CE}_1\)}
\]
Then
\[
 \zeta+C_a\subset (L_a^\times)^\ell
 \iff S_{a,k,\ell}=4^t.                              \tag{\(\mathsf{CE}_2\)}
\]
More precisely, attach to \(\chi\) the primitive even Dirichlet
character modulo \(X^h+\omega\) over \(\F_4[X]\), and write its
\(L\)-polynomial as
\[
 L_\chi(T)
  =(1-T)\prod_{j=1}^{h-2}(1-\alpha_jT),
 \qquad |\alpha_j|=2.
\]
Then constant-field extension gives the exact selected moment
\[
 S_{a,k,\ell}
   =-\left(1+\sum_{j=1}^{h-2}\alpha_j^t\right),
 \qquad
 |S_{a,k,\ell}|\leq1+(h-2)2^t.                       \tag{\(\mathsf{CE}_3\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 2^t\geq h-1
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 m_\ell\leq4^t-1.                                    \tag{\(\mathsf{CE}_4\)}
\]
If \(t\) is the least power of two at least
\(\lceil\log_2(h-1)\rceil\), then
\[
 m_\ell<4^t<16h^4.                                   \tag{\(\mathsf{CE}_5\)}
\]
Thus this gives a direct polynomial upper bound on the exceptional
column, but not an absolute one.  For any fixed \(a\), the Riemann--Weil
bound in \((\mathsf{CE}_3)\) is compatible with the extremal value
\(S_{a,k,\ell}=4^t\) for all sufficiently large \(h\).  A fixed-window
proof by this route must therefore prove the selected nonextremality
\[
 S_{a,k,\ell}\ne4^t
\]
uniformly in \(k\) and \(\ell\).  At \(a=2\), this is exactly the
full-\(\F_{16}\)-line formulation of \(D'_k\); larger fixed \(a\) give
genuinely weaker selected least-nonresidue assertions rather than a
consequence of the four maximal-order arms.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since \(h\) is odd and \(t\) is a power of two, the fields \(K\) and
\(C_a\) are linearly disjoint over \(\F_4\), and
\([L_a:K]=t\).  Norm followed by inclusion on the cyclic
\(\ell\)-Kummer quotients is multiplication by \(t\).  As
\(\ell\nmid t\), the norm induces an isomorphism
\[
 L_a^\times/(L_a^\times)^\ell
   \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
 K^\times/(K^\times)^\ell.                           \tag{\(\mathsf{CE}_6\)}
\]
In particular \(\chi_a\) has exact order \(\ell\), its kernel is
\((L_a^\times)^\ell\), and no term \(\zeta+c\) vanishes because
\(\zeta\) has odd degree \(h>1\) over \(\F_4\), whereas every element
of \(C_a\) has degree dividing \(t\).  Equation
\((\mathsf{CE}_2)\) now follows from equality in the complex triangle
inequality: a sum of \(4^t\) roots of unity equals the positive real
number \(4^t\) precisely when every summand is one.

The polynomial \(X^h+\omega\) remains irreducible after extension of
constants to \(C_a\), again because \(\gcd(h,t)=1\).  Since
\(\ell\ne3\), the order-\(\ell\) character is trivial on
\(\F_4^\times\), so the attached Dirichlet character is even.
The character \(\chi_a\) is exactly its constant-field norm lift.
If the reciprocal roots of \(L_\chi\) are
\(1,\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_{h-2}\), constant extension raises them
to their \(t\)-th powers.  The degree-one coefficient of the extended
\(L\)-polynomial is on the one hand
\[
 \sum_{c\in C_a}\chi_a(\zeta+c)=S_{a,k,\ell},
\]
and on the other hand the negative of the sum of those reciprocal
roots.  This proves the equality in \((\mathsf{CE}_3)\); the
function-field Riemann hypothesis gives \(|\alpha_j|=2\), proving its
inequality.  Notice that \(1\) is the reciprocal root of the
trivial even factor \(1-T\), so neither the sign nor the additive
constant is being suppressed.

If \(2^t\geq h-1\), then
\[
 1+(h-2)2^t
 \leq1+(2^t-1)2^t
 =4^t-2^t+1
 <4^t.
\]
Equations \((\mathsf{CE}_2)\)--\((\mathsf{CE}_3)\) therefore supply
some \(c\in C_a\) for which \(\zeta+c\) is not an \(\ell\)-th power
in \(L_a\), and hence is not one in its defining subfield.  The finite
nimbers \(0,1,\ldots,4^t-1\) are exactly the elements of \(C_a\),
proving \((\mathsf{CE}_4)\).

Finally put \(R=\lceil\log_2(h-1)\rceil\).  The least power of two
\(t\geq R\) satisfies
\[
 t<2R<2(\log_2 h+1).
\]
Hence
\[
 4^t<4^{2(\log_2 h+1)}=16h^4,
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{CE}_5)\).  For fixed \(t\), the right side of
the bound in \((\mathsf{CE}_3)\) eventually exceeds \(4^t\), proving
the stated limitation.  The case \(a=2\) has \(t=2\) and
\(C_a=\F_{16}\), so Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-line-saturation} gives
the final identification with \(D'_k\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[composite-conductor cyclotomic-unit form of \(D'_k\)]
\label{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-gap}, put \(N_0=15h\), and
choose \(\theta\in L^\times\) so that
\[
 \theta^5=\zeta,\qquad \theta^h=u.
\]
Then \(\theta\) has exact order \(N_0\), and
\[
 \rho:=\frac{\zeta}{u}=\theta^{5-h}
\]
also has exact order \(N_0\).  If
\(\widetilde\rho\) is a primitive complex \(N_0\)-th root of unity,
\[
 \varepsilon=1-\widetilde\rho
 \in\mathbb Q(\mu_{N_0})^\times
\]
is a global cyclotomic unit.  There is a prime
\(\mathfrak p\mid2\) whose residue field is \(L=\mathbb F_{2^{4h}}\)
and for which
\[
 \overline\varepsilon=1+\rho,\qquad
 \zeta+u=u(1+\rho).
\]
For every prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\),
\[
 D'_k\text{ holds at }\ell
 \iff
 \left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\mathfrak p}\right)_\ell\ne1.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CU}_{15h}\)}
\]
Equivalently, the chosen prime over two has nontrivial Frobenius in the
Kummer extension obtained by adjoining an \(\ell\)-th root of
\(\varepsilon\) after adjoining \(\mu_\ell\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The elements \(\zeta\) and \(u\) have orders \(3h\) and \(15\), and
their \(h\)-th and fifth powers agree:
\(\zeta^h=u^5=\omega\).  The compatible pair therefore lifts in the
cyclic group of order \(\operatorname{lcm}(3h,15)=15h\) to the stated
\(\theta\).  Its two displayed images have their full orders, so
\(\theta\) has order \(15h\).  Moreover
\[
 \gcd(5-h,15h)=1
\]
because \(h\) is a power of three and is not divisible by five; hence
\(\rho\) is primitive of the same order.

The conductor \(15h\) has two distinct prime divisors, so
\(\Phi_{15h}(1)=1\), which makes \(1-\widetilde\rho\) a unit.  Also
\[
 \operatorname{ord}_{15h}(2)
 =\operatorname{lcm}\bigl(\operatorname{ord}_{3h}(2),
                           \operatorname{ord}_5(2)\bigr)
 =\operatorname{lcm}(2h,4)=4h.
\]
Thus a prime over two has residue field \(L\), and it may be chosen to
send \(\widetilde\rho\) to \(\rho\).  Reduction gives the two displayed
identities.  Finally \(\ell\nmid15\), so \(u\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-translated-binomial} identifies \(D'_k\) at
\(\ell\) with \(\zeta+u\) being a non-\(\ell\)-th-power; the standard
power-residue/Frobenius criterion now gives
\((\mathsf{CU}_{15h})\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exceptional failure as simultaneous Jacobi flatness]
\label{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness}
Let \(k\geq2\), put
\[
 h=3^k,\qquad M=15h,\qquad Q=2^{4h},
\]
and let the prime \(\ell\) divide \(\Psi_k\).  Choose the reduction
\(z\in\mathbb F_Q^\times\) of the selected primitive \(M\)-th root in
Corollary~\ref{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit}, choose a generator \(g\) with
\(g^{(Q-1)/M}=z\), and form the mixed Jacobi sums
\[
 J_j=\sum_{x\in\mathbb F_Q}
 \chi_M(x)^j\chi_\ell(1-x)
 \qquad(1\leq j<M)
\]
of Proposition~\ref{prop:mixed-jacobi-phase}.  At a chosen
\(\ell\)-adic place put \(\pi=\zeta_\ell-1\).  Then failure of
\(D'_k\) at \(\ell\) is equivalent to the simultaneous first-order
flatness
\[
 J_j\equiv-1\pmod{\pi^2}
 \qquad(1\leq j<M).                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}\)}
\]
Thus mixed Jacobi sums see the complete exceptional Kummer vector, but
the missing theorem is exactly that this vector is not identically
zero; Stickelberger ideal factorization alone does not evaluate its
first local phase.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write the unique coprime-order decomposition
\[
 z=ab,\qquad \ord(a)=3h,\qquad \ord(b)=5,
\]
and put \(q=2^h\), \(L=\mathbb F_{q^4}=\mathbb F_Q\), and
\(K_0=\mathbb F_{q^2}\).  By
Corollary~\ref{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit}, failure at \(\ell\) says that
\(1+ab\) is an \(\ell\)-th power in \(L\).  The norm
\(L^\times\to K_0^\times\) acts on the one-dimensional
\(\ell\)-Kummer quotient by
\[
 q^2+1\equiv2\pmod\ell,
\]
because \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\).  It is therefore an isomorphism on that
quotient, and
\[
 U=(1+ab)(1+ab^{-1})
\]
is an \(\ell\)-th power in \(K_0\).

Since \(a^q=a^{-1}\),
\[
 (1+a)^{q-1}=a^{-1},
 \qquad
 (1+a^5)^{q-1}=a^{-5}.
\]
Here \(q-1\equiv-2\pmod\ell\), while \(a\) has order prime to
\(\ell\).  Hence both \(1+a\) and \(1+a^5\) are \(\ell\)-th powers.
If
\[
 U'=(1+ab^2)(1+ab^{-2}),
\]
then
\[
 UU'=\Phi_5(a)=\frac{1+a^5}{1+a}
\]
is an \(\ell\)-th power.  Thus \(U'\) is an \(\ell\)-th power, and the same norm
isomorphism implies that \(1+ab^2\) is an \(\ell\)-th power in \(L\).

Now
\[
 \ord_M(2)=4h,\qquad \varphi(M)=8h.
\]
The primitive \(M\)-th roots consequently form two Frobenius orbits,
represented by \(ab\) and \(ab^2\).  These representatives are not in
the same orbit: a power \(2^j\) fixing the \(3h\)-component has
\(j\equiv0\pmod{2h}\), whereas no such exponent sends \(b\) to
\(b^2\).  Hence \(1+v\) is an \(\ell\)-th power for every primitive
\(M\)-th root \(v\).

It remains to treat proper-order powers of \(z\).  If \(d\mid M\) is
proper, the divisibility
\(2h\mid\ord_d(2)\) can occur only for \(d=3h\).  For an element
\(v\) of that order, \(v^q=v^{-1}\), and
\[
 (1+v)^{q-1}=v^{-1}.
\]
As above, \(q-1\equiv-2\pmod\ell\) and
\(\ell\nmid\ord(v)\), so \(1+v\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.  For every
other proper \(d\), the smallest finite field containing \(v\) has
unit group of order prime to \(\ell\); its \(\ell\)-power map is an
automorphism.  We have therefore proved
\[
 1-z^a\in(\mathbb F_Q^\times)^\ell
 \qquad(1\leq a<M).
\]
Proposition~\ref{prop:mixed-jacobi-phase} now gives
\((\mathsf{DJ})\).  Conversely, \((\mathsf{DJ})\) makes every Fourier
coefficient \(C_j\) vanish, so \((\mathsf{MJ}_2)\) at \(a=1\) says
that \(1-z\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.  This is precisely failure of
\(D'_k\) at \(\ell\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the exceptional Jacobi vector has one selected coordinate]
\label{prop:dk-jacobi-top}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness}, and for

\[
 U=(\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z)^\times
\]

define

\[
 \lambda(\mathord\pm2^i)=2^i\pmod\ell
 \qquad(0\leq i<4h).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_1\)}
\]

Then \(\lambda:U\to\mathbb F_\ell^\times\) is a primitive even
Dirichlet character modulo \(M\).  If

\[
 L(a)=\operatorname{ind}_\ell(1-z^a),\qquad
 G_j=\sum_{a\in U}\lambda(a)\overline{\zeta_M}^{ja},
\]

then

\[
 L(a)=
 \begin{cases}
  0,&(a,M)>1,\\
  \lambda(a)L(1),&(a,M)=1,
 \end{cases}
 \qquad
 C_j=L(1)G_j.                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_2\)}
\]

Moreover

\[
 G_1G(\lambda^{-1})=\lambda(-1)M=M\ne0\pmod\ell,     \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_3\)}
\]

and consequently

\[
 D'_k\text{ holds at }\ell
 \iff L(1)\ne0
 \iff C_1\ne0
 \iff J_1\not\equiv-1\pmod{(\zeta_\ell-1)^2}.        \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_4\)}
\]

Thus the simultaneous Jacobi flatness in
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness} is a rank-one condition, not
\(M-1\) independent conditions.

This coordinate is exactly the first regulator of the quadratic-twist
antiunit in Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-antiunit}.  With

\[
 E_1=(1-ab)(1-ab^{-1}),\qquad
 E_2=(1-ab^2)(1-ab^{-2}),\qquad d_k=E_1/E_2,
\]

one has

\[
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(E_1)=2L(1),\qquad
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(E_2)=-2L(1),\qquad
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)=4L(1),                 \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_5\)}
\]

and hence

\[
 C_1=\frac{G_1}{4}\operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k).      \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_6\)}
\]

In particular the exact remaining exceptional theorem is the single
nonvanishing assertion

\[
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)\ne0
 \qquad(\ell\mid\Psi_k).                              \tag{\(\mathsf{DJ}_7\)}
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The group \(U\) has order \(\varphi(M)=8h\), while
\(\ord_M(2)=4h\).  Moreover \(-1\notin\langle2\rangle\), because a
power of two congruent to \(-1\) modulo \(3h\) has exponent
\(h\pmod{2h}\), whereas a power of two congruent to \(-1\) modulo
five has exponent \(2\pmod4\), and these conditions are incompatible
for odd \(h\).  Hence

\[
 U=\{\mathord\pm2^i:0\leq i<4h\}.
\]

Since \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\), the rule in \((\mathsf{DJ}_1)\) is
well-defined, multiplicative, and even.  It is primitive.  Indeed it
cannot factor modulo \(M/3\): the kernel of
\(U\to(\mathbb Z/(M/3)\mathbb Z)^\times\) has order three, whereas
the kernel of \(\lambda\) has order four.  It cannot factor modulo
\(M/5=3h\) either.  The CRT class \(r\) with

\[
 r\equiv1\pmod{3h},\qquad r\equiv2\pmod5
\]

belongs to the reduction kernel but satisfies \(\lambda(r)=-1\): in
the factorization

\[
 \lambda=\lambda_-\chi_5,
\]

the first factor is trivial on \(r\), while \(\chi_5(2)=-1\).
Removing either prime divisor of \(M\) therefore destroys the
character, which proves primitivity.

If \((a,M)>1\), then \(z^a\) has proper order.  The proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness} shows unconditionally that
every such \(1-z^a\) is an \(\ell\)-th power, and hence \(L(a)=0\).
If \((a,M)=1\), write \(a=\mathord\pm2^i\).  Frobenius covariance
gives \(L(2^i)=2^iL(1)\); replacing \(z^{2^i}\) by its inverse
changes \(1-z^{2^i}\) only by an \(M\)-torsion factor, whose
\(\ell\)-index is zero.  This proves the first identity in
\((\mathsf{DJ}_2)\).  Substitution into the Fourier coefficient
\((\mathsf{MJ}_1)\) of Proposition~\ref{prop:mixed-jacobi-phase}
gives \(C_j=L(1)G_j\).

The standard Gauss identity for a primitive Dirichlet character gives
\((\mathsf{DJ}_3)\).  Here \(\ell\nmid M\), so \(G_1\ne0\).
Corollary~\ref{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit} identifies \(D'_k\) with
\(L(1)\ne0\), while the first-order Jacobi expansion identifies
\(C_1\ne0\) with the final congruence in \((\mathsf{DJ}_4)\).

Finally the two exponents carrying \(ab\) and \(ab^{-1}\) are
trivial under the quadratic character modulo five, so both factors of
\(E_1\) have index \(L(1)\).  The exponents carrying \(ab^2\) and
\(ab^{-2}\) have quadratic character \(-1\), so both factors of
\(E_2\) have index \(-L(1)\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{DJ}_5)\), and \((\mathsf{DJ}_6)\)--\((\mathsf{DJ}_7)\)
follow because four and \(G_1\) are nonzero modulo \(\ell\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[canonical four-Jacobi detector]
\label{prop:dk-four-jacobi}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-top}.  Write the primitive
Dirichlet character as \(\lambda=\lambda_-\chi_5\), where
\(\chi_5=(\frac{\cdot}{5})\).  Decompose the selected character
\(\chi_M\) of Proposition~\ref{prop:mixed-jacobi-phase} uniquely as \(AB\),
where \(A,B\) have orders \(3h,5\), and set
\(X=\chi_\ell\) with the normalization
\(X(g)=\zeta_\ell\) used there.  For \(r=1,2,3,4\), let
\(j_r\bmod M\) be the CRT class
\[
 j_r\equiv1\pmod{3h},\qquad j_r\equiv r\pmod5,
\]
and put \(J_r=J(AB^r,X)\).  In the fraction field of the local
cyclotomic ring define
\[
 \mathcal D_{k,\ell}=\frac{J_1J_4}{J_2J_3}.
\]
Then, with \(\pi=\zeta_\ell-1\),
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal D_{k,\ell}
 \equiv1-4\pi C_1
 =1-\pi G_1\operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)
 \pmod{\pi^2}.}                                          \tag{\(\mathsf{FJ}_1\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 D'_k\text{ fails at }\ell
 \iff \mathcal D_{k,\ell}\equiv1\pmod{\pi^2},           \tag{\(\mathsf{FJ}_2\)}
\]
and
\[
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)
 =-G_1^{-1}\overline{\frac{\mathcal D_{k,\ell}-1}{\pi}}.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{FJ}_3\)}
\]

This four-term quotient is canonical and minimal under the conductor-five
symmetries.  Indeed, an inversion-invariant integer weight vector on the four
phases has the form \((u,v,v,u)\); zero augmentation forces \(v=-u\),
and the resulting detector line over \(\F_\ell\) is spanned by
\((1,-1,-1,1)\).  The primitive integral weights are its two signs.

There is also a single-antiunit form.  For a multiplicative character
\(C\), fixed in the conductor-five coordinate, such that \(C^2\) and
the displayed characters are nontrivial, put
\[
 \mathcal H_5(C)=
 \frac{J(CB,CB^{-1})}{J(CB^2,CB^{-2})}
 =\frac{G(CB)G(CB^{-1})}{G(CB^2)G(CB^{-2})}.              \tag{\(\mathsf{FJ}_4\)}
\]
Here \(G(\cdot)\) denotes the finite-field Gauss sum; the common
\(G(C^2)\) denominator has cancelled.
Then
\[
 \boxed{\mathcal D_{k,\ell}=\frac{\mathcal H_5(A)}{\mathcal H_5(AX)}.}
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{FJ}_5\)}
\]
For \(C=A\) or \(AX\), let \(E\) be the field generated by the values
of \(C\).  Conductor-five conjugation \(B\mapsto B^{-1}\) fixes
\(\mathcal H_5(C)\), so it lies in
\(E(\zeta_5+\zeta_5^{-1})\).  The nontrivial automorphism of this
quadratic extension, induced by \(B\mapsto B^2\), sends
\(\mathcal H_5(C)\) to its inverse.  Hence
\[
 \Norm_{E(\sqrt5)/E}\mathcal H_5(C)=1.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{FJ}_6\)}
\]
If the forward \(X\)-tangent is normalized by
\[
 \partial_X\log\mathcal H_5(A)
 =\overline{\frac{\mathcal H_5(AX)/\mathcal H_5(A)-1}{\pi}},
\]
then \((\mathsf{FJ}_3)\) reads
\(\operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)=G_1^{-1}\partial_X\log\mathcal H_5(A)\).
Order-five product formulas evaluate the invariant norm and therefore
erase, rather than evaluate, this anti-invariant tangent.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The four CRT classes have
\[
 C_{j_1}=C_{j_4}=C_1,
 \qquad C_{j_2}=C_{j_3}=-C_1.
\]
Indeed \(G_j=\lambda(j)^{-1}G_1\), while
\(\lambda(j_r)=\chi_5(r)\).
Since \(J_r\equiv-1+\pi C_{j_r}\pmod{\pi^2}\), and every
denominator is a \(\pi\)-adic unit,
\[
 \frac{(1-\pi C_1)^2}{(1+\pi C_1)^2}
 \equiv1-4\pi C_1\pmod{\pi^2}.
\]
Now use \((\mathsf{DJ}_6)\) and the nonvanishing of \(G_1\) to obtain
\((\mathsf{FJ}_1)\)--\((\mathsf{FJ}_3)\).  The minimality assertion
is the displayed two-dimensional linear calculation.

Finally the Gauss--Jacobi identity gives
\[
 \frac{J(AB,X)J(AB^{-1},X)}
      {J(AB^2,X)J(AB^{-2},X)}
 =\frac{\mathcal H_5(A)}{\mathcal H_5(AX)},
\]
because the common factor \(G(X)^2\) cancels.  Replacing \(B\) by \(B^2\)
interchanges numerator and denominator in \((\mathsf{FJ}_4)\), while
replacing it by \(B^{-1}\) only swaps factors.  This proves the last
claims.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[relative Eisenstein determinant]
\label{prop:dk-eisenstein-determinant}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-four-jacobi}, and put
\[
 S=2^{2h},\qquad K=\F_S,\qquad L=\F_{S^2}=\F_Q.
\]
The characters \(A\) and \(X\) are the norm lifts of unique characters
\(a_0,x_0\) of \(K^\times\), while \(B|_{K^\times}=1\):
\[
 A=a_0\circ\Norm_{L/K},\qquad
 X=x_0\circ\Norm_{L/K}.
\]
For \(r=1,2\), define the relative Eisenstein sums
\[
 \mathcal E_r(c)=
 \sum_{\operatorname{Tr}_{L/K}(z)=1}(c\circ\Norm_{L/K})(z)B^r(z).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_1\)}
\]
For every \(c\) with \(c^2\ne1\), one has
\[
 G_L((c\circ\Norm)B^r)=G_K(c^2)\mathcal E_r(c),
 \qquad \mathcal E_r(c)=\mathcal E_{-r}(c),                 \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_2\)}
\]
and the four-Jacobi antiunit has the exact quadratic-relative form
\[
 \boxed{\mathcal H_5(c\circ\Norm)
       =\left(\frac{\mathcal E_1(c)}{\mathcal E_2(c)}\right)^2,}
 \qquad
 \boxed{\mathcal D_{k,\ell}
 =\left(
   \frac{\mathcal E_1(a_0)\mathcal E_2(a_0x_0)}
        {\mathcal E_2(a_0)\mathcal E_1(a_0x_0)}
  \right)^2.}                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_3\)}
\]

Normalize \(X(z)=\zeta_\ell^{\nu(z)}\).  At the selected prime above
\(\ell\), set
\[
 e_r=\overline{\mathcal E_r(a_0)},\qquad
 u_r=\sum_{\operatorname{Tr}z=1}\overline{A(z)B^r(z)}\,\nu(z).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_4\)}
\]
Every \(e_r\) is nonzero, and
\[
 \mathcal D_{k,\ell}
 \equiv1-2\pi\left(\frac{u_1}{e_1}-\frac{u_2}{e_2}\right)
 \pmod{\pi^2}.                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_5\)}
\]
Consequently the surviving selected coordinate is exactly the determinant
\[
 \boxed{
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)
 =2G_1^{-1}\frac{\Delta_{k,\ell}}{e_1e_2},\qquad
 \Delta_{k,\ell}:=u_1e_2-u_2e_1,}                            \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_6\)}
\]
and therefore
\[
 D'_k\text{ holds at }\ell\iff\Delta_{k,\ell}\ne0.          \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_7\)}
\]

The first-order complete-\(B\)-orbit Hasse--Davenport relation does not
decide this determinant.  Indeed, for any conductor-five-fixed \(C\),
Frobenius gives
\[
 y_1=G(CB)=G(CB^{-1}),\qquad
 y_2=G(CB^2)=G(CB^{-2}),
\]
while the order-five product formula has the form
\[
 G(C)y_1^2y_2^2
 =-G(C^5)\prod_{j=0}^4G(B^j).                                \tag{\(\mathsf{ES}_8\)}
\]
Thus it constrains only the invariant product \(y_1y_2\), and after
the \(X\)-linearization only
\(u_1/e_1+u_2/e_2\).  Over the odd residue characteristic, this one
symmetric linear relation leaves the anti-invariant difference
\(u_1/e_1-u_2/e_2\) free, and in particular is compatible with its
vanishing.  Therefore the first-order relation \((\mathsf{ES}_8)\) cannot
prove \((\mathsf{ES}_7)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
One has \(3h,\ell\mid S-1\), whereas \(S\equiv-1\pmod5\).
Thus \(A\) and \(X\) descend through the norm and \(B\) is trivial on
\(K^\times\).  Decompose the Gauss sum over the fibres of
\(\operatorname{Tr}_{L/K}\).  On a nonzero fibre, write \(z=tw\) with
\(t\in K^\times\) and \(\operatorname{Tr}(w)=1\).  Since
\(\Norm_{L/K}(t)=t^2\), its contribution separates into
\(G_K(c^2)\mathcal E_r(c)\).  The trace-zero fibre is \(K\), and its
contribution vanishes because \(c^2\) is nontrivial.  This proves the first
identity in \((\mathsf{ES}_2)\).  The map \(z\mapsto z^S\) fixes trace and
norm and sends \(B\) to \(B^{-1}\), proving the second.

Substitute \((\mathsf{ES}_2)\) into \((\mathsf{FJ}_4)\).  The base-field
Gauss factors cancel and the two Frobenius-paired factors agree, giving the
first formula in \((\mathsf{ES}_3)\); \((\mathsf{FJ}_5)\) gives the second.
Since
\[
 X(z)=(1+\pi)^{\nu(z)}\equiv1+\pi\nu(z)\pmod{\pi^2},
\]
one has
\[
 \mathcal E_r(a_0x_0)
 \equiv\mathcal E_r(a_0)+\pi u_r\pmod{\pi^2}.
\]
Gauss sums over fields of characteristic two are units at every prime above
the odd number \(\ell\); hence \((\mathsf{ES}_2)\) makes each \(e_r\) a
unit.  Expanding the square in \((\mathsf{ES}_3)\) proves
\((\mathsf{ES}_5)\), and comparison with \((\mathsf{FJ}_1)\) proves
\((\mathsf{ES}_6)\)--\((\mathsf{ES}_7)\).

For a unit-circle form of the same determinant, every trace-one element of
\(L\) is uniquely
\[
 z=t^{-1}v,\qquad v\in\ker\Norm_{L/K}\setminus\{1\},
 \qquad t=v+v^{-1}.
\]
Thus \(e_r\) is a quintic Soto--Andrade sum weighted by
\(a_0^{-2}(t)\), while \(u_r\) inserts the extra weight
\(-2\operatorname{ind}_{x_0}(t)\).  Their determinant is genuinely the
anti-quintic Wronskian, rather than an unweighted full-field Fourier
coefficient.

Finally, apply the order-five Hasse--Davenport formula to the complete
\(B\)-orbit of \(C\).  In characteristic two the usual \(C(5^{-5})\)
factor is one, which gives \((\mathsf{ES}_8)\).  Its invariance under
\(B\mapsto B^2\) shows directly that its first-order term is the symmetric
sum.  Since that sum imposes no nonvanishing condition on the alternating
difference, the final assertion follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[semiprimitive parity obstruction on the mixed line]
\label{prop:dk-mixed-semiprimitive-no-go}
Let \(h=3^k\), let the prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), and retain the
primitive mixed character of conductor \(M\ell=15h\ell\) from
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-top}.  There is no exponent \(t\) for
which
\[
  2^t\equiv-1\pmod{5\ell}.
  \tag{\(\mathsf{SMP}_1\)}
\]
In particular, two is not semiprimitive modulo \(M\ell\).  Hence the
semiprimitive Gauss--Jacobi evaluations available for the pure
\(\ell\)-character and the proper-conductor components do not evaluate
the surviving primitive mixed coefficient \(C_1=G_1L(1)\).  More
precisely, the mixed conductor has decomposition index
\[
 \frac{\varphi(M\ell)}{\ord_{M\ell}(2)}=2(\ell-1),
 \tag{\(\mathsf{SMP}_2\)}
\]
whereas the pure \(M\)-component has index two and the pure
\(\ell\)-component is semiprimitive.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The cyclotomic-divisor criterion gives
\(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\), and \(2^h\equiv-1\pmod\ell\).  The unique
element of order two in \(\langle2\rangle\) is therefore reached exactly
at exponents
\[
  t\equiv h\pmod{2h}.
\]
Since \(h\) is odd, every such \(t\) is odd.  On the other hand,
\(\ord_5(2)=4\), so \(2^t\equiv-1\pmod5\) forces
\(t\equiv2\pmod4\), which is even.  Thus
\((\mathsf{SMP}_1)\) is impossible.  Because \(5\ell\mid M\ell\),
semiprimitivity modulo \(M\ell\) is impossible as well.  The final
statement follows: the mixed character is precisely the component whose
first-order value is \(G_1L(1)\), while the semiprimitive proper
components are the already forced-flat coordinates of
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness}.

Finally \(\gcd(M,\ell)=1\), so
\[
 \ord_{M\ell}(2)
 =\operatorname{lcm}(\ord_M(2),\ord_\ell(2))
 =\operatorname{lcm}(4h,2h)=4h,
\]
while \(\varphi(M\ell)=8h(\ell-1)\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{SMP}_2)\).  Thus the two explicitly evaluable constituent
Gauss sums leave a mixed Gauss sum of growing decomposition index in
their Jacobi quotient.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[only a weighted exceptional resolvent sees the first line]
\label{cor:dk-weighted-only}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-top}, put
\(U=(\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z)^\times\), and choose integer lifts
\(w_j\equiv\lambda(j)\pmod\ell\).  Then
\[
 \prod_{j\in U}(-J_j)\equiv1\pmod{\pi^2},
 \qquad
 \prod_{j\in U}(-J_j)^{w_j}
 \equiv1-8h\pi C_1\pmod{\pi^2}.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{HD}_1\)}
\]
The same unweighted congruence holds after adjoining all nonunit
indices \(1\leq j<M\).  Consequently every ordinary equal-weight
Hasse--Davenport product is flat whether or not \(D'_k\) holds, while
the weighted product is nonflat exactly when \(D'_k\) holds.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Changing variables in the Gauss sum of \((\mathsf{DJ}_2)\) gives
\(G_j=\lambda(j)^{-1}G_1\), hence
\(C_j=\lambda(j)^{-1}C_1\) for \(j\in U\).  Since
\(-J_j\equiv1-\pi C_j\pmod{\pi^2}\), the unweighted linear term is
\[
 -\pi C_1\sum_{j\in U}\lambda(j)^{-1}=0,
\]
whereas the weighted term is
\(-\pi|U|C_1=-8h\pi C_1\).  Here \(\ell\nmid8h\), so
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-top} proves the detector assertion.
For nonunit \(j\), Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness} already
gives \(J_j\equiv-1\pmod{\pi^2}\), proving the final extension.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the ordinary cyclotomic derivative is blind]
\label{prop:dk-cyclotomic-derivative-no-go}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-top}, write \(M=15h\), and
let \(\widetilde\rho\) be the complex primitive \(M\)-th root whose
reduction at the selected prime above two is \(z\).  Put
\(Q=2^{4h}\), choose a generator \(g\in\F_Q^\times\), and normalize
\[
 z=g^{(Q-1)/M},\qquad
 \xi=g^{(Q-1)/\ell},\qquad
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell(g^v)=v\pmod\ell.
\]
Then the selected Euler residue is exactly
\[
 (1-z)^{(Q-1)/\ell}=\xi^{L(1)}.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{ED}_1\)}
\]
By contrast, the reduction of the ordinary cyclotomic derivative is
unconditionally an \(\ell\)-th power:
\[
 \overline{\Phi'_M(\widetilde\rho)}
 =z^{-1}\frac{z^h-1}{(z^{3h}-1)(z^{5h}-1)}
 \in(\F_Q^\times)^\ell.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{ED}_2\)}
\]
Thus neither \(\Phi'_M(\widetilde\rho)\) nor any formula depending
only on its ordinary reduction can evaluate the selected exponent
\(L(1)\).

There is an exact weighted replacement, but it is the original target.
For \(a\in U=(\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z)^\times\), choose integer lifts
\(r_a\equiv\lambda(a)^{-1}\pmod\ell\) and put, modulo global
\(\ell\)-th powers,
\[
 \varepsilon_{\lambda^{-1}}
   =\prod_{a\in U}(1-\widetilde\rho^a)^{r_a}.
\]
Its selected reduction satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell
   (\overline{\varepsilon_{\lambda^{-1}}})
 =8hL(1).                                            \tag{\(\mathsf{ED}_3\)}
\]
Consequently \(D'_k\) holds at \(\ell\) exactly when this weighted
circular unit has nontrivial \(\ell\)-power residue at the selected
prime above two.

Finally, if \(\widetilde\lambda\) is the characteristic-zero lift of
the primitive even nontrivial character \(\lambda\), then
\[
 B_{1,\widetilde\lambda^{-1}}
 =-L(0,\widetilde\lambda^{-1})=0.                   \tag{\(\mathsf{ED}_4\)}
\]
This vanishing is independent of the exceptional target: for example
\(D'_2\) holds analytically by Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-projection},
so its selected \(L(1)\) is nonzero although
\((\mathsf{ED}_4)\) vanishes.  Hence the first generalized Bernoulli
value cannot be a nonzero scalar multiple of the selected Kummer index.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Equation \((\mathsf{ED}_1)\) is the Euler criterion after writing
\(1-z=g^v\): both sides are \(g^{v(Q-1)/\ell}\), and
\(v\bmod\ell=L(1)\).

Since \(M=5\cdot3^{k+1}=15h\), one has the polynomial identity
\[
 \Phi_M(X)
 =\frac{(X^M-1)(X^h-1)}{(X^{3h}-1)(X^{5h}-1)}.
\]
Differentiating at the primitive root \(\widetilde\rho\) gives
\[
 \Phi'_M(\widetilde\rho)
 =M\widetilde\rho^{-1}
   \frac{\widetilde\rho^h-1}
        {(\widetilde\rho^{3h}-1)(\widetilde\rho^{5h}-1)}.
\]
After reduction in characteristic two the odd scalar \(M\) becomes
one, proving the displayed equality in \((\mathsf{ED}_2)\).  The
element \(z\) has order \(M\), prime to \(\ell\).  Moreover
\(z^h,z^{3h},z^{5h}\) have orders \(15,5,3\), respectively, so the
three factors involving them lie in \(\F_{16}^\times\) or
\(\F_4^\times\).  All four factors therefore have order prime to
\(\ell\), and each is an \(\ell\)-th power in \(\F_Q^\times\).

The choice of the lifts \(r_a\) changes
\(\varepsilon_{\lambda^{-1}}\) only by an \(\ell\)-th power.  Using
\((\mathsf{DJ}_2)\) gives
\[
 \operatorname{ind}_\ell
   (\overline{\varepsilon_{\lambda^{-1}}})
 =\sum_{a\in U}\lambda(a)^{-1}L(a)
 =\sum_{a\in U}L(1)=8hL(1),
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{ED}_3)\); here \(\ell\nmid8h\).
Finally, for any nontrivial even character \(\chi\) modulo \(M\),
pairing \(a\) with \(M-a\) in
\[
 B_{1,\chi}=M^{-1}\sum_{a=1}^{M}a\chi(a)
\]
and using \(\sum_a\chi(a)=0\) gives \(B_{1,\chi}=0\).  Apply this to
\(\chi=\widetilde\lambda^{-1}\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{ED}_4)\) and the final separation.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the wild regulator is explicit but homogeneous]
\label{prop:dk-wild-regulator-no-go}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-top}.  Let
\(K_0/\mathbb Q_\ell\) be the unramified extension of degree
\(f=\operatorname{ord}_M(\ell)\) containing \(\mu_M\), with residue
field \(k_0\),
and for an unramified unit \(y\)
define
\[
 q_\ell(y)=\frac{y^{\ell^f-1}-1}{\ell}\pmod\ell.
\]
For \(\tau\in\mu_M\setminus\{1\}\), one has the finite truncated-log
formula
\[
 q_\ell(1-\tau)
 =-\frac1{1-\tau}
   \sum_{a=1}^{\ell-1}
    \frac{\tau^{a\ell^{f-1}}}{a}                    \tag{\(\mathsf{WL}_1\)}
\]
in the residue field.  For the characteristic-zero antiunit
\(d_k=E_1/E_2\) of \((\mathsf{DJ}_5)\), put
\[
 \begin{split}
 A_d={}&q_\ell(1-\alpha\beta)
       +q_\ell(1-\alpha\beta^{-1})\\
      &-q_\ell(1-\alpha\beta^2)
       -q_\ell(1-\alpha\beta^{-2}).
 \end{split}
 \tag{\(\mathsf{WL}_2\)}
\]
If \([\overline d_k]\) is its Teichm\"uller part, then
\[
 d_k=[\overline d_k]
       \bigl(1-\ell A_d+O(\ell^2)\bigr).             \tag{\(\mathsf{WL}_3\)}
\]

Now pass to \(K=K_0(\zeta_\ell)\), with
\(\pi=\zeta_\ell-1\).  For a mixed local selector
\[
 w=[\overline w](1+\pi B_w+O(\pi^2)),
\]
the critical explicit norm-residue formula has the form
\[
 (d_k,w)_{K,\ell}
 =\zeta_\ell^{
   \operatorname{Tr}_{k_0/\mathbb F_\ell}
       (c_\ell A_dB_w)},                             \tag{\(\mathsf{WL}_4\)}
\]
where \(c_\ell\) is a nonzero scalar depending only on the
uniformizer normalization.  Base-field selectors have \(B_w=0\).
For the canonical weighted Jacobi resolvent
\[
 \mathcal R_\lambda
 =\prod_{j\in(\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z)^\times}(-J_j)^{w_j},
 \qquad w_j\equiv\lambda(j)\pmod\ell,
\]
one has
\[
 B_w=-8hC_1=-8hG_1L(1),
 \qquad \operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)=4L(1).          \tag{\(\mathsf{WL}_5\)}
\]
Thus the wild pairing with the only canonical selector that isolates the
exceptional character is homogeneous in the still-unknown \(L(1)\).
When exceptional failure holds, both the selected Kummer symbol and the
critical wild term are one.  Explicit wild reciprocity therefore computes
the local regulator \(A_d\), but does not force
\(\operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)\ne0\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Because \(\tau^{\ell^f}=\tau\), expand
\((1-\tau)^{\ell^f}-(1-\tau)\) modulo \(\ell^2\).
The only interior binomial coefficients of valuation one have indices
\(a\ell^{f-1}\), \(1\leq a<\ell\), and
\[
 \frac1\ell\binom{\ell^f}{a\ell^{f-1}}
 \equiv\frac{(-1)^{a-1}}a\pmod\ell.
\]
Since \(\ell^{f-1}\) is odd, multiplication by
\((-\tau)^{a\ell^{f-1}}\), followed by division by \(1-\tau\),
proves \((\mathsf{WL}_1)\).  The Fermat quotient is additive on
products and subtractive on quotients modulo \(\ell\), which gives
\((\mathsf{WL}_2)\).  If
\(d_k=[\overline d_k](1+\ell c+O(\ell^2))\), then
\(\ell^f-1\equiv-1\pmod\ell\), so
\(q_\ell(d_k)=-c\); this proves \((\mathsf{WL}_3)\).

In \(K\), the first entry of the pairing lies in
\(U_{\ell-1}\) and the second in \(U_1\).  The critical case of
Iwasawa's explicit norm-residue formula~\cite{Iwasawa68} is the
nondegenerate residue pairing \((\mathsf{WL}_4)\); the unit converting
\(\ell\) to \(\pi^{\ell-1}\) is absorbed into \(c_\ell\).
Finally, first-order expansion of the displayed weighted product and
\((\mathsf{DJ}_2)\) give
\[
 \mathcal R_\lambda
 \equiv1-\pi\sum_{j\in U}\lambda(j)C_j
 =1-8h\pi C_1\pmod{\pi^2}.
\]
Equation \((\mathsf{DJ}_5)\) supplies the second identity in
\((\mathsf{WL}_5)\), completing the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[binary cyclic-parity form of exceptional failure]
\label{prop:dk-cyclic-parity}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness}, set
\[
 E=\frac{Q-1}{\ell},\qquad
 S_M(X)=1+X+\cdots+X^{M-1},
\]
and let \(R_{k,\ell}(X)\) be the degree-\(<M\) reduction of
\((1+X)^E\) modulo \(X^M-1\).  Then failure of \(D'_k\) at \(\ell\)
is equivalent to
\[
 R_{k,\ell}(X)=1+S_M(X)
 \quad\text{in }\mathbb F_2[X]/(X^M-1).               \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_1\)}
\]
Equivalently, among the binary submasks \(u\) of \(E\), the number
with \(u\equiv0\pmod M\) is even, while the number in every nonzero
residue class modulo \(M\) is odd.

Moreover, raising \((\mathsf{DP}_1)\) to the \(\ell\)-th power gives
only the unconditional identity
\[
 (1+X)^{Q-1}=1+S_M(X)
 \quad\text{in }\mathbb F_2[X]/(X^M-1).               \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_2\)}
\]
Thus ordinary exponent, determinant, or product consequences of
\((\mathsf{DP}_1)\) cannot rule out exceptional failure; the
remaining assertion is exactly that this selected binary-residue
parity pattern never occurs.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-jacobi-flatness} established
that failure is equivalent to
\[
 1-z^a\in(\mathbb F_Q^\times)^\ell
 \qquad(1\leq a<M).                                   \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_3\)}
\]
Here \(\ell\mid Q-1\), and in the cyclic group
\(\mathbb F_Q^\times\) an element \(v\) is an \(\ell\)-th power
exactly when \(v^E=1\).  Consequently \((\mathsf{DP}_3)\) says that
\(R_{k,\ell}(\xi)=1\) at every nonidentity \(M\)-th root \(\xi\).
At \(\xi=1\), it has value \(0\).  Since \(M\) is odd,
\(X^M-1\) is squarefree, and \(1+S_M\) has exactly these values.
This proves \((\mathsf{DP}_1)\), including its converse by evaluation
at \(z\).

Lucas's theorem gives
\[
 (1+X)^E=\sum_{u\subseteq_2 E}X^u,
\]
so cyclic reduction proves the submask-parity formulation.  Finally
\(S_M^2=MS_M=S_M\), hence \(1+S_M\) is idempotent.  The right side of
\((\mathsf{DP}_1)\) is therefore unchanged on taking its
