\(\ell\)-th power.  Identity \((\mathsf{DP}_2)\) is unconditional:
at \(X=1\) both sides vanish, while at every other \(M\)-th root
\(\xi\in\mathbb F_Q\), Fermat gives
\((1+\xi)^{Q-1}=1\).  Squarefree interpolation completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[single-deletion words exhaust the code and design bounds]
\label{prop:parity-code-no-go}
Let \(m\) be odd and put
\[
 A_m=\mathbb F_2[X]/(X^m-1),\qquad
 J_m=1+X+\cdots+X^{m-1},\qquad v_r=J_m+X^r.
\]
Then
\[
 v_r=X^r(1+J_m),\qquad
 \operatorname{supp}(v_r)=\mathbb Z/m\mathbb Z\setminus\{r\},
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_1\)}
\]
and, at the \(m\)-th roots in a splitting field,
\[
 v_r(1)=0,\qquad v_r(\xi)=\xi^r\quad(\xi\ne1).       \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_2\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \gcd(v_r,X^m-1)=X+1,                                \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_3\)}
\]
so the cyclic code generated by \(v_r\) is exactly the binary
single-parity-check code \([m,m-1,2]\).  Its coefficient support and
its finite-field Fourier support both have size \(m-1\).

The same support is the trivial cyclic difference set
\[
 (m,m-1,m-2),                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_4\)}
\]
whose nontrivial complex character sums have squared absolute value
one.  Adding the exceptional monomial gives
\[
 v_r+X^r=J_m,                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{CD}_5\)}
\]
the generator of the repetition code, whose Fourier support has size
one.

Under cubic failure, Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-cyclic-parity} gives
the word \(v_r\); under exceptional failure,
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-cyclic-parity} gives \(v_0\).  Hence cyclic
distance bounds, support-uncertainty inequalities, ordinary
difference-set multiplier conditions, and character-magnitude
conditions all admit the two failure patterns exactly.  After
\((\mathsf{CD}_5)\), the associated vanishing sum is merely the full
regular polygon \(J_m(\zeta)=0\), which decomposes through the proper
conductor primes.  These methods discard the same primitive CRT
coordinate isolated in \((\mathsf{CP}_7)\) and
\((\mathsf{DP}_1)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Translation fixes \(J_m\), so
\(X^r(1+J_m)=X^r+J_m=v_r\), proving
\((\mathsf{CD}_1)\).  Since \(m\) is odd,
\(J_m(1)=1\) in characteristic two, while
\(J_m(\xi)=0\) for every nonidentity \(m\)-th root.  This proves
\((\mathsf{CD}_2)\).  The polynomial \(X^m-1\) is squarefree, so
one is the only common root and \((\mathsf{CD}_3)\) follows.  The
ideal generated by \(X+1\) is the even-weight code, giving the stated
parameters.

Every nonzero group element has exactly \(m-2\) representations as a
difference of two elements outside a fixed singleton.  This proves
\((\mathsf{CD}_4)\); equivalently, a nontrivial complex character has
sum \(-\chi(r)\) over the support.  Equation \((\mathsf{CD}_5)\) is
immediate.  The two applications are exactly
\((\mathsf{CP}_3)\) and \((\mathsf{DP}_1)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[higher two-adic lifts carry no odd Kummer obstruction]
\label{prop:two-adic-kummer-no-go}
Let \(\mathcal O\) be the ring of integers of a finite unramified
extension of \(\mathbb Q_2\), with residue field \(K\), and let
\(\ell\) be odd.  Then
\[
 1+2\mathcal O\longrightarrow1+2\mathcal O,
 \qquad x\longmapsto x^\ell                         \tag{\(\mathsf{2K}_1\)}
\]
is an automorphism.  Under the Teichm\"uller splitting
\[
 \mathcal O^\times=[K^\times]\times(1+2\mathcal O), \tag{\(\mathsf{2K}_2\)}
\]
a unit is therefore an \(\ell\)-th power exactly when its residue in
\(K^\times\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.

The statement applies coordinatewise to the unramified lift of
\(\mathbb F_2[C_m]\) for odd \(m\), because that group algebra is
squarefree and hence a product of finite fields.  Consequently
modulo-four, modulo-eight, and higher carry refinements of the cubic or
exceptional parity identities cannot supply an independent obstruction:
the principal-unit lift is uniquely \(\ell\)-divisible once the
selected residue Kummer coordinate is fixed.  The same conclusion
applies to the Fermat and ordinary arms.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For \(u\in1+2\mathcal O\), the polynomial \(Y^\ell-u\) has the root
one modulo two and derivative \(\ell Y^{\ell-1}\), which is a unit.
Hensel's lemma gives a unique root in \(1+2\mathcal O\).  Thus
\((\mathsf{2K}_1)\) is bijective.  The standard Teichm\"uller
decomposition gives \((\mathsf{2K}_2)\) and the power criterion.  The
coordinatewise assertion follows from the squarefreeness of
\(X^m-1\) in characteristic two when \(m\) is odd.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exceptional half-block norm and its automatic symmetries]
\label{prop:dk-half-block}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-cyclic-parity}, and put
\[
 A=2^{2h},\qquad u=\frac{A-1}{\ell},\qquad
 R=\mathbb F_2[X]/(X^M-1),\qquad e=1+S_M.
\]
Let \(I=eR\), with identity \(e\), and set
\[
 U=1+X\in I^\times,\qquad \tau(X)=X^A,\qquad F=U^u.
\]
Then
\[
 F^\ell=\frac{\tau(U)}U,\qquad
 F\tau(F)=U^{u(A+1)},                                 \tag{\(\mathsf{DH}_1\)}
\]
and exceptional failure is exactly
\[
 F\tau(F)=e.                                          \tag{\(\mathsf{DH}_2\)}
\]
On the selected primitive \(M\)-th-root component of
Corollary~\ref{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit}, put
\[
 w=(1+\rho)(1+\rho^A)\in\mathbb F_A^\times,\qquad
 \upsilon=w^u\in\mu_\ell.
\]
Then failure is precisely \(\upsilon=1\).

There is no further Hilbert--90 or quadratic-norm obstruction to
\((\mathsf{DH}_2)\).  Indeed \(\tau(U)/U\) belongs to the norm-one
group of \(\mathbb F_{A^2}/\mathbb F_A\), whose order is \(A+1\);
since \(\gcd(\ell,A+1)=1\), it has a unique \(\ell\)-th root in that
group.  Failure says only that the distinguished root \(F=U^u\) is
this norm-one root.

The coefficient symmetries are automatic as well.  The
\(\tau\)-fixed residues modulo \(M\) are exactly the multiples of
five.  For such a residue \(j\),
\[
 [X^j]F\tau(F)
 =\sum_{\substack{a\pmod M\\(1+A)a=j}}[X^a]F.          \tag{\(\mathsf{DH}_3\)}
\]
After identifying the fixed subgroup with \(C_{3h}\), the right side
is, up to an invertible multiplier of its indices, the cyclic
reduction of \((1+Y)^u\); unconditionally,
\[
 (1+Y)^u=e_{3h}\quad\hbox{in }\mathbb F_2[C_{3h}].    \tag{\(\mathsf{DH}_4\)}
\]
Thus the fixed-residue part of the exceptional parity pattern is
forced before the selected Kummer question is asked.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since \(U^A=\tau(U)\), the first identity in
\((\mathsf{DH}_1)\) is
\[
 F^\ell=U^{A-1}=\frac{\tau(U)}U.
\]
The second follows by multiplying \(U^u\) and
\(\tau(U)^u=U^{Au}\).  Since
\[
 u(A+1)=\frac{A^2-1}{\ell}=\frac{Q-1}{\ell},
\]
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-cyclic-parity} proves
\((\mathsf{DH}_2)\).  On a primitive component, \(\tau\) is the
quadratic Frobenius of
\(\mathbb F_{A^2}/\mathbb F_A\), and evaluation gives
\(F\tau(F)=w^u=\upsilon\).  The norm-one assertion and uniqueness
then follow from
\(\ell\mid A-1\) and \(\gcd(A-1,A+1)=1\).

One has
\[
 \gcd(A-1,M)=3h,\qquad \gcd(A+1,M)=5.
\]
Hence the fixed residues of multiplication by \(A\) are precisely
the multiples of five.  In the convolution coefficient of such a
residue, swapping the two selected submasks preserves the residue.
Every off-diagonal pair cancels in characteristic two, leaving only
the diagonal pairs; this is \((\mathsf{DH}_3)\).  Multiplication by
\((A+1)/5\) is invertible modulo \(3h\), so the displayed pushforward
is the reduction of \((1+Y)^u\), up to a permutation of coefficients.

Put \(q=2^h\).  If \(\xi\ne1\) is a \(3h\)-th root of unity, then
\(\xi^q=\xi^{-1}\), and therefore
\[
 (1+\xi)^{q-1}=\xi^{-1}.
\]
Also \(u=(q-1)(q+1)/\ell\), while
\(3h\mid(q+1)/\ell\).  It follows that
\((1+\xi)^u=1\).  At \(\xi=1\) it vanishes, so squarefree
interpolation proves \((\mathsf{DH}_4)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[one-coordinate trace form of the exceptional phase]
\label{prop:dk-selected-trace}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-half-block}, put \(s=h/3\), and let
\[
 B\equiv2^s\pmod\ell.
\]
Then \(B\) has order six modulo \(\ell\) and
\(B^2-B+1=0\).  For \(\upsilon\in\mu_\ell\subset
\mathbb F_{2^{6s}}\), define
\[
 \mathcal T(\upsilon)
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathbb F_{2^{6s}}/\mathbb F_{2^s}}(\upsilon)
 =\sum_{j=0}^{5}\upsilon^{B^j}.
\]
One has the exact equivalence
\[
 \mathcal T(\upsilon)=0\iff\upsilon=1.                \tag{\(\mathsf{DT}_1\)}
\]
Consequently the exceptional assertion at \(\ell\) is exactly
\[
 \mathcal T\!\left(
   \bigl((1+\rho)(1+\rho^A)\bigr)^{(A-1)/\ell}
 \right)\ne0.                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{DT}_2\)}
\]
This is a single relative-trace coordinate of the selected residue
symbol.  It is equivalent to, rather than a generic norm consequence
of, the missing nontriviality.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The order identity \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h=6s\) proves the assertions about
\(B\).  Put \(x=\upsilon\) and \(y=\upsilon^B\).  The six powers of
\(B\), together with \(B^2=B-1\), give the elementary factorization
\[
 xy\,\mathcal T(\upsilon)
 =(x+y)(x+1)(y+1).                                    \tag{\(\mathsf{DT}_3\)}
\]
If \(\upsilon\ne1\), then \(x\ne1\), \(y\ne1\), and
\(x\ne y\), because \(\ell\) is prime and
\(B\not\equiv1\pmod\ell\).  Thus the right side is nonzero.  Conversely
\(\mathcal T(1)=6=0\) in characteristic two, proving
\((\mathsf{DT}_1)\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-half-block} identifies
failure with \(\upsilon=1\), so its negation is
\((\mathsf{DT}_2)\).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Cyclotomic antiunit and regulator bounds}

\begin{proposition}[quadratic-twist antiunit and the two exceptional zeros]
\label{prop:dk-antiunit}
Retain Corollary~\ref{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit}, put
\(m=3h\), and write the unique coprime-order decomposition
\[
 \widetilde\rho=\alpha\beta,
 \qquad \ord(\alpha)=m,\qquad \ord(\beta)=5.
\]
Set
\[
 s=\beta+\beta^{-1},\qquad s'=\beta^2+\beta^{-2},
\]
and define the cyclotomic antiunit
\[
 d_k:=
 \frac{(1-\alpha\beta)(1-\alpha\beta^{-1})}
      {(1-\alpha\beta^2)(1-\alpha\beta^{-2})}
 =\frac{\alpha^2-s\alpha+1}{\alpha^2-s'\alpha+1}
 =\frac{\alpha+\alpha^{-1}-s}
        {\alpha+\alpha^{-1}-s'}.                        \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_1\)}
\]
Then \(d_k\) belongs to
\[
 F_k^{\mathrm{tw}}=\mathbb Q(\alpha+\alpha^{-1},\sqrt5),
\]
is fixed by complex conjugation, and is inverted by the nontrivial
automorphism of
\(F_k^{\mathrm{tw}}/\mathbb Q(\alpha+\alpha^{-1})\).  In particular its relative
norm is one.

The invariant conductor-five norm loses exactly the information carried
by \(d_k\):
\[
 \Norm_{\mathbb Q(\mu_{5m})/\mathbb Q(\mu_m)}(1-\widetilde\rho)
 =\Phi_5(\alpha)=\frac{1-\alpha^5}{1-\alpha}.             \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_2\)}
\]
More precisely, reduce at the prime selected in
Corollary~\ref{cor:dk-cyclotomic-unit}, write
\[
 a=\overline\alpha,\quad b=\overline\beta,\quad
 c=b+b^{-1}\in\F_4\setminus\F_2,
\]
and put, in \(K=\F_{2^{2h}}\),
\[
 U_k=a^2+ca+1,\qquad U'_k=a^2+c^2a+1,\qquad
 R_k=\frac{U_k}{U'_k}=\overline{d_k}.                    \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_3\)}
\]
For every prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), the element \(\Phi_5(a)\) is an
\(\ell\)-th power in \(K\), and
\[
 D'_k\text{ holds at }\ell
 \iff U_k\notin (K^\times)^\ell
 \iff R_k\notin (K^\times)^\ell.                        \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_4\)}
\]
Thus the two conductor-five places over the cubic field carry inverse
Kummer classes; their invariant product is identically blind.

This antiunit is an exact quadratic lift of a conjugate of the cubic
Gaussian period.
If
\[
 q=2^h,\qquad \gamma=a+a^{-1},\qquad
 A=\gamma^2+\gamma+1,
\]
then
\[
 R_k^q=R_k^{-1},\qquad
 R_k+R_k^{-1}=A^{-1},\qquad
 \frac{R_k}{(R_k+1)^2}=A.                                \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_5\)}
\]
After choosing compatible roots \(a_k^3=a_{k-1}\) and keeping \(c\)
fixed, the degree-three norm swaps the two factors:
\[
 \Norm(U_k)=U'_{k-1},\qquad
 \Norm(U'_k)=U_{k-1},\qquad
 \Norm(R_k)=R_{k-1}^{-1}.                               \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_6\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \mathcal E_k:=R_k^{(-1)^k}
 \quad\text{satisfies}\quad
 \Norm(\mathcal E_k)=\mathcal E_{k-1}.                  \tag{\(\mathsf{QT}_7\)}
\]

Finally the cubic and exceptional arms are the untwisted and
quadratic-modulo-five twists of the same simple exceptional zero.  On
the current Kummer line the \(3\)-power cyclotomic generator acts as
\(+2\) for \(\ell\mid\Phi_h(2)\), and as \(-2\) for
\(\ell\mid\Phi_h(-2)=\Phi_{2h}(2)\).  With \(r=h/3\), the relative
norm polynomial
\[
 g_r(X)=1+X^r+X^{2r}
\]
has a simple zero at \(X=2\) in the cubic case and at \(X=-2\) in the
exceptional case.  Hence the norm coherence in
\((\mathsf{QT}_7)\) transports old factors but annihilates the current
one.  It does not prove the still-open nonvanishing in
\((\mathsf{QT}_4)\); both arms require the distinguished first-order
unit regulator in their respective \(\pm2\)-eigenspaces.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The coprime orders \(m\) and \(5\) give the unique decomposition
\(\widetilde\rho=\alpha\beta\).  The four automorphisms of
\(\mathbb Q(\mu_{5m})/\mathbb Q(\mu_m)\) fix \(\alpha\) and send
\(\beta\) through the four primitive fifth roots.  Therefore
\[
 \prod_{j=1}^4(1-\alpha\beta^j)
 =\Phi_5(\alpha)=\frac{1-\alpha^5}{1-\alpha},
\]
which proves \((\mathsf{QT}_2)\).  The two values \(s,s'\) are the
roots of \(X^2+X-1\), so their interchange is the nontrivial
automorphism of \(\mathbb Q(\sqrt5)\).  It interchanges the numerator
and denominator of \(d_k\).  Complex conjugation replaces \(\alpha\)
by \(\alpha^{-1}\) and multiplies both quadratic factors by
\(\alpha^{-2}\), so their quotient is fixed.  This proves all the
characteristic-zero assertions preceding \((\mathsf{QT}_2)\).

The residue of \(\alpha\) has order \(m\) and lies in \(K\), while the
residue of \(\beta\) lies in \(\F_{16}\).  Since
\[
 2^{2h}\equiv1\pmod m,\qquad 2^{2h}\equiv-1\pmod5,
\]
the local norm from \(L=\F_{2^{4h}}\) to \(K\) gives
\[
 \Norm_{L/K}(1+ab)=(1+ab)(1+ab^{-1})=U_k.
\]
Also \(2^{2h}+1\equiv5\pmod{15}\), so
\(\Norm_{L/K}(u)=u^5=\omega\).  Hence the corrected norm \(N\) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-translated-binomial} is \(\omega U_k\).
Because \(\ell\ne3\), it has the same \(\ell\)-Kummer class as
\(U_k\).

It remains to show that the invariant product is blind.  If \(x=1+a\),
then \(a^q=a^{-1}\) and
\[
 x^{q-1}=a^{-1}.
\]
Here \(\ell\nmid q-1\), whereas \(a\) has order \(m\), prime to
\(\ell\).  Projection to the \(\ell\)-Sylow subgroup of \(K^\times\)
therefore shows that \(1+a\) is an \(\ell\)-th power.  The same argument
applies to \(1+a^5\).  Thus
\[
 U_kU'_k=\Phi_5(a)=\frac{1+a^5}{1+a}
 \in (K^\times)^\ell.
\]
In additive notation on the one-dimensional Kummer quotient this says
\([U'_k]=-[U_k]\), and hence \([R_k]=2[U_k]\).  Since \(\ell\) is
odd, Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-translated-binomial} now proves
\((\mathsf{QT}_4)\).

As \(h\) is odd, \(q\)-Frobenius sends \(c\) to \(c^2\).  Directly,
\[
 U_k^q=a^{-2}U'_k,\qquad (U'_k)^q=a^{-2}U_k,
\]
which gives \(R_k^q=R_k^{-1}\).  Moreover
\[
 U_k+U'_k=a,\qquad U_kU'_k=\Phi_5(a).
\]
It follows that
\[
 R_k+R_k^{-1}
 =\frac{a^2}{\Phi_5(a)}
 =\frac1{(a+a^{-1})^2+(a+a^{-1})+1}.
\]
The last identity in \((\mathsf{QT}_5)\) is the characteristic-two
identity
\(R/(R+1)^2=1/(R+R^{-1})\).

The nonvanishing in \((\mathsf{QT}_4)\) is unchanged when \(a\) is
replaced by any primitive \(m\)-th root or \(c\) is interchanged with
\(c^2\): the former roots form one Frobenius orbit, and odd Frobenius
powers merely perform the latter interchange.  We may therefore choose
\(a_k^3=a_{k-1}\).  Factoring
\[
 U_k=(1+a_kb)(1+a_kb^{-1})
\]
and, after the harmless quadratic scalar extension adjoining \(b\),
taking the product over the three conjugates obtained by multiplying
\(a_k\) by a cube root of unity gives
\[
 \Norm(U_k)
 =(1+a_{k-1}b^3)(1+a_{k-1}b^{-3})=U'_{k-1}.
\]
The same calculation with \(b^2\) gives
\(\Norm(U'_k)=U_{k-1}\), proving
\((\mathsf{QT}_6)\)--\((\mathsf{QT}_7)\).

For the last assertion, let \(\sigma\) be the \(3\)-power cyclotomic
generator.  In the untwisted real field its reduction is Frobenius, so
it acts as \(2\) on the current \(\ell\)-Kummer line.  In the quadratic
twist, rational Frobenius is the product of \(\sigma\) and the
\(\sqrt5\)-involution.  The latter reduces to \(q\)-Frobenius and acts
as \(q=-1\) because \(\ell\mid q+1\); hence \(\sigma\) acts as
\(-2\).  If \(\ell\mid\Phi_h(2)\), then \(2^r\) is a primitive cube
root modulo \(\ell\).  If \(\ell\mid\Phi_h(-2)\) and \(\ell\ne3\),
then \((-2)^r\) is a primitive cube root instead.  Thus \(g_r\) vanishes
at the asserted scalar.  Its derivative there is
\[
 g_r'(X)=rX^{r-1}(1+2X^r)\ne0\pmod\ell:
\]
one has \(\ell\nmid r\), and a primitive cube root equal to
\(-1/2\) would force \(\ell\mid9\).  The zero is therefore simple.
The final open/non-open distinction follows: the norm operator vanishes
on the current line, while its first derivative only identifies the
one-dimensional regulator coordinate and does not evaluate \(d_k\) in
that coordinate.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[selected exceptional sextic and the Dickson-factor boundary]
\label{prop:dk-selected-sextic}
Retain Propositions~\ref{prop:dk-half-block},
\ref{prop:dk-selected-trace}, and \ref{prop:dk-antiunit}.  Put
\[
 s=3^{k-1},\qquad A_0=2^s,\qquad q=A_0^3=2^h,\qquad
 \Lambda=A_0^2-A_0+1=3\Psi_k,
\]
and write
\[
 \gamma=\gamma_k,\qquad g=\gamma_{k-1},\qquad
 C=g^2+g+1.
\]
Choose the orientation \(c\in\F_4\setminus\F_2\) from
\((\mathsf{QT}_3)\), and define
\[
 R=\frac{\gamma+c}{\gamma+c^2},\qquad
 r=R^{A_0+1},\qquad y=r+r^{-1}.
\]
Then \(r^\Lambda=1\).  If \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\) is prime and
\(e=\Lambda/\ell\), the half-block phase is exactly
\[
 \upsilon=(r^e)^{-1}.                                 \tag{\(\mathsf{DS}_1\)}
\]

Put \(z=\gamma^{A_0^2}\).  The selected trace coordinate collapses to
\[
 y=\frac{(g+1)z+g}{C},                                \tag{\(\mathsf{DS}_2\)}
\]
and has the irreducible cubic over \(\F_{A_0}\)
\[
 G(Y)=C^2Y^3+gCY^2+Y+g^2.                            \tag{\(\mathsf{DS}_3\)}
\]
The element \(r\) has degree six over \(\F_{A_0}\), with scaled
irreducible reciprocal polynomial
\[
\begin{split}
 S(T)=T^3G(T+T^{-1})
 ={}&C^2T^6+gCT^5+(C^2+1)T^4+g^2T^3\\
    &{}+(C^2+1)T^2+gCT+C^2.
\end{split}
\tag{\(\mathsf{DS}_4\)}
\]
Writing \(D_n(Y)=\mathcal D_n(Y,1)\), exceptional failure at \(\ell\)
is equivalent to each of
\[
 D_e(y)=0,\qquad
 G(Y)\mid D_e(Y),\qquad
 S(T)\mid T^e-1.                                     \tag{\(\mathsf{DS}_5\)}
\]
Moreover the trace in Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-selected-trace}
satisfies
\[
 \mathcal T(\upsilon)
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{A_0^3}/\F_{A_0}}\!\left(D_e(y)\right).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{DS}_6\)}
\]

There is an equivalent reverse-Dickson form.  Put
\[
 \mathcal A=\gamma^2+\gamma+1.
\]
Then \(\mathcal A\) and \(\mathcal A^{-1}\) have the irreducible
cubics
\[
\begin{split}
 \mathcal A^3+\mathcal A^2+(g+1)\mathcal A+C&=0,\\
 C Z^3+(g+1)Z^2+Z+1&=0.
\end{split}
\tag{\(\mathsf{DS}_7\)}
\]
and
\[
 y=D_{A_0+1}(\mathcal A^{-1}).
\]
Thus, with \(n=(q+1)/\ell=(A_0+1)e\), failure is also equivalent to
\[
 C Z^3+(g+1)Z^2+Z+1\mid D_n(Z).                       \tag{\(\mathsf{DS}_8\)}
\]

Finally the ambient Dickson polynomial has the exact factorization
\[
 D_e(Y)
 =Y(Y+1)^2
   \prod_{i=1}^{(e-3)/6}Q_i(Y)^2,                    \tag{\(\mathsf{DS}_9\)}
\]
where every \(Q_i\) is an irreducible cubic over \(\F_{A_0}\).
When \(e=3\), this independently recovers the prime-\(\Psi_k\) case
already proved in Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-projection}.  For composite
\(\Psi_k\), the recursion selects the explicit cubic \(G\) among
\((e-3)/6\) structurally admissible cubics; excluding that selected
factor remains exactly the exceptional theorem.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Use the notation in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-antiunit}.
Thus, for the order-\(3h\) element \(\alpha\),
\[
 U=\alpha^2+c\alpha+1=\alpha(\gamma+c),\qquad
 U'=\alpha^2+c^2\alpha+1=\alpha(\gamma+c^2),
\]
and \(R=U/U'\).  The half-block element \(w\) is \(U\).  Put
\[
 u=\frac{A_0^6-1}{\ell},\qquad
 \xi=r^e=R^{(q+1)/\ell}.
\]
Since \(UU'\) is an \(\ell\)-th power,
\((UU')^u=1\).  If \(\upsilon'= (U')^u\), then
\(\upsilon\upsilon'=1\), and hence
\[
 R^u=\frac{\upsilon}{\upsilon'}=\upsilon^2.
\]
On the other hand \(R^u=\xi^{q-1}=\xi^{-2}\), because
\(\xi\in\mu_\ell\) and \(q\equiv-1\pmod\ell\).  The group
\(\mu_\ell\) has odd order, so \((\upsilon\xi)^2=1\) proves
\((\mathsf{DS}_1)\).  Also \(R^{q+1}=1\), which gives
\(r^\Lambda=1\).

Let \(x=\gamma\), \(x_1=\gamma^{A_0}\), and
\(z=\gamma^{A_0^2}\).  The Gaussian-period recursion gives
\[
 x+x_1+z=0,\qquad z^3+z+g=0.
\]
Since \(c^{A_0}=c^2\),
\[
 r=RR^{A_0}
 =\frac{(x+c)(x_1+c^2)}{(x+c^2)(x_1+c)}
 =\frac ND.
\]
Here \(N+D=z\).  Also
\[
 ND=(x^2+x+1)(x_1^2+x_1+1).
\]
For \(f(T)=T^3+T+g\),
\[
 \prod_{t\in\{x,x_1,z\}}(t^2+t+1)
 =f(c)f(c^2)=(g+c)(g+c^2)=C.
\]
Therefore
\[
 y=\frac{(N+D)^2}{ND}
 =\frac{z^2(z^2+z+1)}C
 =\frac{(g+1)z+g}C,
\]
proving \((\mathsf{DS}_2)\).  Both \(C\) and \(g+1\) are nonzero,
so \(y\) generates the same cubic extension of \(\F_{A_0}\) as \(z\).
Substitution in \(z^3+z+g=0\) gives
\((\mathsf{DS}_3)\).

The quadratic \(T^2+yT+1\) of \(r\) over \(\F_{A_0}(y)\) is
irreducible.  Otherwise \(r\in\F_{A_0^3}\); but \(r^q=r^{-1}\),
so \(r=1\), which would force \(y=0\), contrary to
\((\mathsf{DS}_3)\).  This proves the degree-six assertion and
\((\mathsf{DS}_4)\).

Dickson's identity gives
\[
 D_e(y)=r^e+r^{-e}.
\]
As \(\ord(r)\) is odd, this vanishes exactly when \(r^e=1\), which by
\((\mathsf{DS}_1)\) is failure.  Minimal-polynomial divisibility
proves \((\mathsf{DS}_5)\).  Pairing the six
\(A_0\)-Frobenius conjugates of \(\upsilon=\xi^{-1}\) under the
inversion \(A_0^3\)-Frobenius gives
\((\mathsf{DS}_6)\).

The three conjugates \(x_i\) of \(\gamma\) satisfy
\[
 \sum_i(x_i^2+x_i+1)=1,\qquad
 \sum_{i<j}(x_i^2+x_i+1)(x_j^2+x_j+1)=g+1,
\]
and the product of the three transformed conjugates
\(x_i^2+x_i+1\) is \(C\).  This proves the first cubic in
\((\mathsf{DS}_7)\).  Since \(\gamma\) has degree three over
\(\F_{A_0}\), while it is at most quadratic over
\(\F_{A_0}(\mathcal A)\), the odd tower law forces
\(\F_{A_0}(\mathcal A)=\F_{A_0}(\gamma)\); hence the cubic is
irreducible.  Inversion gives the second cubic.  Directly
\[
 \frac{R}{(R+1)^2}=(\gamma+c)(\gamma+c^2)=\mathcal A,
\]
so the semiconjugacy in
Proposition~\ref{prop:fermat-coset-semiconjugacy} gives
\[
 \Phi(R)=\mathcal A,
 \qquad
 \Phi(r)
 =F_{A_0+1}(\mathcal A)
 =\frac1{D_{A_0+1}(\mathcal A^{-1})},
\]
and \(\Phi(r)=1/y\).  Therefore
\[
 y=D_{A_0+1}(\mathcal A^{-1}).
\]
The Dickson composition law, with \(n=(A_0+1)e\), now proves
\((\mathsf{DS}_8)\).

It remains to factor \(D_e\).  One has \(v_3(\Lambda)=1\), while
\(\ell\ne3\); hence \(e\equiv3\pmod6\).  Over an algebraic closure,
\[
 D_e(Y)
 =Y
  \prod_{\substack{\{t,t^{-1}\}\\t\in\mu_e\setminus\{1\}}}
    \bigl(Y-(t+t^{-1})\bigr)^2.                        \tag{\(\mathsf{DS}_{10}\)}
\]
The pair of nontrivial cube roots gives \((Y+1)^2\).
For every remaining \(t\), its order has a prime divisor other than
three.  Since \(A_0^3\equiv-1\pmod\Lambda\), while
\(\gcd(A_0-1,\Lambda)=1\) and
\(\gcd(A_0+1,\Lambda)=3\), its inverse-pair Frobenius orbit has
length three.  Grouping the remaining roots in
\((\mathsf{DS}_{10})\) proves \((\mathsf{DS}_9)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the selected Dickson phase has trivial downward norm]
\label{prop:dk-selected-norm-collapse}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-selected-sextic}.  Thus
\[
 A_0=2^{3^{k-1}},\qquad
 \Lambda=A_0^2-A_0+1=3\Psi_k,
 \qquad r\in\F_{A_0^6}^\times,\qquad r^\Lambda=1.
\]
Then
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{A_0^6}/\F_{A_0^2}}(r)=1.                \tag{\(\mathsf{NC}_1\)}
\]
More strongly, if \(\ell^a\parallel\Psi_k\), the norm annihilates
the full \(\ell\)-primary subgroup of
\(\F_{A_0^6}^\times\), while
\(\F_{A_0^2}^\times\) has no \(\ell\)-torsion.  Hence the induced
corestriction
\[
 \F_{A_0^6}^\times/
   (\F_{A_0^6}^\times)^{\ell^a}
 \longrightarrow
 \F_{A_0^2}^\times/
   (\F_{A_0^2}^\times)^{\ell^a}                    \tag{\(\mathsf{NC}_2\)}
\]
is zero on the entire current primary line.  In particular, the exact
antiunit recurrence cannot transport any part of the assertion
\[
 r^{\Lambda/\ell}\ne1
\]
from level \(k-1\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The norm exponent factors as
\[
 \frac{A_0^6-1}{A_0^2-1}
 =A_0^4+A_0^2+1
 =(A_0^2-A_0+1)(A_0^2+A_0+1)
 =\Lambda(A_0^2+A_0+1).
\]
Equation \(r^\Lambda=1\) proves \((\mathsf{NC}_1)\).  If
\(\ell^a\parallel\Psi_k\), then
\(\ell^a\mid\Lambda\), whereas
\(\operatorname{ord}_\ell(2)=6\cdot3^{k-1}\) makes \(A_0\) have
exact order six modulo \(\ell\), and hence
\[
 \ell\nmid(A_0^2-1)(A_0^2+A_0+1).
\]
Thus the displayed norm exponent kills the full current
\(\ell\)-primary subgroup, and raising to the \(\ell^a\)-th power is
an automorphism of the lower unit group.  Its Kummer quotient is
therefore trivial, proving \((\mathsf{NC}_2)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the trace--constant shape of every Dickson cubic]
\label{prop:dk-cubic-shape}
Let \(Q\) be a power of two with \(Q\equiv2\pmod3\), and put
\(\Lambda=Q^2-Q+1\).  If
\(t\in\mu_\Lambda\setminus\mu_3\) and \(y=t+t^{-1}\), then
\([\F_Q(y):\F_Q]=3\), and the monic minimal polynomial of \(y\) has
the form
\[
 Y^3+aY^2+bY+a^2,                                   \tag{\(\mathsf{CS}_1\)}
\]
where
\(a=\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{Q^3}/\F_Q}(y)\).

Consequently every irreducible cubic \(Q_i\) in
\((\mathsf{DS}_9)\) has this trace--constant shape.  The selected
cubic \(G/C^2\) has
\[
 a=\frac gC,\qquad b=\frac1{C^2},\qquad
 \frac{g^2}{C^2}=a^2.                               \tag{\(\mathsf{CS}_2\)}
\]
Thus its trace and constant term satisfy the universal Dickson-cubic
identity; coefficient shape cannot exclude the selected factor.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The congruence \(Q^3\equiv-1\pmod\Lambda\) shows that
\(y\in\F_{Q^3}\).  If \(y\in\F_Q\), equality of the inverse pairs
\(\{t^Q,t^{-Q}\}=\{t,t^{-1}\}\) would give
\(t^{Q-1}=1\) or \(t^{Q+1}=1\).  But
\[
 \gcd(\Lambda,Q-1)=1,
 \qquad
 \gcd(\Lambda,Q+1)=3,
\]
contrary to \(t\notin\mu_3\).  Hence the degree is three.

Put \(y_i=t^{Q^i}+t^{-Q^i}\) for \(0\leq i<3\), and
\(a=y_0+y_1+y_2\).  Since \(Q^2\equiv Q-1\pmod\Lambda\), expanding
the eight terms of the product and pairing inverse exponents gives
\[
 \begin{split}
 y_0y_1y_2
  ={}&(t^2+t^{-2})+(t^{2Q}+t^{-2Q})
       +(t^{2Q^2}+t^{-2Q^2})+1+1\\
  ={}&a^2.
 \end{split}                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{CS}_3\)}
\]
The trace and product are respectively the quadratic and constant
coefficients of the monic cubic in characteristic two, proving
\((\mathsf{CS}_1)\).  Dividing \((\mathsf{DS}_3)\) by \(C^2\)
gives \((\mathsf{CS}_2)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[depressing the selected cubic recovers the Gaussian recursion]
\label{prop:dk-depressed-selected-cubic}
Over a field of characteristic two, put
\[
 f(Y)=Y^3+aY^2+bY+a^2,
 \qquad \delta^2=a^2+b.
\]
Then
\[
 f(\delta U+a)
 =\delta^3\left(U^3+U+\frac{ab+a^2}{\delta^3}\right).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{DC}_1\)}
\]
For the recursively selected cubic \(f=G/C^2\), so that
\[
 a=\frac gC,\qquad b=\frac1{C^2},\qquad C=g^2+g+1,
\]
one may take \(\delta=(g+1)/C\), and the constant in
\((\mathsf{DC}_1)\) is exactly \(g\).  Thus
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 (G/C^2)\left(\frac{g+1}{C}U+\frac gC\right)
 =\left(\frac{g+1}{C}\right)^3(U^3+U+g).
 \quad}                                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{DC}_2\)}
\]
In particular, depressing and scaling the selected Dickson cubic is a
lossless recovery of the preceding Gaussian-tower equation; it supplies
no invariant independent of the recursion.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Direct expansion in characteristic two cancels the quadratic and
constant terms coming from \((\delta U+a)^3+a(\delta U+a)^2\), and
the linear coefficient is
\(\delta(a^2+b)=\delta^3\).  This proves
\((\mathsf{DC}_1)\).  In the selected case,
\[
 a^2+b=\frac{(g+1)^2}{C^2},
 \qquad
 ab+a^2=\frac{g(1+gC)}{C^3}.
\]
The identity \(1+gC=(g+1)^3\) now gives
\((ab+a^2)/\delta^3=g\), proving \((\mathsf{DC}_2)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the first Dickson Hasse jets are universal]
\label{prop:dk-dickson-hasse-jets}
Let \(F\) have characteristic two, let \(e\) be odd, and let
\(D_e\) be characterized by

\[
 D_e(T+T^{-1})=T^e+T^{-e}.
\]

Suppose that \(t^e=1\), \(t\ne1\), and put \(y=t+t^{-1}\).  If
\(D_e^{[j]}\) denotes the \(j\)-th Hasse derivative, then

\[
\begin{aligned}
 D_e^{[1]}(y)&=0,&
 D_e^{[2]}(y)&=y^{-2},&
 D_e^{[3]}(y)&=y^{-3},\\
 D_e^{[4]}(y)&=(y^2+1)y^{-6},&
 D_e^{[5]}(y)&=(y^2+1)y^{-7}.
\end{aligned}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{HJ}_1\)}
\]

The first place at which the exponent can enter the local expansion is
order six.  More precisely,

\[
 D_e^{[6]}(y)=\epsilon_e y^{-6},\qquad
 \epsilon_e=
 \begin{cases}
  1,&e\equiv1,7\pmod8,\\
  0,&e\equiv3,5\pmod8.
 \end{cases}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{HJ}_2\)}
\]

Consequently the derivative, multiplicity, and Hasse-jet data through
degree five impose no condition beyond the root coordinate itself:
they are fixed rational functions of \(y\), independent of \(e\).
Even the sixth jet adds only \(e\bmod8\), common to every root of the
same Dickson polynomial.  Thus higher multiplicity does not furnish a
new exponent-sensitive ancestry invariant for the recursively selected
cubic in Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-selected-sextic}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The element \(y\) is nonzero: otherwise \(t^2=1\), and the odd order of
\(t\) would force \(t=1\).  Work in the completed local ring and write

\[
 T=t(1+u),\qquad x=(T+T^{-1})-y,\qquad a=t^{-1}.
\]

Then

\[
 x=yu+a(u^2+u^3+u^4+u^5)+O(u^6).                    \tag{\(\mathsf{HJ}_3\)}
\]

Since \(t^e=1\),

\[
 D_e(y+x)=(1+u)^e+(1+u)^{-e}.
\]

For odd \(e\), the coefficient

\[
 \binom ej+\binom{e+j-1}j\pmod2
\]

is zero for \(j=0,1\), is one for \(2\leq j\leq5\), and at \(j=6\)
is \(\epsilon_e\).  Hence

\[
 D_e(y+x)=u^2+u^3+u^4+u^5+\epsilon_eu^6+O(u^7).
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{HJ}_4\)}
\]

Successively inverting \((\mathsf{HJ}_3)\) gives

\[
 u=px+qx^2+rx^3+sx^4+O(x^5),
\]

where

\[
 p=y^{-1},\qquad q=ay^{-3},\qquad r=ay^{-4},\qquad
 s=a(1+y^2)y^{-7}.                                  \tag{\(\mathsf{HJ}_5\)}
\]

Substitution in \((\mathsf{HJ}_4)\) gives the coefficients of
\(x^2,x^3,x^4,x^5\) as

\[
 p^2,\quad p^3,\quad q^2+p^2q+p^4,\quad
 p^2r+pq^2+p^5.
\]

Using \(a^2+ay=1\), these are exactly the four values in
\((\mathsf{HJ}_1)\).  The coefficient of \(x^6\) is

\[
 r^2+p^2s+q^3+p^4q+\epsilon_ep^6
 =\epsilon_ey^{-6};
\]

the non-\(\epsilon_e\) terms cancel again by \(a^2+ay=1\).  By the
defining Taylor identity for Hasse derivatives, these local
coefficients are \(D_e^{[j]}(y)\), proving both formulas and the stated
no-go.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the Gaussian depressed class is not a selected invariant]
\label{prop:dk-depressed-class-multiplicity}
Let \(Q=2^s\) with \(s\) odd, let \(g\in\F_Q\), and suppose that

\[
 h(U)=U^3+U+g
\]

is irreducible over \(\F_Q\).  For every \(\xi\in\F_Q^\times\), put

\[
 H_\xi=\xi^3+\xi+g,\qquad
 \delta_\xi=\frac{\xi^2}{H_\xi},\qquad
 a_\xi=\xi\delta_\xi,\qquad
 b_\xi=a_\xi^2+\delta_\xi^2,
\]

and

\[
 f_\xi(Y)=Y^3+a_\xi Y^2+b_\xi Y+a_\xi^2.
\]

Then \(H_\xi\ne0\), the cubics \(f_\xi\) are irreducible and pairwise
distinct, and

\[
 f_\xi(\delta_\xi U+a_\xi)
 =\delta_\xi^3(U^3+U+g).                            \tag{\(\mathsf{GM}_1\)}
\]

Put \(\Lambda=Q^2-Q+1\).  If

\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_Q/\F_2}(\xi^{-1})=0,          \tag{\(\mathsf{GM}_2\)}
\]

then \(f_\xi\mid D_\Lambda\).  There are exactly \(Q/2-1\) such
parameters \(\xi\).  Consequently the single depressed recursion
\(U^3+U+g\) occurs in \(Q/2-1\) distinct irreducible cubic factors of
the ambient Dickson polynomial \(D_\Lambda\).

For the exceptional arm, take \(Q=A_0\), \(g=\gamma_{k-1}\).  The
recursively selected cubic of
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-depressed-selected-cubic} is the member with

\[
 \xi=\frac{a}{\delta}=\frac g{g+1}.
\]

Thus, for \(k\geq2\), at least \(Q/2-2\) nonselected Dickson cubics
have exactly the same affine depressed equation.  Excluding the
selected factor from \(D_e\) must use its multiplicative placement in
the subgroup \(\mu_e\), not its full affine cubic class.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Irreducibility of \(h\) gives \(H_\xi\ne0\).  Put
\(a=a_\xi\), \(b=b_\xi\), and \(\delta=\delta_\xi\).  Since
\(a/\delta=\xi\) and \(\delta H_\xi=\xi^2\), direct substitution in
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-depressed-selected-cubic} gives

\[
 \frac{ab+a^2}{\delta^3}
 =\xi^3+\xi+\frac{\xi^2}{\delta}=g,
\]

which proves \((\mathsf{GM}_1)\).  Hence every \(f_\xi\) is
irreducible.  Moreover \(\delta\) is the unique square root of
\(a^2+b\), so the parameter \(\xi=a/\delta\) is recovered from the
coefficients of \(f_\xi\); the cubics are pairwise distinct.

Let \(y\) be a root of \(f_\xi\) in \(\F_{Q^3}\).  The quadratic

\[
 T^2+yT+1
\]

is irreducible over \(\F_{Q^3}\) exactly when

\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{Q^3}/\F_2}(y^{-2})=1.
\]

If \(y_0,y_1,y_2\) are the three conjugates of \(y\), then

\[
 \sum_i y_i^{-1}=\frac b{a^2},
\]

so invariance of absolute trace under squaring gives

\[
\begin{aligned}
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{Q^3}/\F_2}(y^{-2})
 &=\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_Q/\F_2}\!\left(\frac b{a^2}\right)\\
 &=\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_Q/\F_2}(1+\xi^{-2})
  =1+\operatorname{Tr}_{\F_Q/\F_2}(\xi^{-1}).
\end{aligned}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{GM}_3\)}
\]

Here \(\operatorname{Tr}(1)=1\) because \(s\) is odd.  Thus
\((\mathsf{GM}_2)\) makes the quadratic irreducible.  Choose a root
\(t\in\F_{Q^6}\), so \(y=t+t^{-1}\) and \(t^{Q^3}=t^{-1}\).

It remains to recover the smaller torus \(\mu_\Lambda\), rather than
only \(\mu_{Q^3+1}\).  Put

\[
 x=t,\qquad u=t^Q,\qquad v=t^{Q^2}.
\]

The trace--constant identity for \(f_\xi\) has the exact factorization

\[
\begin{aligned}
 &\prod_{w\in\{x,u,v\}}(w+w^{-1})
  +\left(\sum_{w\in\{x,u,v\}}(w+w^{-1})\right)^2\\
 &\qquad=
 \frac{(u+xv)(v+xu)(x+uv)(1+xuv)}{x^2u^2v^2}=0.
\end{aligned}                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{GM}_4\)}
\]

The four factors respectively assert that the order of \(t\) divides

\[
 Q^2-Q+1,\quad Q^2-Q-1,\quad Q^2+Q-1,\quad Q^2+Q+1.
\]

On the other hand it divides \(Q^3+1\).  The last three displayed
integers are coprime to \(Q^3+1\): for the first two of them this
follows by Euclidean reduction to the coprime pairs
\((Q+1,Q^2-Q-1)\) and \((Q,Q^2+Q-1)\), while
\(Q^2+Q+1\mid Q^3-1\) and \(\gcd(Q^3-1,Q^3+1)=1\) because both
relevant orders are odd.  Since \(t\ne1\), only the first factor can
vanish.  Hence \(t^\Lambda=1\), and Dickson's identity gives

\[
 D_\Lambda(y)=t^\Lambda+t^{-\Lambda}=0.
\]

Thus \(f_\xi\mid D_\Lambda\).  Finally inversion permutes
\(\F_Q^\times\), and the absolute-trace-zero hyperplane has \(Q/2\)
elements, one of which is zero.  Therefore exactly \(Q/2-1\) nonzero
parameters satisfy \((\mathsf{GM}_2)\).  The specialization to the
selected cubic follows from
\(a=g/C\) and \(\delta=(g+1)/C\); the reciprocal sextic is
irreducible by Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-selected-sextic}, so
\((\mathsf{GM}_3)\) also places the selected parameter among the
\(Q/2-1\) counted above.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the selected cubic's absolute-trace fingerprint]
\label{prop:dk-selected-fingerprint}
Put \(K_j=\F_{2^{3^j}}\), set \(\gamma_0=1\), and choose the
compatible Gaussian periods so that
\(\gamma_j^3+\gamma_j=\gamma_{j-1}\).  Define
\[
 \phi(X)=\frac{X}{X^2+X+1},
 \qquad
 \psi(X)=\frac1{X^2+X+1}.
\]
Then, for every \(j\geq1\),
\[
 \begin{split}
 \operatorname{Tr}_{K_j/K_{j-1}}\!\bigl(\phi(\gamma_j)\bigr)
   &=\phi(\gamma_{j-1}),\\
 \operatorname{Tr}_{K_j/K_{j-1}}\!\bigl(\psi(\gamma_j)\bigr)
   &=\phi(\gamma_{j-1})+\psi(\gamma_{j-1}).
 \end{split}                                         \tag{\(\mathsf{CF}_1\)}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{K_j/\F_2}\!\bigl(\phi(\gamma_j)\bigr)=1,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{K_j/\F_2}\!\bigl(\psi(\gamma_j)\bigr)
   =j+1\pmod2.                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{CF}_2\)}
\]
In the notation of Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-selected-sextic}, this
gives the selected coefficient fingerprint
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{A_0}/\F_2}\!\left(\frac gC\right)=1,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_{A_0}/\F_2}\!\left(\frac1{C^2}\right)
   =k\pmod2.                                        \tag{\(\mathsf{CF}_3\)}
\]
These identities distinguish two absolute coordinates of \(G\), but
the factorization \((\mathsf{DS}_9)\) supplies no incompatible
coordinate identity; they do not by themselves exclude \(G\mid D_e\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let \(x_0,x_1,x_2\) be the roots of
\(f(T)=T^3+T+b\), where \(b=\gamma_{j-1}\), and work after the
harmless scalar extension adjoining
\(c\in\F_4\setminus\F_2\).  The denominators below are nonzero:
\(\gamma_j\) has odd degree \(3^j\), so it cannot be a nontrivial
element of \(\F_4\).  The partial fractions
\[
 \phi(x)=\frac c{x+c}+\frac{c^2}{x+c^2},
 \qquad
 \psi(x)=\frac1{x+c}+\frac1{x+c^2}
\]
and the logarithmic-derivative identity
\[
 \sum_{i=0}^2\frac1{x_i+a}=\frac{f'(a)}{f(a)}
\]
apply.  Since
\[
 f(c)=b+c^2,\quad f(c^2)=b+c,quad
 f'(c)=c,\quad f'(c^2)=c^2,
\]
direct simplification gives \((\mathsf{CF}_1)\).

The first identity iterates to
\(\phi(\gamma_0)=\phi(1)=1\).  If
\(U_j=\operatorname{Tr}_{K_j/\F_2}(\psi(\gamma_j))\), the second
gives \(U_j=1+U_{j-1}\), with \(U_0=\psi(1)=1\); this proves
\((\mathsf{CF}_2)\).  Finally take \(j=k-1\), use
\(C=g^2+g+1\), and use invariance of the absolute trace under
squaring to obtain \((\mathsf{CF}_3)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the joint fingerprint admits a nonselected factor]
\label{prop:dk-fingerprint-countermodel}
At \(k=4\), the two absolute traces in
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-selected-fingerprint} are simultaneously
realized by an irreducible cubic factor of the bad Dickson polynomial
which is not the selected cubic.  More precisely, put
\[
 A=2^{27},\qquad
 \Psi_4=163\cdot135433\cdot272010961,
 \qquad \Lambda=A^2-A+1=3\Psi_4,
\]
and let \(P_4\) be the projected element of
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-projection}.  In the exact trinomial model
\(\F_2[X]/(X^{162}+X^{81}+1)\), one has
\(\ord(P_4)=\Psi_4\).  Set
\[
 t=P_4^{163\cdot7},\qquad
 e=\frac{\Lambda}{163}
   =3\cdot135433\cdot272010961,
 \qquad y_t=t+t^{-1}.
\]
Then
\[
 \ord(t)=135433\cdot272010961,
\]
so \(t\in\mu_e\setminus\mu_3\).  If
\[
 a_t=\sum_{i=0}^2y_t^{A^i},\qquad
 b_t=\sum_{0\leq i<j\leq2}y_t^{A^i}y_t^{A^j},
\]
then
\[
 Q_t(Y)=Y^3+a_tY^2+b_tY+a_t^2
\]
is an irreducible cubic factor of \(D_e(Y)\), distinct from \(G\), and
the exact finite-field certificate gives
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_A/\F_2}(a_t)=1,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\F_A/\F_2}(b_t)=0.              \tag{\(\mathsf{CF}_4\)}
\]
This is exactly the selected pair \((1,k\bmod2)=(1,0)\).  Thus even
the two trace coordinates jointly cannot exclude the selected factor.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The order and trace statements in this proposition are an exact finite
certificate, not an analytical instance of \(D'_k\).  They use the same
bit-polynomial arithmetic as
\path{experiments/exception_column_m4.py}: represent \(\zeta\) by \(X\),
reduce with \(X^{162}=X^{81}+1\), form \(P_4\) from
\((\mathsf D_2)\), and use repeated squaring for Frobenius and absolute
trace.  Exact powering first gives
\[
 P_4^{\Psi_4}=1,
 \qquad
 P_4^{\Psi_4/r}\ne1
 \quad(r=163,135433,272010961).
\]
Since \(\gcd(163\cdot7,\Psi_4)=163\), this proves the displayed order
of \(t\), hence \(t^e=1\).  Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-cubic-shape}
then gives the irreducible cubic \(Q_t\), and the root factorization
\((\mathsf{DS}_{10})\) gives \(Q_t\mid D_e\).

The same exact reduction evaluates the two Frobenius sums defining
\(a_t,b_t\); summing their \(27\) absolute Frobenius conjugates gives
respectively \(1\) and \(0\), which is \((\mathsf{CF}_4)\).  Finally
the certified selected element at \(k=4\) retains its \(163\)-primary
order, whereas \(t\) does not.  Their inverse-pair Frobenius orbits
therefore cannot agree, so \(Q_t\ne G\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[the plus and minus current tori have disjoint orders]
\label{rem:plus-minus-tori}
At the same cubic step \(Q=2^{3^{k-1}}\), the current rational-point
groups in arms \((C)\) and \((D)\) have respective orders
\[
 Q^2+Q+1,\qquad Q^2-Q+1.
\]
Their gcd divides both \(2Q\) and two odd integers coprime to \(Q\),
so it is one.  Hence every homomorphism between these two finite
rational-point groups is trivial.  The common antiunit formalism does
not transfer a current primary order assertion from one arm to the
other; the two selected Kummer classes require separate evaluations.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[partition regulator sieve for the exceptional arm]
\label{prop:dk-partition-regulator}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-projection}.  Let
\[
 H_k=\left\lfloor\frac{h-1}{8}\right\rfloor
\]
and let \(B_-(h)\) be the number of partitions into distinct positive
parts congruent to \(1\pmod3\) whose total is at most \(H_k\).  Then
\[
 \ord(P_k)\geq B_-(h)
 =\exp\!\left(
    \left(\frac{\pi}{6\sqrt2}+o(1)\right)\sqrt h
   \right).                                             \tag{\(\mathsf{PR}_1\)}
\]
Consequently, if \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\) and
\[
 \ell>\frac{\Lambda_k}{B_-(h)},                         \tag{\(\mathsf{PR}_2\)}
\]
then \(P_k\), and hence \(M_k\), contains the full
\(\ell\)-primary part of \(\Lambda_k\) in its order.  Thus \(D'_k\)
holds at every current prime satisfying \((\mathsf{PR}_2)\).  This
replaces the linear denominator in \((\mathsf D_6)\) by an
unconditional quantity exponential in \(\sqrt h\); current factors at
or below the new threshold remain open.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The element \(\zeta\) has degree \(h\) over \(\F_4\), because
\(\ord_{3h}(4)=h\).  The subgroup generated by \(4\) in
\((\mathbb Z/3h\mathbb Z)^\times\) consists exactly of the classes
congruent to \(1\pmod3\).  Hence, for every positive
\(j\equiv1\pmod3\) with \(j<3h\), there is \(z_j\) such that
\(4^{z_j}\equiv j\pmod{3h}\).

Put
\[
 A_j(X)=X^{4j}+X^j+\omega,
 \qquad
 B_j(X)=\omega^2X^{4j}+X^{3j}+1.
\]
Equation~\((\mathsf D_2)\), followed by \(4^{z_j}\)-Frobenius, gives
\[
 P_k^{\,4^{z_j}}=\frac{A_j(\zeta)}{B_j(\zeta)}.          \tag{\(\mathsf{PR}_3\)}
\]
For a partition \(I\) counted by \(B_-(h)\), set
\(E_I=\sum_{j\in I}4^{z_j}\).  We claim that the powers
\(P_k^{E_I}\) are pairwise distinct.

Suppose two of them, indexed by \(I\) and \(J\), were equal.  Cancel
the common parts and cross-multiply in \((\mathsf{PR}_3)\).  The
resulting polynomial in \(\F_4[X]\) has degree at most
\[
 4\left(\sum_{j\in I}j+\sum_{j\in J}j\right)
 \leq8H_k<h.                                           \tag{\(\mathsf{PR}_4\)}
\]
It is nonzero.  Indeed \(A_j(0)/B_j(0)=\omega\), so different
cardinalities modulo three are already separated by the constant
term.  After dividing each factor by \(\omega\), its expansion at
zero is
\[
 \omega^{-1}\frac{A_j(X)}{B_j(X)}
 =1+\omega^2X^j+O(X^{2j}).                             \tag{\(\mathsf{PR}_5\)}
\]
If the constant terms agree, the coefficient belonging to the least
part in \(I\mathbin{\triangle}J\) therefore separates the two
products.  A nonzero polynomial of degree below \(h\) cannot vanish at
\(\zeta\), proving the claim.  The \(B_-(h)\) distinct powers give the
first inequality in \((\mathsf{PR}_1)\).

For the asymptotic, the generating function for distinct parts
congruent to one modulo three is
\[
 \prod_{r\geq0}(1+x^{3r+1}).
\]
Its logarithm at \(x=e^{-t}\) is
\(\pi^2/(36t)+O(\log(1/t))\).  The usual
Meinardus/saddle-point estimate~\cite{Meinardus54} therefore gives
\(\log B_-(h)\sim(\pi/3)\sqrt{H_k}\), which is the second assertion
of \((\mathsf{PR}_1)\).

Finally \(P_k\) lies in the cyclic subgroup of order \(\Lambda_k\).
If its order missed the full \(\ell\)-primary part, then \(P_k\)
would lie in the subgroup of \(\ell\)-th powers, of order
\(\Lambda_k/\ell\).  All the powers just constructed would lie there,
so \(B_-(h)\leq\Lambda_k/\ell\), contrary to
\((\mathsf{PR}_2)\).  Equation~\((\mathsf D_1)\) transfers the
conclusion to \(M_k\).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Exact bad-locus exhaustions}

\begin{proposition}[semiprimitive exhaustion and the saturated planes]
\label{prop:dk-semiprimitive}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-line-saturation}, put

\[
 h=3^k,\qquad q=2^h,\qquad
 K=\F_{q^2},\qquad L=\F_{q^4},
\]

and, for a prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), set

\[
 H_K=(K^\times)^\ell,\qquad H_L=(L^\times)^\ell.
\]

Every nontrivial order-\(\ell\) character \(\chi\) on \(K^\times\)
has, for the canonical additive character,

\[
 G_K(\chi^j)=q\qquad(1\leq j<\ell).                 \tag{\(\mathsf{SP}_1\)}
\]

Every order-\(\ell\) character on \(L^\times\) is the norm lift of
one on \(K^\times\), and quadratic Hasse--Davenport gives

\[
 G_L(\widetilde\chi^j)=-q^2\qquad(1\leq j<\ell).    \tag{\(\mathsf{SP}_2\)}
\]

Consequently, whenever \(a,b,a+b\not\equiv0\pmod\ell\),

\[
 J_K(\chi^a,\chi^b)=q,\qquad
 J_L(\widetilde\chi^a,\widetilde\chi^b)=-q^2,      \tag{\(\mathsf{SP}_3\)}
\]

while \(J(\chi^a,\chi^{-a})=-1\) in either field.

Failure of \(D'_k\) at \(\ell\) has the following two equivalent
additive forms:

\[
 W:=\F_{16}+\F_{16}\zeta,\qquad
 W\setminus\{0\}\subset H_L,                       \tag{\(\mathsf{SP}_4\)}
\]

and

\[
 V:=\F_4+\F_4\zeta+\F_4\zeta^2,\qquad
 V\setminus\{0\}\subset H_K.                       \tag{\(\mathsf{SP}_5\)}
\]

Thus a bad translate saturates an eight-dimensional \(\F_2\)-space in
\(L\), and its norm-down form saturates a six-dimensional
\(\F_2\)-space in \(K\), not merely the original affine line.

The exact semiprimitive Fourier equations nevertheless permit both
planes.  If \(W^\perp\) is the additive annihilator of \(W\), put

\[
 A_L=|(W^\perp\setminus\{0\})\cap H_L|,\qquad R=q^2,\qquad w=256.
\]

Then orthogonality and \((\mathsf{SP}_2)\) force

\[
 A_L=\frac{(R-1)(R+w)-\ell R(w-1)}{\ell w},
 \qquad
 |W^\perp\setminus(H_L\cup\{0\})|
 =\frac{(R-1)(R+w)(\ell-1)}{\ell w}.                \tag{\(\mathsf{SP}_6\)}
\]

Likewise, for \(w_0=64\),

\[
 A_K:=|(V^\perp\setminus\{0\})\cap H_K|
 =\frac{(q+1)(q-w_0)+\ell q(w_0-1)}{\ell w_0},       \tag{\(\mathsf{SP}_7\)}
\]

and

\[
 |V^\perp\setminus(H_K\cup\{0\})|
 =\frac{(\ell-1)(q+1)(q-w_0)}{\ell w_0}.            \tag{\(\mathsf{SP}_8\)}
\]

All four quantities are nonnegative integers with the required total
sizes.  Hence the complete semiprimitive Gauss and Jacobi table, even
together with the dimensions of the saturated spaces, gives no
contradiction.  A proof of \(D'_k\) must use the distinguished
cyclotomic placement of \(W\) or \(V\), not only the semiprimitive
character table or additive-subspace incidence.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\), one has \(q\equiv-1\pmod\ell\).
Thus every order-\(\ell\) character of \(K^\times\) is trivial on
\(\F_q^\times\).  Decompose \(K^\times\) into its \(q+1\)
projective \(\F_q\)-lines.  On the trace-zero line \(\F_q\), the
additive sum is \(q-1\); on every other line it is \(-1\).  The induced
nontrivial projective character sums to zero, giving
\(G_K(\chi)=q\).  The same argument applies to every nontrivial power.
The norm induces an isomorphism on the order-\(\ell\) character groups,
so Hasse--Davenport proves \((\mathsf{SP}_2)\).  The standard quotient
formula for Jacobi sums proves \((\mathsf{SP}_3)\), with the inverse
case evaluated separately.

If \(D'_k\) fails, Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-line-saturation} puts every
\(\zeta+c\), \(c\in\F_{16}\), in \(H_L\).  Since
\(\F_{16}^\times\subset H_L\), every nonzero
\(a\zeta+b\) is an \(\ell\)-th power, proving
\((\mathsf{SP}_4)\); the converse follows by taking \(a=1,b=u\).

For \((\mathsf{SP}_5)\), every polynomial of degree at most two over
\(\F_4\) is a product of linear factors or is an irreducible quadratic.
In the first case its value at \(\zeta\) is an \(\ell\)-th power by
\((\mathsf{SP}_4)\).  In the second, choose a root
\(c\in\F_{16}\setminus\F_4\); its value is

\[
 (\zeta+c)(\zeta+c^4)
 =\Norm_{L/K}(\zeta+c),
\]

again an \(\ell\)-th power.  Conversely the corrected norm
\(N=\zeta^2+\zeta+\omega\) belongs to \(V\); membership in \(H_K\)
is exactly failure of \(D'_k\).

Finally the two additive Fourier values of \(H_L\) are

\[
 \frac{-1-R(\ell-1)}\ell\quad\hbox{on }H_L,
 \qquad
 \frac{R-1}\ell\quad\hbox{off }H_L,
\]

by \((\mathsf{SP}_2)\).  Fourier inversion applied to
\(|W\cap H_L|=w-1\) gives \((\mathsf{SP}_6)\).  The corresponding
values for \(H_K\) are

\[
 \frac{-1+q(\ell-1)}\ell\quad\hbox{on }H_K,
 \qquad
 \frac{-1-q}\ell\quad\hbox{off }H_K,
\]

and \(|V\cap H_K|=w_0-1\) gives
\((\mathsf{SP}_7)\)--\((\mathsf{SP}_8)\).  Integrality follows from
\(\ell\mid q+1\), \(w\mid q^2\), and \(w_0\mid q\); nonnegativity is
immediate from \(\ell\leq q+1\) and \(q\geq2^9\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[cyclotomic Dirichlet coefficient and subspace-polynomial exhaustion]
\label{prop:dk-dirichlet-exhaustion}
Retain \(k\geq2\), put \(h=3^k\), and write
\[
 K=\mathbb F_{4^h}=\mathbb F_4(\zeta),\qquad
 \zeta^h=\omega.
\]
Let \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\), let
\(\chi:K^\times\to\mu_\ell\) have exact order \(\ell\), realized
complexly, and let
\(\widetilde\chi=\chi\circ\operatorname{Norm}_{\mathbb F_{16^h}/K}\).
Put
\[
 s=h/3,\qquad A=2^s\pmod\ell,
\]
choose \(u\in\mathbb F_{16}\) of order fifteen with \(u^5=\omega\),
and set \(t=\widetilde\chi(\zeta+u)\).  For \(0\leq r<h\), define
\[
 \mathcal A_r(\chi)=
 \sum_{\substack{f\in\mathbb F_4[X]\ {\rm monic}\\\deg f=r}}
       \chi(f(\zeta)).
\]
Then
\[
 \mathcal A_0=1,\qquad \mathcal A_1=4,\qquad
 \mathcal A_2=10+\sum_{j=0}^5t^{A^j}.                  \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_1\)}
\]
Failure of \(D'_k\) at \(\ell\) is equivalent to the single extremal
coefficient
\[
 \mathcal A_2=16.                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_2\)}
\]
If \(\mathcal A'_1\) is the degree-one coefficient after the quadratic
constant-field extension to \(\mathbb F_{16}\), then
\[
 \mathcal A'_1=2\mathcal A_2-\mathcal A_1^2
               =2\mathcal A_2-16.                     \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_3\)}
\]
Thus the sixteen-term \(\mathbb F_{16}\)-line extremum and the
six-term irreducible-quadratic extremum are the same constraint under
constant-field base change, not two independent conditions.

More precisely, let
\[
 L_\chi(T)=\sum_{r=0}^{h-1}\mathcal A_rT^r
          =(1-T)L_\chi^*(T),\qquad
 L_\chi^*(T)=\sum_{r=0}^{d}b_rT^r,\qquad d=h-2,
\]
so \(b_r=\sum_{j=0}^r\mathcal A_j\).  The functional equation has root
number \(+1\) and gives
\[
 b_{d-r}=2^{d-2r}\overline{b_r}.                       \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_4\)}
\]
If \(D'_k\) fails, then
\[
 (b_0,b_1,b_2)=(1,5,21),\qquad
 b_{d-2}=21\cdot2^{h-6}.                               \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_5\)}
\]
For
\[
 V=\langle1,\zeta,\zeta^2\rangle_{\mathbb F_4},
\]
the distinguished trace annihilator is
\[
 V^\perp
 =\zeta\{f(\zeta):f\in\mathbb F_4[X],\ \deg f\leq h-4\}.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_6\)}
\]
Consequently, in the bad case,
\[
 \sum_{x\in V^\perp}\chi(x)
 =3b_{d-2}=63\cdot2^{h-6},                             \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_7\)}
\]
exactly the value forced independently by additive Poisson summation
and \(G_K(\chi)=2^h\).  Thus the first two Dirichlet coefficients,
the exact cyclotomic trace-dual placement, the semiprimitive Gauss
sign, the functional equation, and the Riemann hypothesis are mutually
compatible with failure.

Finally put
\[
 D=\zeta^4+\zeta,\qquad E=D^5(D^3+1).
\]
The \(\mathbb F_4\)-subspace polynomial of \(V\) is
\[
\begin{split}
 P_V(Z)={}&Z^{64}
 +\bigl((1+D^3)^4+E^3\bigr)Z^{16}\\
 &+\bigl(D^{12}+E^3(1+D^3)\bigr)Z^4
 +E^3D^3Z.
\end{split}                                             \tag{\(\mathsf{DP}_8\)}
\]
Both \(D\) and \(E\) are \(\ell\)-th powers unconditionally.  Hence
the derivative coefficient \(E^3D^3\) supplies no missing nonresidue;
a subspace-polynomial proof would have to use an additive relation
among the \(Z^{16}\)- and \(Z^4\)-coefficients rather than merely
products of the saturated elements.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The four monic linears \(X+c\), \(c\in\mathbb F_4\), evaluate to
\(\ell\)-th powers, so \(\mathcal A_1=4\).  Of the sixteen monic
quadratics over \(\mathbb F_4\), ten are reducible and therefore also
have character value one.  The six irreducibles are
\[
 f_c(X)=(X+c)(X+c^4),
\]
indexed by the six Frobenius pairs in
\(\mathbb F_{16}\setminus\mathbb F_4\).  Since
\[
 \widetilde\chi(\zeta+c)=\chi(f_c(\zeta)),
\]
the orbit table of Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-line-saturation} gives
their six values exactly \(t^{A^j}\), \(0\leq j<6\).
This proves \((\mathsf{DP}_1)\).  The six summands have modulus one,
so \(\mathcal A_2=16\) if and only if all are one; the \(j=0\) term
then gives \(t=1\), which is exactly failure of \(D'_k\).

The reciprocal roots of \(L_\chi\) are squared under quadratic
constant-field extension.  Newton's identity therefore gives
\[
 \mathcal A'_1=2\mathcal A_2-\mathcal A_1^2,
\]
proving \((\mathsf{DP}_3)\).

The character is primitive modulo \(X^h+\omega\) and even because it
is trivial on \(\mathbb F_4^\times\).  Standard function-field
Dirichlet theory gives \(L_\chi=(1-T)L_\chi^*\), with
\(\deg L_\chi^*=d=h-2\), and the coefficient relation in
\((\mathsf{DP}_4)\) up to its root number.  We determine that sign
directly.  Let \(U\) be the \(\mathbb F_4\)-space of polynomials in
\(\zeta\) of degree at most \(h-2\).  Newton sums for
\(X^h+\omega\) give
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}(\zeta^r)=0\qquad(1\leq r<h),
\]
so \(U^\perp=\mathbb F_4\zeta\).  Additive Poisson summation and
\(\chi(\mathbb F_4^\times\zeta)=1\) give
\[
 3b_d=\sum_{x\in U}\chi(x)=\frac34G_K(\chi).
\]
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-semiprimitive} gives
\(G_K(\chi)=2^h\), so
\[
 b_d=2^{h-2}=2^d.
\]
The root number is therefore \(+1\).  Under failure,
\(b_2=1+4+16=21\), and \((\mathsf{DP}_4)\) at \(r=2\)
gives \((\mathsf{DP}_5)\).

For \(x=\sum_{j=0}^{h-1}x_j\zeta^j\), the same Newton-sum calculation
gives
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}(x)=x_0,\qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}(x\zeta)=\omega x_{h-1},\qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}(x\zeta^2)=\omega x_{h-2}.
\]
This proves \((\mathsf{DP}_6)\).  Every nonzero polynomial of degree
at most \(h-4\) is a unique \(\mathbb F_4^\times\)-multiple of a
monic one, while \(\chi(\zeta)=1\).  Hence
\[
 \sum_{x\in V^\perp}\chi(x)=3b_{h-4}=3b_{d-2}.
\]
Direct Poisson summation under
\(V\setminus\{0\}\subset\ker\chi\) gives
\((63/64)G_K(\chi)\), the same value
\(63\cdot2^{h-6}\).  This proves \((\mathsf{DP}_7)\).

It remains to derive the subspace polynomial.  First put
\(U_1=\langle1,\zeta\rangle_{\mathbb F_4}\).  Since
\(\prod_{c\in\mathbb F_4}(Z+c)=Z^4+Z\),
\[
\begin{split}
 P_{U_1}(Z)
 &=(Z^4+Z)^4+D^3(Z^4+Z)\\
 &=Z^{16}+(1+D^3)Z^4+D^3Z.
\end{split}
\]
Evaluating at \(\zeta^2\) gives
\[
 E=P_{U_1}(\zeta^2)=D^8+D^5=D^5(D^3+1).
\]
Because \(P_{U_1}\) is \(\mathbb F_4\)-linearized,
\[
 P_V(Z)=P_{U_1}(Z)^4+E^3P_{U_1}(Z),
\]
which expands to \((\mathsf{DP}_8)\).

Finally,
\[
 D=\prod_{c\in\mathbb F_4}(\zeta+c),
\]
so \(\chi(D)=1\).  Also \(E\) is the product of all sixteen
monic-quadratic values.  The ten reducible values have character one,
and the other six multiply to
\[
 t^{\sum_{j=0}^5A^j}=1
\]
because \(A\) has order six modulo \(\ell\).  Thus \(\chi(E)=1\)
without assuming failure, which finishes the proof.

\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[relative Dickson form and its degree ceiling]
\label{prop:dk-relative-dickson}
Retain

\[
 s=3^{k-1},\quad a=2^s,\quad E=\F_a,\quad K=\F_{a^6},
 \quad \Lambda_k=a^2-a+1,
\]

and set \(T_k=\{x\in K^\times:x^{\Lambda_k}=1\}\).  The element
\(P_k\in T_k\) has degree six over \(E\).  Put

\[
 Y_k=P_k+P_k^{-1}\in\F_{a^3},\qquad
 G_k(T)=\prod_{j=0}^2(T+Y_k^{a^j})\in E[T].
\]

Then \(G_k\) is irreducible of degree three and
\(X^3G_k(X+X^{-1})\) is the irreducible reciprocal minimal polynomial
of \(P_k\) over \(E\).  If \(\mathcal D_\ell\) is characterized by

\[
 \mathcal D_\ell(v+v^{-1})=v^\ell+v^{-\ell},
\]

then, for every prime \(\ell\mid\Psi_k\),

\[
 P_k\notin T_k^\ell
 \iff G_k(\mathcal D_\ell(X))
      \text{ is irreducible over }E.                 \tag{\(\mathsf{RD}\)}
\]

In the bad case the composition splits over \(\F_{a^3}\), and all
its irreducible factors over \(E\) have degree dividing three.
Moreover

\[
 T_k\cap\F_{a^2}^\times=\mu_3,\qquad
 T_k\cap\F_{a^3}^\times=\{1\}.
\]

Thus the elements of \(T_k\) of relative degree below six are exactly
\(\mu_3\), while the bad subgroup \(T_k^\ell\) contains exactly

\[
 \frac{\Lambda_k}{\ell}-3
\]

elements of exact degree six.  Except in the already settled case
\(\Lambda_k/\ell=3\), full relative degree and reciprocal-torus
structure cannot prove \((D'_k)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-projection} gives
\([\F_2(P_k):\F_2]=6s\), so \(E(P_k)=K\).  Since
\(a^3+1=(a+1)\Lambda_k\), one has
\(P_k^{a^3}=P_k^{-1}\), whence \(Y_k\in\F_{a^3}\).  The element
\(P_k\) is quadratic over \(E(Y_k)\); its relative degree six forces
\([E(Y_k):E]=3\), proving the minimal-polynomial assertions.

Suppose \(P_k\notin T_k^\ell\).  The whole current
\(\ell\)-primary part of \(K^\times\) lies in \(T_k\), so this is
equivalent to \(P_k\notin(K^\times)^\ell\).  Kummer theory and
Capelli make

\[
 X^{3\ell}G_k(X^\ell+X^{-\ell})
\]

irreducible of degree \(6\ell\).  If
\(\alpha^\ell=P_k\) and \(t=\alpha+\alpha^{-1}\), then
\(\mathcal D_\ell(t)=Y_k\).  Since \(\alpha\) is at most quadratic
over \(E(t)\), the degree of \(t\) is at least \(3\ell\); it is a root
of the degree-\(3\ell\) polynomial
\(G_k(\mathcal D_\ell(X))\), proving the forward implication in
\((\mathsf{RD})\).

Conversely, if \(P_k=\alpha^\ell\) with \(\alpha\in T_k\), then
\(\alpha^{a^3}=\alpha^{-1}\), so
\(t\in\F_{a^3}\).  The same holds after multiplying \(\alpha\) by
any \(\ell\)-th root of unity and after applying the three
\(E\)-conjugates.  Hence every root of the Dickson composition lies in
\(\F_{a^3}\), proving the bad-case factorization statement and the
reverse implication.

Finally the two displayed intersections have orders

\[
 \gcd(\Lambda_k,a^2-1)=3,
 \qquad \gcd(\Lambda_k,a^3-1)=1.
\]

Every proper intermediate field of \(K/E\) lies in \(\F_{a^2}\) or
\(\F_{a^3}\), so only \(\mu_3\) has relative degree below six.  The
cyclic group \(T_k^\ell\) has order \(\Lambda_k/\ell\) and contains
\(\mu_3\), because \(\ell\ne3\).  Removing those three elements gives
the asserted exact count.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[trace-degree ceiling for the fibotomic criterion]
\label{prop:dk-trace-degree-ceiling}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-fibotomic}, and let
\[
 \mathcal I_\ell:=\pi\bigl(U^\ell\setminus\{1\}\bigr)\subseteq\F_q
 \qquad(\ell\text{ a prime divisor of }\Psi_k).
\]
Every element of \(\mathcal I_\ell\) has absolute trace one, and
\[
 |\mathcal I_\ell|
 =\frac{(q+1)/\ell-1}{2}
 \geq\frac{3a+2}{2}>a.                                \tag{\(\mathsf T\)}
\]
At least \(a/2+1\) elements of \(\mathcal I_\ell\) have exact degree
\(h\) over \(\F_2\).  Consequently, absolute trace one together with
full degree \(h\) cannot prove that the distinguished
\(Z\) lies outside \(\pi(U^\ell)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The two-to-one trace-one parametrization in
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-fibotomic} restricts to \(U^\ell\).  This
cyclic subgroup has order \((q+1)/\ell\), which is odd, so its only
self-inverse element is \(1\).  This proves the equality in
\((\mathsf T)\).  Since \(\ell\leq\Psi_k=\Lambda_k/3\) and
\(q+1=(a+1)\Lambda_k\), it also gives
\[
 |\mathcal I_\ell|\geq\frac{3(a+1)-1}{2}
 =\frac{3a+2}{2}.
\]
Every proper subfield of \(\F_q\) lies in
\(\F_{2^{h/3}}=\F_a\), because \(h=3^k\).  Hence at most \(a\)
elements of \(\mathcal I_\ell\) have degree smaller than \(h\), leaving
at least \((3a+2)/2-a=a/2+1\) of exact degree \(h\).

For completeness, the distinguished \(Z\) itself has exact degree
\(h\).  Indeed, \((\mathsf D_3)\) and \(P_k\in\F_2(M_k)\) force
\([\F_2(M_k):\F_2]=2h\).  The element \(M_k\) is quadratic over
\(\F_2(Z)\), satisfying
\[
 X^2+Z^{-1}X+1=0,
\]
while \(Z\in\F_q\).  Therefore \([\F_2(Z):\F_2]=h\).
\end{proof}

\subsection{The limits of norm induction}

\begin{proposition}[all proper-subfield norms are blind]
\label{prop:dk-all-norms-blind}
Retain \(k\geq2\) and the notation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-current-level}.  Let \(\ell\) be a prime
divisor of \(\Psi_k\), and let \(d\) be any proper divisor of \(2h\).
Then
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{2^{2h}}/\F_{2^d}}
\]
annihilates the entire \(\ell\)-Sylow subgroup of
\(\F_{2^{2h}}^\times\).  Equivalently, multiplying an element by any
current \(\ell\)-primary component leaves every multiplicative norm to
a proper subfield unchanged.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The norm exponent is
\[
 E_d=\frac{2^{2h}-1}{2^d-1}.
\]
Since \(\ord_\ell(2)=2h\) and \(d<2h\), one has
\(\ell\nmid2^d-1\).  Therefore
\[
 v_\ell(E_d)=v_\ell(2^{2h}-1),
\]
so exponentiation by \(E_d\) kills the full \(\ell\)-Sylow subgroup.
\end{proof}

For $k=1$, $\Psi_1=1$ and $D'_1$ is vacuous.  Exact finite evidence for
the subsequent levels, together with its factorization and primality
dependencies, is recorded in Appendix~\ref{sec:exception-evidence}.

\begin{proposition}[twisted norm tower]\label{prop:twisted}
With $\eta=\zeta^3=\kappa_{3^{k-1}}$,
\[
  \Norm_{\F_{2^{2\cdot3^k}}/\F_{2^{2\cdot3^{k-1}}}}(N_k)
  =\eta^2+\omega^2\eta+1 ,
\]
which is neither $N_{k-1}=\eta^2+\eta+\omega$, nor one of its Frobenius
conjugates (their exponent pattern is $(2^{j+1},2^j)$), nor a scaling
$N_{k-1}(\lambda\eta)$ with $\lambda\in\mu_3$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The generator $\tau$ of the degree-$3$ step sends $\zeta\mapsto
\zeta^{c}$ with $c=2^{2\cdot3^{k-1}}$ a primitive cube root of unity
modulo $3^{k+1}$; the cube roots are $1+a3^k$, so
$\tau(\zeta)=\omega^{a}\zeta$ ($a\neq0$) while $\tau(\omega)=\omega$.
Hence the norm is $\prod_{r^3=\eta}(r^2+r+\omega)$ over the three cube
roots $r$ of $\eta$. Writing $\alpha,\beta$ for the two roots of
$T^2+T+\omega$ (so $\alpha+\beta=1$, $\alpha\beta=\omega$), each factor
is $(r+\alpha)(r+\beta)$, and the product collapses by the resolvent
\[
  \prod_{r^3=\eta}(r^2+r+\omega)
  =(\alpha^3+\eta)(\beta^3+\eta)
  =\eta^2+(\alpha^3+\beta^3)\eta+(\alpha\beta)^3
  =\eta^2+\omega^2\eta+1,
\]
since $\alpha^3+\beta^3=(\alpha+\beta)^3+\alpha\beta(\alpha+\beta)
=1+\omega=\omega^2$ and $(\alpha\beta)^3=\omega^3=1$. Machine-checked at
$k=2,3,4$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[trace form and norm blindness]
\label{prop:dk-trace}
Retain the notation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-current-level}, and put
\[
 \gamma_k:=\zeta+\zeta^{-1},\qquad
 A_k:=\gamma_k^2+\gamma_k+1,\qquad
 Q:=2^{2\cdot3^{k-1}}=2^{2h/3}.
\]
Set $\gamma_0=A_0=1$.
Then, with $\overline N=N^{2^h}$,
\[
 N+\overline N=A_k,\qquad N\overline N=A_k^Q,
 \qquad M_k+M_k^{-1}=A_k^{2-Q},
 \qquad Z=A_k^{Q-2}.                                    \tag{4}
\]
Moreover
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{2^{3^k}}/\F_{2^{3^{k-1}}}}(A_k)=A_{k-1},
 \qquad
 \Norm_{\F_{2^{3^k}}/\F_{2^{3^{k-1}}}}(Z)=A_{k-1}^{-1}. \tag{5}
\]
For every prime $\ell\mid\Psi_k$, however, this relative norm is
blind to the required $\ell$-power residue class.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since $2^h\equiv-1\pmod{3h}$ and
$\zeta^h+\zeta^{2h}=1$, direct expansion gives
\[
 N+\overline N
 =\zeta^2+\zeta^{-2}+\zeta+\zeta^{-1}+1=A_k.
\]
The congruence $Q\equiv h+1\pmod{3h}$ follows inductively by cubing
$1+3^r$ modulo $3^{r+2}$.  Hence
$\gamma_k^Q=\zeta^{h+1}+\zeta^{-(h+1)}$, and another direct
expansion yields $N\overline N=A_k^Q$.  Therefore
\[
 M_k+M_k^{-1}
 =\frac{(N+\overline N)^2}{N\overline N}=A_k^{2-Q},
\]
and \(Z=\pi(M_k)=1/(M_k+M_k^{-1})\), which proves (4).

Over $B=\F_{2^{h/3}}$, the element $\gamma_k$ has minimal polynomial
$X^3+X+\gamma_{k-1}$.  Base-change this cubic extension by
$B_2=B\F_4=\F_{2^{2h/3}}$; its compositum is
$\F_{2^{2h}}$, and the degree remains three.  With
$\omega=\zeta^h\in\F_4$ one has
$A_k=(\gamma_k+\omega)(\gamma_k+\omega^2)$.  Evaluating that minimal
polynomial at $\omega$ and $\omega^2$ gives
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{2^{2h}}/B_2}(\gamma_k+\omega)
   =\gamma_{k-1}+\omega^2,
 \qquad
 \Norm_{\F_{2^{2h}}/B_2}(\gamma_k+\omega^2)
   =\gamma_{k-1}+\omega.
\]
Their product is $A_{k-1}$.  Since norm commutes with this scalar
extension, this is precisely
$\Norm_{\F_{2^h}/B}(A_k)$, proving the first identity in (5).
Furthermore \(Z=A_k^{Q-2}\), so
\[
 \Norm_{\F_{2^h}/B}(Z)=A_{k-1}^{Q-2}=A_{k-1}^{-1},
\]
because \(Q-2\equiv-1\pmod{2^{h/3}-1}\).
This proves the second identity.

If $\ell\mid\Psi_k$, then $\ell\nmid2^h-1$ and
$\ell\nmid2^{h/3}-1$.  Thus both $A_k$ and $A_{k-1}$ lie in
multiplicative groups on which raising to the $\ell$-th power is an
automorphism, so (5) is tautological on $\ell$-power classes.  Equivalently,
$\ord_\ell(Q)=3$ and $1+Q+Q^2\equiv0\pmod\ell$: the degree-three norm
annihilates the relevant character.  The surviving current-level
condition is the Capelli
test $R_k(X^\ell)$ irreducible from
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-current-level}.
\end{proof}

So, unlike the $\gamma$ tower of Proposition~\ref{prop:tower},
old-prime full-order parts do \emph{not propagate by that argument}, and
$D'_k$ is genuinely per-level for the present method: the certification at
each $k$ stands on its own.  Structurally, at the degree-$3$ step with
$Q=2^{2\cdot3^{k-1}}$, the relative norm kernel has order
\[
 Q^2+Q+1=\Phi_{3^k}(2)\Phi_{2\cdot3^k}(2).
\]
Its gcd with $Q-1$ is $3$, so there is no direct-product splitting at
the $3$-part as in Proposition~\ref{prop:norm-kernel-seam}; nevertheless,
for every $\ell\ne3$ the new $\ell$-Sylow subgroups of both columns lie
entirely in the norm kernel.  This is the
prime-to-$3$ analogue of Proposition~\ref{prop:norm-kernel-seam} and
explains why a lower-level norm identity cannot determine $D'_k$.

\begin{remark}[the $3$-part]\label{rem:threepart}
Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-projection} and
\(v_3(a+1)=k\) imply
\[
 v_3(\ord M_k)=k+1\quad(k\text{ odd}),\qquad
 v_3(\ord M_k)\leq k\quad(k\text{ even}).
\]
Thus the odd-level full \(3\)-part is now explained analytically.
\emph{Computational evidence.} At even levels \(2\leq k\leq6\), the sharper observed
value is \(v_3(\ord M_k)=k-1\); the remaining one-step deficiency is
not determined by the projection theorem.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The exceptional-spine conjecture \texorpdfstring{\((D)\)}{(D)}}

The exceptional arm is precisely Conjecture~\ref{conj:dk}: for each
$k$, the distinguished antiunit $M_k$ must contain the full
current-level factor $\Psi_k$ in its order.  The projection and
partition-regulator theorems settle explicit large primary factors,
while the translated-binomial, semiprimitive, Dirichlet, Dickson, and
norm calculations prove that the remaining smaller factors survive all
present generic criteria.  What remains is the single arithmetic
evaluation \(\operatorname{ind}_\ell(d_k)\ne0\).  The explicit wild
regulator in Proposition~\ref{prop:dk-wild-regulator-no-go} and the
code/design and two-adic lifting no-go results in
Propositions~\ref{prop:parity-code-no-go} and
\ref{prop:two-adic-kummer-no-go} show that neither a derived local symbol
nor a higher lift supplies an independent value.  This is a distinguished
cyclotomic Kummer-class problem, not a further degree or norm identity.

\begin{proposition}[common selected-prime reciprocity boundary]
\label{prop:selected-principalizer}
Let \(\ell\) be an odd prime, let \(E\) be a number field containing
\(\mu_\ell\), let \(u\in\mathcal O_E^\times\), and let
\(\mathfrak P\nmid\ell\).  Fix a finite set \(S\) of primes, disjoint
from \(\{\mathfrak P\}\) and the primes over \(\ell\).  Suppose that
\(d\in\mathbb Z_{>0}\), \(n_{\mathfrak r}\in\mathbb Z\), and
\(\beta\in E^\times\) principalize the selected prime up to \(S\), as
an equality of fractional ideals:
\[
 (\beta)=\mathfrak P^d
          \prod_{\mathfrak r\in S}\mathfrak r^{n_{\mathfrak r}}.
                                                               \tag{\(\mathsf{PR}_1\)}
\]
Use the convention
\[
 (u,b)_{v,\ell}
 :=\frac{\operatorname{rec}_v(b)(\sqrt[\ell]{u})}
          {\sqrt[\ell]{u}},
\]
where local reciprocity sends a uniformizer to arithmetic Frobenius.
Then
\[
 \left(\frac{u}{\mathfrak P}\right)_\ell^{d}
 =\left[
    \prod_{\mathfrak r\in S}
       \left(\frac{u}{\mathfrak r}\right)_\ell^{n_{\mathfrak r}}
    \prod_{\mathfrak L\mid\ell}
       (u,\beta)_{\mathfrak L,\ell}
 \right]^{-1}.                                      \tag{\(\mathsf{PR}_2\)}
\]
Here a negative exponent \(n_{\mathfrak r}\) means the corresponding
inverse residue symbol.
If \(\ell\nmid d\), this determines the selected symbol from the
displayed local product after raising the right side to
\(d^{-1}\bmod\ell\).  If \(\ell\mid d\), its left side is identically
one and the formula gives no selected information.

Suppose in addition that \(E/K\) is Galois, that
\(u\in\mathcal O_K^\times\), and that a prime
\(\mathfrak q\mid2\) of \(K\) splits into the \(\ell-1\) primes
\(\mathfrak P_a=\tau_a\mathfrak P_1\),
\(a\in\F_\ell^\times\), where
\(\tau_a(\zeta_\ell)=\zeta_\ell^a\).  If
\[
 A=\left(\frac{u}{\mathfrak P_1}\right)_\ell,
\]
then
\[
 \left(\frac{u}{\mathfrak P_a}\right)_\ell=A^a,
 \qquad
 \prod_{a\in\F_\ell^\times}
 \left(\frac{u}{\mathfrak P_a}\right)_\ell
 =A^{\ell(\ell-1)/2}=1.                              \tag{\(\mathsf{PR}_3\)}
\]
Consequently an element of the base field, whose valuations are equal
at all the \(\mathfrak P_a\), can recover only the tautological orbit
product.  Isolating one Conway-selected lift requires a genuinely mixed
principalizer in the \(K\)- and \(\ell\)-cyclotomic directions.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
At a finite place away from \(\ell\), adjoining an \(\ell\)-th root of
the unit \(u\) gives either the split extension or an unramified Kummer
extension.  Local units therefore act trivially.  Equation
\((\mathsf{PR}_1)\) gives
\[
 (u,\beta)_{\mathfrak P,\ell}
   =\left(\frac{u}{\mathfrak P}\right)_\ell^d,
 \qquad
 (u,\beta)_{\mathfrak r,\ell}
   =\left(\frac{u}{\mathfrak r}\right)_\ell^{n_{\mathfrak r}}.
\]
All other finite places away from \(\ell\) contribute one, and the
archimedean symbols are trivial because \(\ell\) is odd.  The global
Hilbert product formula is exactly \((\mathsf{PR}_2)\).

For the second assertion, Galois covariance of the Hilbert symbol acts
on its value through the cyclotomic character, giving the first formula
in \((\mathsf{PR}_3)\).  The sum of the nonzero residue classes is
\(\ell(\ell-1)/2\), a multiple of \(\ell\), while \(A^\ell=1\).
This proves the orbit cancellation.  Equal valuations of a base-field
element at conjugate lifts cannot replace that product by one selected
factor.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the cyclotomic-unit arms share one wild ray boundary]
\label{cor:cyclotomic-ray-coordinate}
The cyclotomic-unit specializations of the cubic arm, the ordinary arm,
the exact two-component zero reduction, and the exceptional arm all
meet the same principalizer boundary:
\begin{itemize}
\item in the cubic arm one may take
\(u=(1+\xi)(1+\xi^{-1})\), whose selected residue symbol is the
root of unity determined by the coefficient \(L(1)\) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:cubic-mixed-jacobi-top};
\item the ordinary arm and exact two-component zero reduction use
respectively their norm-one quotient and
\(1+\zeta=(1-\zeta^2)/(1-\zeta)\), both global cyclotomic units;
\item in the exceptional arm, writing
\(\rho=\alpha\beta\), with \(\ord(\alpha)=3h\) and
\(\ord(\beta)=5\), the antiunit
\[
 d_k=\frac{(1-\alpha\beta)(1-\alpha\beta^{-1})}
           {(1-\alpha\beta^2)(1-\alpha\beta^{-2})}
\]
has the same selected \(\ell\)-class as \(1-\rho\), up to the
invertible factor four.  At a prime over five one has \(d_k\equiv1\).
At a prime over three,
\[
 \overline d_k=\frac{\beta}{(1+\beta)^2}\in\F_{3^4}^{\times},
\]
whose order divides eighty; hence it and the principal-unit correction
are \(\ell\)-th powers for every current \(\ell\).
\end{itemize}
In each case choose \(d\) to be the order of the selected prime in the
ordinary ideal class group, so that \((\mathsf{PR}_1)\) has
\(S=\varnothing\).  Formula \((\mathsf{PR}_2)\) then identifies the
mixed \(\ell\)-adic local product with the \(d\)-th power of the
selected symbol.  If \(\ell\nmid d\), this transports the selected ray
coordinate but does not evaluate it; if \(\ell\mid d\), the formula is
tautological.  Equivalently, the surviving quantities
\(\Theta_{q,s}\), the cubic \(L(1)\), and the exceptional antiunit
index are distinct instances of the same selected-prime ray-coordinate
problem, not consequences of conductor-local reciprocity.

The abstract proposition also applies to a chosen characteristic-zero
lift of the singleton-even Conway minimal polynomial, whose constant
coefficient is one and whose root is therefore a global unit.  That
lift is noncanonical and the resulting mixed local coordinate is still
unevaluated.  No claim here replaces the separate synchronized-phase
problem for zero arms with three or more components.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Finiteness of the ordinary ideal class group supplies the stated
choice of \(d\) and a generator of \(\mathfrak P^d\).  Only the
exceptional local calculation is not already part of the arm-specific
propositions.  At five, \(\beta\) reduces to one, so the
two numerator and two denominator factors agree.  At three,
\(\alpha\) reduces to one and direct cancellation gives the displayed
element.  Its residue order divides \(3^4-1=80\); the current primes are
different from two, three, and five.  Raising to the \(\ell\)-th power is
therefore an automorphism on this residue group, and also on the
pro-three and pro-five principal units.  The remaining assertions are
the respective selected-symbol identifications and
Proposition~\ref{prop:selected-principalizer}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Jacobi failure is the second ramified torsion layer]
\label{prop:jacobi-second-layer}
Let \(M\) and \(\ell\) be coprime odd integers, with \(\ell\) prime,
and suppose \(M\ell\mid Q-1\).  Over \(\F_Q\), let
\[
 X_{M,\ell}:\qquad y^\ell=1-x^M,
 \qquad J=\operatorname{Jac}(X_{M,\ell}),
 \qquad \pi=\zeta_\ell-1.
\]
For nontrivial characters of orders dividing \(M\) and \(\ell\), put
\[
 J_{j,t}=\sum_{a\in\F_Q}
     \chi_M(a)^j\chi_\ell(1-a)^t
 \quad(1\leq j<M,\ 1\leq t<\ell).
\]
Then
\[
 g(X_{M,\ell})=\frac{(M-1)(\ell-1)}2,
 \qquad
 P_X(T)=\prod_{j=1}^{M-1}\prod_{t=1}^{\ell-1}
             (1+J_{j,t}T).                            \tag{\(\mathsf{JT}_1\)}
\]
In particular, the Frobenius eigenvalues are
\(\alpha_{j,t}=-J_{j,t}\).

The simultaneous first-order flatness condition
\[
 J_{j,1}\equiv-1\pmod{\pi^2}
 \qquad(1\leq j<M)                                   \tag{\(\mathsf{JT}_2\)}
\]
is equivalent to
\[
 J[\pi^2]\subseteq J(\F_Q).                          \tag{\(\mathsf{JT}_3\)}
\]
Consequently it implies the exact divisibility
\[
 \ell^{2(M-1)}\mid\#J(\F_Q).                        \tag{\(\mathsf{JT}_4\)}
\]
The congruence modulo \(\pi\), and hence rationality of
\(J[\pi]\), is unconditional.  Failure is exactly rationality through
the second ramified layer and may lift further; it does not assert
rationality of the full \(\ell\)-torsion.

For the cubic arm take
\[
 M=3^{k+1},\qquad Q=2^{2\cdot3^k};
\]
for the exceptional arm, with \(k\geq2\), take
\[
 M=15\cdot3^k,\qquad Q=2^{4\cdot3^k}.
\]
Their respective mixed-Jacobi failure criteria are exactly
\((\mathsf{JT}_2)\).  In both cases
\[
 \ell<\sqrt Q+1.                                     \tag{\(\mathsf{JT}_5\)}
\]
Thus even the much stronger hypothetical inclusion
\(J[\ell]\subseteq J(\F_Q)\), which would force the divisor
\(\ell^{2g}\), is compatible with the Weil bound
\[
 \#J(\F_Q)\leq(\sqrt Q+1)^{2g}.                      \tag{\(\mathsf{JT}_6\)}
\]
Neither the point-count bound nor the polarization excludes the actual
second-layer failure pattern.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The polynomial \(1-x^M\) is squarefree and \((M,\ell)=1\), so the
superelliptic genus formula gives the first identity in
\((\mathsf{JT}_1)\).  To count points, expand the number of
\(\ell\)-th roots of \(1-x^M\) by \(\ell\)-characters, and expand the
number of \(M\)-th roots of a field element by \(M\)-characters.  The
two nontrivial character sums are exactly the \(J_{j,t}\); the unique
point at infinity supplies the remaining one.  Hence
\[
 \#X_{M,\ell}(\F_Q)=Q+1+\sum_{j,t}J_{j,t}.
\]
The same calculation over every finite extension, together with
Hasse--Davenport, gives the second identity in
\((\mathsf{JT}_1)\).

Let \(\mathcal O=\mathbb Z_\ell[\zeta_\ell]\) and
\[
 R=\mathcal O[T]\big/\bigl((T^M-1)/(T-1)\bigr).
\]
Because \(\ell\nmid M\), this is finite \(\acute{e}\)tale of rank
\(M-1\) over \(\mathcal O\).  The automorphisms
\((x,y)\mapsto(\zeta_Mx,y)\) and
\((x,y)\mapsto(x,\zeta_\ell y)\) act on \(J\).  After the unramified
extension adjoining \(\zeta_M\), the quotient by either deck group is
genus zero, so neither the \(M\)- nor the \(\ell\)-invariant part of
the Tate module occurs.  Because \(\ell\nmid M\), the \(M\)-action is
\(\ell\)-adically semisimple; \(T_\ell J\) is
\(\mathcal O\)-torsion-free, and formula \((\mathsf{JT}_1)\) shows
that every nontrivial \(M\)-character component is free of
\(\mathcal O\)-rank one.  Descent from this unramified splitting then
makes \(T_\ell J\) rank-one projective over \(R\).  Frobenius acts on
its character lines by the \(\alpha_{j,1}\), up to harmless inversion
of \(j\) according to the eigenspace convention.

Every automorphism \(\zeta_\ell\mapsto\zeta_\ell^t\) sends \(\pi\)
to a unit multiple of \(\pi\) and sends
\(J_{j,1}\) to \(J_{j,t}\).  Therefore
\((\mathsf{JT}_2)\) says exactly that Frobenius is the identity modulo
\(\pi^2\) on \(T_\ell J\).  This is equivalent to
\((\mathsf{JT}_3)\).  Since
\[
 |R/\pi^2R|=\ell^{2(M-1)},
\]
equation \((\mathsf{JT}_4)\) follows.  Reduction modulo \(\pi\)
makes every \(\ell\)-character value one away from zero, giving
\(J_{j,t}\equiv-1\pmod\pi\) without any failure hypothesis.

For the cubic arm, put \(n=3^k\) and \(x=2^{n/3}\).  A current prime
satisfies
\[
 \ell\leq x^2+x+1<x^3+1=\sqrt Q+1.
\]
For the exceptional arm, with \(h=3^k\),
\[
 \ell\leq\Psi_k<2^{2h}=\sqrt Q.
\]
This proves \((\mathsf{JT}_5)\), and raising it to the power \(2g\)
proves compatibility with \((\mathsf{JT}_6)\).
Finally \(Q\equiv1\pmod\ell\), so the Weil pairing permits rational
\(\ell\)-torsion.  More sharply, for the present \(\ell\geq7\), the
established \(\mathcal O\)-freeness gives
\(J[\pi^2]=\pi^{\ell-3}J[\ell]\).  The Rosati involution sends
\(\zeta_\ell\) to \(\zeta_\ell^{-1}\), so \(\pi^\dagger\) is a unit
times \(\pi\).  For
\(x=\pi^{\ell-3}x_0\) and \(y=\pi^{\ell-3}y_0\), the product
\((\pi^{\ell-3})^\dagger\pi^{\ell-3}\) has \(\pi\)-valuation
\(2\ell-6\geq\ell-1=v_\pi(\ell)\); the Weil pairing of \(x\) and
\(y\) is therefore one.  Thus \(J[\pi^2]\) is totally isotropic, and
its rank respects the polarization bound.  This completes the claimed
geometric no-go.
\end{proof}

\section{Consequences and the Conjectural Frontier}\label{sec:bounded}

\subsection{Boundedness}

Lenstra conjectured that \(m_p\) is globally bounded
\cite{Lenstra77}.  His unconditional rules give positive excess on
singleton odd component sets and the lower bound \(m_p\geq4\) when
\(f(p)=2\cdot3^k\), but do not give an absolute upper bound.  The
four-arm equivalence of Theorem~\ref{thm:rule-four-arms} shows exactly
what would settle the question: Conjecture~\ref{conj:rule} implies
\[
  m_p\in\{0,1,4\}\qquad\text{for every odd prime }p.
\]

There is also an exact formulation of the weaker boundedness question
